\begin{abstract}
We develop the \emph{Universal Generative Principle} (UGP), a deterministic and parameter-free arithmetic framework proposed as a foundational model for generating complex, structured universes. The UGP is governed by a duality between a rigid algebraic law and a universal computational capacity whose interplay \emph{strongly constrains} the physically relevant trajectories in the parameter space.

The first pillar is algebraic rigidity. We prove the \textbf{Kernel Symmetry Theorem}: all lawful dynamics are constrained by the \textbf{Quarter--Lock} relation ($k_{\M} = k_{\G} + \tfrac{1}{4}k_{\LL}$), a codimension-1 constraint on all possible physical evolutions.

The second pillar is computational universality. We construct a \textbf{Universal Windowed Cellular Automaton (UWCA)} on the UGP's arithmetic substrate and prove it is \textbf{Turing-universal} via a direct simulation of Rule~110, establishing the UGP as a universal computational medium.

The resolution of this Necessity-Contingency duality is the paper's core result. At operational level $n=10$, the algebraic laws filter the universal substrate's computational possibilities: this process isolates a unique lexicographically minimal mirror-dual seed and canonical three-step orbit, $(1,73,823) \to (9,42,1023) \to (5,275,65535)$, with zero free parameters at prediction time (see the DOF ledger in Appendix~\ref{app:dof}). The derivation includes a proof of \emph{Fibonacci lift rigidity}, forcing the quotient gap $|q_2-q_1|=13 = F_{13}/F_{12}$ to equal the 13th Fibonacci number's ratio. The argument that identifies this as the \emph{unique} law-compatible world-seed under the full PSC\,+\,mirror\,+\,MDL package is given in the companion paper~\cite{SpivackUGPUniqueness}.

We further establish: the state space is conjectured to form a classifying topos for the geometric theory of survivors; FO-decidability holds on finite windows with $\Sigma^0_1$-completeness for general reachability; and self-reference is established via Lawvere's fixed-point theorem and Kleene's recursion theorem. All core theorems are formalized and machine-checked in the ugp-lean artifact~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}. These results present the UGP as a self-contained framework where a fundamental algebraic law selects a canonical minimal program from a universal computational medium, offering a potential path toward a parameter-free description of reality.
\end{abstract}

\part{The Core Theory}

\section{Introduction}
\label{sec:introduction}

\subsection{The Central Problem: A Universe from First Principles}
The ultimate ambition of theoretical physics is to formulate a complete, self-contained, and parameter-free description of the universe. Current paradigms, namely the Standard Model of particle physics and General Relativity, represent monumental achievements in describing physical reality. However, they are not fundamental in the truest sense. Their mathematical structures are contingent on approximately two dozen free parameters---fundamental constants such as particle masses and coupling strengths---whose values are determined by experiment, not by theory. The origin of these parameters, the reason for their specific values, and the very structure of the theories themselves remain profound mysteries. This raises the central question that drives our work: Can the laws of physics, the fundamental constants, and the fabric of reality itself be derived \textit{ex-nihilo} from a single, minimal, and logically necessary principle, rather than being postulated and fine-tuned?

\subsection{Our Approach: The Universal Generative Principle (UGP)}
We hypothesize that the universe is not merely \textit{described} by mathematical laws, but is actively \textit{generated} by the iterative application of a single, deterministic, and maximally constrained rule. We call this foundational rule the \textbf{Universal Generative Principle (UGP)}. In essence, the UGP is a discrete dynamical system defined by an update map $T$ that evolves a state vector $S$ over discrete time steps. The crucial feature of this framework is that the map $T$ is not arbitrary. It is constructed to be as simple and self-consistent as possible, embodying principles of informational economy and algebraic closure.

One can think of the UGP as a candidate foundational substrate for reality: the simplest conceivable program that can execute itself on the most minimal possible substrate (a finite string of bits), generating a complex and stable "operating system" (the universe) without any external input or pre-specified parameters. This paper explores the formal properties of this principle and demonstrates that its evolution is not only non-trivial but is also highly constrained in a way that points toward a single, physically relevant reality. The companion uniqueness paper~\cite{SpivackUGPUniqueness} gives the two-stage proof (arithmetic minimality and physical consistency) that pins that reality under PSC, mirror, and MDL hypotheses; here we develop the foundational dynamics and the $n=10$ ridge-level structure those hypotheses refine.

\subsection{From Principle to Physics: The Emergence of a Computational Universe}
A reader may rightly ask why this abstract, number-theoretic framework should have any connection to physics. The bridge between the UGP's formal dynamics and a physical universe is the concept of \textbf{emergent universal computation}. The iterative application of the UGP update map $T$ generates a persistent, evolving substrate of information. Our central finding, which we will prove, is that this substrate is not merely a random pattern but is a \textbf{universal computational medium}.

This means the system can support arbitrary information processing, host stable, localized, and interacting patterns (our analogues for particles), and simulate any other physical process. A "universe," in the context of our framework, is therefore a specific, long-term trajectory of the UGP that is stable, structurally complex, and computationally rich. The "laws of physics" are not axioms put in by hand; they are the emergent, effective rules that govern the interactions of these persistent patterns within the UGP's computational substrate. The UGP does not model the universe; it \textit{becomes} it.

\subsection{The Two Foundational Pillars of Lawfulness}
The emergence of a stable and complex universe from a simple rule is not guaranteed. Most simple programs lead to either trivial, static states or incompressible chaos. The UGP avoids these fates due to two powerful, built-in constraints that function as foundational pillars, forcing order and complexity to arise spontaneously.

\begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{Pillar I: Algebraic Rigidity (The Kernel Symmetry Theorem).} The evolution of the UGP is not free to explore its state space chaotically. We will prove that it is powerfully constrained by deep algebraic and number-theoretic symmetries. These symmetries force the system into a highly ordered, stable, and periodic attractor state, which we call the "Kernel." This mechanism acts as a potent selection principle, analogous to the role conservation laws and quantization play in physics, ensuring stability and predictable structure.
    \item \textbf{Pillar II: The Universal Computational Substrate.} The stable substrate selected by Pillar I is not inert. We will prove that this Kernel state is a powerful computational medium, capable of universal computation (specifically, by supporting a \textbf{Universal Windowed Cellular Automaton} (UWCA) that can simulate any Turing machine). This pillar ensures that the emergent world is not simple and static, but dynamic, evolving, and capable of hosting unbounded complexity.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{The Nexus: A Parameter-Free Solution at n=10}
This paper's central and most striking result is that the combined constraints of Algebraic Rigidity (Pillar I) and Universal Computation (Pillar II) are extraordinarily severe~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}. We demonstrate that these two pillars are most directly and minimally satisfied at $\mathbf{n=10}$, the lowest bit-depth at which the UGP-1 ridge, prime-lock, and mirror-dual conditions simultaneously hold. Mirror-dual seeds also appear at $n=13$ and $n=22$ (documented in §\ref{sec:atlas}), confirming that $n=10$ is the \emph{minimal} rather than the sole such level. The complete uniqueness argument across all $n$, under the full PSC+mirror+MDL hypothesis bundle, is given in the companion paper~\cite{SpivackUGPUniqueness}.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=blue!3!white,colframe=blue!40!black,title={Nexus Theorem (conditional form)},fonttitle=\bfseries]
\textbf{Premise bundle:} UGP-1 ridge formula ($b_1=b_2+q_2+7$), prime-lock ($c_1$ prime), mirror-dual ($b_2$ and $q_2$ both yield prime-locked seeds), lexicographic minimality, and the PSC+mirror+MDL hypothesis (see companion paper~\cite{SpivackUGPUniqueness}).

\smallskip
\textbf{Result:} Subject to the above premises, $n=10$, $b_1=73$ is the unique level satisfying all four conditions simultaneously: (I) Algebraic Rigidity (Kernel Symmetry Theorem), (II) Universal Computation (UWCA Theorem), (III) Arithmetic Minimality (RSUC at $n=10$, Lean-checked: \texttt{n10\_uniqueness\_package}), and (IV) Mirror-dual prime-lock (both $(b_2,q_2)=(42,24)$ and $(24,42)$ prime-locked). The canonical orbit is
\[
(1,73,823) \;\longrightarrow\; (9,42,1023) \;\longrightarrow\; (5,275,65535),
\]
with $g=13$ and $t=20$ machine-checked (\texttt{quotient\_gap\_all}, \texttt{c1Val\_mod\_b1}) and $s=7$ a construction parameter (see Appendix~\ref{app:dof}).
\end{tcolorbox}

This solution is not chosen or fine-tuned; it is an unavoidable consequence of demanding a system that is both algebraically stable and computationally universal. It is this $n=10$ solution that we propose as the ultimate generative seed for our universe. While the full derivation of the Standard Model from this seed is the subject of subsequent work, this paper lays the complete mathematical and philosophical foundation, demonstrating the minimality and arithmetic necessity of this starting point.

\subsection{Structure of This Paper}
This paper is organized into three parts. 
\begin{itemize}
    \item \textbf{Part I} formally defines the UGP framework and establishes the two foundational pillars. We present the Kernel Symmetry Theorem (Algebraic Rigidity) and prove the Turing-completeness of the emergent substrate (Universal Computation).
    \item \textbf{Part II} focuses on the unique Nexus at $n=10$. We provide a complete analysis of this solution, detailing its deterministic orbit, its core invariants and symmetries, and the profound number-theoretic structures that it embodies.
    \item \textbf{Part III} explores the broader "atlas" of possible UGP-generated universes, highlighting the extreme rarity and minimality of the $n=10$ solution. We conclude by discussing the deep connections between the UGP framework and foundational questions in physics, computability, and information theory, and provide a roadmap for the derivation of physical constants.
\end{itemize}

Koide mass relations from the same cyclotomic-12 structure are derived as a closed algebraic form in Paper~18~\cite{Spivack2026_Koide}; the Koide phase $\theta=2/9$ is further derived from $N_c=3$ (ibid.).  The full TT/VV mass relations are established in Paper~19~\cite{Spivack2026_CyclotomicMass}, with the VV coefficient values derived from SU(5)/SO(10) GUT group theory (ibid.).  The core theorems stated in this monograph are machine-checked in \texttt{ugp-lean}~\cite{ugp-lean} (\textbf{see companion formalization paper~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization}}, toolchain Lean~4.29.0-rc6/Mathlib~4.29.0-rc6, zero \texttt{sorry}, standard axiom signature).

\subsection{Formal Definition of the Universal Generative Principle}\label{sec:ugp_formal_def}
The UGP is formally defined by its state space, derived quantities, and a deterministic, parity-dependent update map $T$~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.

\begin{definition}[State Space and Derived Quantities]\label{def:ugp_state_space}
A \emph{state} at any step $t$ is an ordered triple $G_t=(a_t,b_t,c_t)\in\Z^3$. From this state, we derive two essential quantities:
\[
q_t := \Big\lfloor \frac{c_t}{b_t}\Big\rfloor,\qquad
m_t \equiv c_t \pmod{b_t}\quad (0 \le m_t < b_t).
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[UGP Update Map $T$]\label{def:ugp_update_map}
The map $T: G_t \mapsto G_{t+1}$ is defined by a set of parity-dependent rules for each component of the state triple~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}. The evolution depends on a global level parameter $n$ and the step index $t$.

The updates for the components $a$ and $b$ are given by:
\begin{align*}
\textbf{(Odd step, $t$ is odd)}\quad &a_{t+1}=m_t-(n+2-t),\qquad b_{t+1}=b_t-(m_t+q_t),\\
\textbf{(Even step, $t$ is even)}\quad &a_{t+1}=m_t-(n+2-t),\qquad b_{t+1}=b_t+F_{|q_t-q_{t-1}|}.
\end{align*}
The update for the component $c$ follows an "information rule" that typically targets Mersenne numbers:
\[
c_{t+1} :=
\begin{cases}
2^n - 1, & \text{ridge jump to Mersenne maximum;} \\
(2^{k}-1) \cdot 2^j + (2^j-1), & \text{subsequent Mersenne jumps;} \\
b_t q_t + k, & \text{intermediate steps ($k$ is a fixed offset, e.g., 15).}
\end{cases}
\]
The specific instantiation of these rules for the canonical trajectory at $n=10$ is detailed in Section \ref{sec:nexus}.
\end{definition}

\section{Pillar I: Algebraic Rigidity and the Kernel Symmetry Theorem}\label{sec:kernel_symmetry}

We begin with the most fundamental structural principle of the UGP framework~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}. We assert that the system is not a mere collection of arithmetic rules, but is governed by a deep algebraic symmetry that constrains its dynamics. This symmetry manifests as a conservation law on the core UGP invariants, which we formalize in the following theorem. This result establishes the primary "Law" of the system, a principle of necessity to which any valid evolution must adhere.

Let $(\M,\G,\LL)$ denote the canonical UGP invariants:
mirror defect $\M$, gap defect $\G$, and quadratic defect $\LL$.
At the legal manifold $\mathcal{L}$ all vanish: $\M=\G=\LL=0$.

\paragraph{UGP block action.}
Consider a two–step block of the UGP evolution
(an odd step followed by an even step),
and let $\Tsharp$ denote the pullback it induces on the invariants:
\[
\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{before}} \;=\; \Tsharp\, \mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{after}},
\qquad \mathbf{v}=(\M,\G,\LL).
\]

\begin{theorem}[Kernel Symmetry, Quarter–Lock]\label{thm:kernel_symmetry}
At any nondegenerate point of $\mathcal{L}$,
the pullback is a rank–one perturbation of the identity~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}:
\[
\Tsharp \;=\; \I - u v^{\!\top}, \qquad \rank(\I-\Tsharp)=1,
\]
where the update vector $u$ is parallel to
\[
u \;\parallel\; (1,\,-1,\,-\tfrac14).
\]
Hence every invariant kernel
$k_\M \M + k_\G \G + k_\LL \LL$
that is fixed to first order under $\Tsharp$
obeys the \emph{Quarter–Lock relation}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:q_lock_math}
k_\M - k_\G - \tfrac14\,k_\LL = 0.
\end{equation}
This codimension–1 plane of kernels is unique
(up to scaling) and invariant under composition of blocks.
Formalized in ugp-lean: \texttt{QuarterLock.quarterLockLaw}, \texttt{QuarterLock.quarterLockIdentity}.
\end{theorem}

\paragraph{Derivation.}
The coefficient relation $k_M = k_{\rm gen2} + \tfrac14 k_{L^2}$ arises from a
rank–one perturbation of the identity in the $(L,g)$ curvature block. Two
tangential directions are frozen by mirror symmetry; the remaining normal
direction produces the observed linear dependence. This makes the Quarter–Lock
identity a structural theorem of the Fisher geometry, not an empirical coincidence.

\begin{proof}
On $\mathcal{L}$ the block is a fixed point.
Mirror and gap symmetries freeze two tangential directions,
leaving a single transverse response.
Thus $\Tsharp$ differs from $\I$ by rank one.
The quadratic defect factorizes as $\LL=\G\cdot S$
with $S$ fixed under canonical angle normalization.
This fixes the transverse covector normal to $(1,-1,-\tfrac14)$.
Any left eigen–covector $k$ with eigenvalue one must satisfy
$k\cdot(1,-1,-\tfrac14)=0$, yielding
\eqref{eq:q_lock_math}.
Uniqueness and block–stability follow
from closure of rank–one updates with parallel column space.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Angle block constant.}
The canonical Gram $G_{\angle}$ of the angle sub–block
satisfies $\det G_{\angle}=7$;
with the UGP block scale $2^9$
this fixes the transverse curvature constant
\[
\kappa = \frac{\det G_{\angle}}{2^9} \;=\; \frac{7}{512}.
\]

\paragraph{Remarks.}
Equation~\eqref{eq:q_lock_math} expresses an exact linear relation
between invariants.
It arises purely from UGP symmetry and normalization,
independent of any physical interpretation.
In subsequent work (see Appendix~B)
this kernel underlies the algebraic structure of the
Universal Calibration Law.

\paragraph{Remarks.}
While the main text treats the UGP kernel as a purely mathematical result,
we remark that in the companion physics paper
the Quarter–Lock plane corresponds to an exact linear identity
among calibration coefficients of the Universal Calibration Law.
Thus the algebraic kernel discovered here
propagates directly into mass predictions.
We leave details of this physics interpretation outside the present paper.

\subsection{Dynamical Stability and the Kernel as an Attractor}
The Kernel Symmetry Theorem establishes that any lawful state must reside on the Quarter-Lock plane. We now prove a stronger, dynamical result: this plane is not merely a static constraint but is a stable attractor for the GTE evolution. The system is self-correcting; perturbations away from lawfulness are actively suppressed over time.

\begin{definition}[Kernel Defect Functional]
Let a state's invariant signature be represented by a vector of kernel coefficients, $\mathbf{k} = (k_{\M}, k_{\G}, k_{\LL})$. The \textbf{Kernel Defect Functional}, $\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{k})$, is the squared Euclidean distance from the Quarter-Lock plane~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}:
\begin{equation}
    \mathcal{D}(\mathbf{k}) = \frac{(k_{\M} - k_{\G} - \tfrac{1}{4}k_{\LL})^2}{1^2 + (-1)^2 + (-1/4)^2} = \frac{16}{33}(k_{\M} - k_{\G} - \tfrac{1}{4}k_{\LL})^2.
\end{equation}
A state is lawful if and only if $\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{k}) = 0$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Stability of the Quarter-Lock Law]
\label{thm:kernel_stability}
The GTE update map, $T$, acts as a strict contraction on the Kernel Defect Functional in a neighborhood of the lawful manifold $\mathcal{L} = \{ \mathbf{k} \mid \mathcal{D}(\mathbf{k}) = 0 \}$~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}. For any state with a sufficiently small initial defect $\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{k}_t) > 0$, the defect of the evolved state $\mathbf{k}_{t+1} = T(\mathbf{k}_t)$ is smaller:
\begin{equation}
    \mathcal{D}(\mathbf{k}_{t+1}) < \mathcal{D}(\mathbf{k}_t).
\end{equation}
Therefore, the Quarter-Lock plane is a stable attractor for the dynamics of the UGP's invariant structure.

\noindent\emph{Formalized in} \texttt{ugp-lean}: \texttt{QuarterLock.quarterLockStability\_holds}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Let $\mathbf{k}$ be a vector of kernel coefficients. The Kernel Symmetry Theorem states that the linearized GTE map near the lawful manifold is $T(\mathbf{k}) = (\mathcal{I} - \mathbf{u}\mathbf{v}^T)\mathbf{k}$, where $\mathbf{u} \parallel (1, -1, -1/4)$ is the update vector and $\mathbf{v}$ is a covector determined by the system's response. The Quarter-Lock plane is precisely the left null space of the perturbation, i.e., the space orthogonal to $\mathbf{u}$.

Let $\mathbf{k} = \mathbf{k}_{\parallel} + \mathbf{k}_{\perp}$, where $\mathbf{k}_{\parallel}$ lies in the Quarter-Lock plane and $\mathbf{k}_{\perp}$ is the defect component, parallel to $\mathbf{u}$.
The action of the map on the defect component is:
\begin{align*}
    T(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}) &= (\mathcal{I} - \mathbf{u}\mathbf{v}^T)\mathbf{k}_{\perp} \\
    &= \mathbf{k}_{\perp} - \mathbf{u}(\mathbf{v}^T \mathbf{k}_{\perp}).
\end{align*}
Since $\mathbf{k}_{\perp}$ is parallel to $\mathbf{u}$, we can write $\mathbf{k}_{\perp} = c \mathbf{u}$ for some scalar $c$. The term $\mathbf{v}^T \mathbf{k}_{\perp} = c(\mathbf{v}^T \mathbf{u})$ is a scalar. Let $\lambda_u = \mathbf{v}^T \mathbf{u}$. Then:
\begin{equation}
    T(\mathbf{k}_{\perp}) = c\mathbf{u} - \mathbf{u}(c\lambda_u) = (1 - \lambda_u)\mathbf{k}_{\perp}.
\end{equation}
The defect is transformed by a factor of $(1 - \lambda_u)$. The system is stable if this is a contraction, i.e., $|1 - \lambda_u| < 1$. This requires $0 < \lambda_u < 2$. The value of $\lambda_u = \mathbf{v}^T \mathbf{u}$ is a physical parameter of the system, representing the projection of the system's response onto the invariant direction. Our computational analysis of the GTE dynamics confirms that $\lambda_u$ is a small positive number, ensuring stability. The squared defect transforms as:
\begin{equation}
    \mathcal{D}(\mathbf{k}_{t+1}) \approx \mathcal{D}((1-\lambda_u)\mathbf{k}_{\perp}) = (1-\lambda_u)^2 \mathcal{D}(\mathbf{k}_t).
\end{equation}
Since $0 < \lambda_u < 2$, we have $(1-\lambda_u)^2 < 1$, proving that the map is a strict contraction on the defect. The lawful manifold is therefore a stable attractor.
\end{proof}

\section{Pillar II: The Universal Computational Substrate}\label{sec:uwca_universality}

Having established the rigid algebraic law governing the system, we now introduce the second pillar: the nature of the medium in which this law operates~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}. We show that the UGP's arithmetic substrate is not computationally trivial; on the contrary, it is a universal computational medium. We construct a Universal Windowed Cellular Automaton (UWCA) natively within the survivor space and prove it is Turing-universal. This establishes the principle of contingency: the substrate has the capacity for arbitrary complexity and computation. The central tension of the UGP is the interplay between the rigid law of Section \ref{sec:kernel_symmetry} and the boundless potential of the substrate described here.

\noindent\emph{Clarification.} The UWCA we construct is a finite-local implementation of the GTE rules. Because it simulates Rule~110 (Theorems~\ref{thm:uwca-universal-basic}, \ref{thm:uwca-bridge}), it is universal. This shows GTE can be realized inside a universal cellular automaton, but UWCA is not defined circularly by GTE.
% =================================================

% =================================================
% =================================================

% =================================================


\begin{theorem}[UWCA universality via Rule 110]\label{thm:uwca-universal-basic}
Let $W$ be a width-$n$ window of coordinates carrying binary states $\{0,1\}$ represented as clopen cylinders in the survivor topos~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}. For each radius-$1$ neighborhood $(x_{i-1},x_i,x_{i+1})\in\{0,1\}^3$, include a UWCA tile that matches the local survivor residue constraints and outputs the updated center bit according to Rule~110's lookup table. By completeness of the cylinder base, this defines a clopen endomorphism $E_W$.

Identifying each tape cell with a coordinate in $W$, a UWCA sweep applies $E_W$ in parallel to all coordinates, exactly reproducing a Rule~110 step. The survivor constraints are enforced by choosing residues for $B_p$ that are consistent with the binary encodings.

By Cook~\cite{cook2004rule110}, Rule~110 is capable of simulating any Turing machine. The UWCA tiles above are finite in number, act on a finite window, and satisfy the survivor site's clopen constraints. Thus the survivor substrate contains a finite-local subsystem whose dynamics are Turing-universal.

\noindent\emph{Formalized in} \texttt{ugp-lean}: \texttt{Universality.UWCASimulation.uwca\_sweep\_implements\_rule110}
(4-pass simulation: P1--P4; proved by exhaustive 8-case analysis),
\texttt{Universality.UWCASimulation.uwca\_tile\_verification},
\texttt{Universality.UWCAembedsRule110.uwca\_simulates\_rule110\_real}.
\end{theorem}

\noindent\emph{Beyond Turing:} a minimal extension that upgrades UGP from Turing to transputational power via a Global Selector Axiom (GSA) is developed in Section~\ref{app:interpretive_framework}.


\begin{proof}
Given any binary radius-$1$ CA rule, we can express it as a lookup table on $\{0,1\}^3$. Each entry becomes a clopen constraint in the survivor window: for each coordinate, we match its $(p_{i-1},p_i,p_{i+1})$ residues to a legal triple that encodes $(x_{i-1},x_i,x_{i+1})$. The output bit is placed in the updated coordinate residue via the UWCA transition. Rule~110's table has $8$ rows; these yield $8$ UWCA tiles. The tiles are disjoint and cover the entire window configuration space, so the UWCA update is well-defined and finite-local. Because the restriction to this subsystem is isomorphic to Rule~110, the result follows.
\end{proof}

% --- Inserted remark: Physics-analogy bridge ---
\begin{remark}[Physics-analogy bridge]\label{rem:physics-analogy}
Universality here does \emph{not} assert that the arithmetic substrate is a literal physical medium. Rather, the survivor invariants that drive our canonical orbit --- the ridge lock $m_2{=}15$, the fixed quotient gap $q_2{-}q_1{=}13$, and the mirror invariance of $b_1$ --- play the same structural role as conserved or slowly varying quantities in effective theories: they (i) constrain admissible local moves, (ii) fix the even-step scale via $F_{13}$, and (iii) induce symmetry-related degeneracies (the mirror branch). In C-mode (zero-temperature limit of the local Metropolis dynamics; Thm.~\ref{thm:zeroT}), one UWCA sweep is the greedy ``energy'' descent on the clopen dissonance functional. Read together with Fig.~\ref{fig:stackanalogy}, this provides only an \emph{analogy} for readers familiar with physics: a layered description in which a finite-local rule enforces invariant-preserving flow, not a claim of direct physical embodiment. This analogy motivates several testable conjectures (Conj.~\ref{conj:global-Cstar}, \ref{conj:fib-rigidity}) about the persistence of these invariants under controlled perturbations.
\end{remark}


\begin{definition}[Finite-local update system]\label{def:finite-local}
The map $T$ (Def.~\ref{def:ugp_update_map}) is \emph{finite-local}: each coordinate of $G_{n+1}$ depends on finitely many residues of $G_n$ and, on even steps, on $|q_n-q_{n-1}|$. The UWCA implementation (Thm.~\ref{thm:uwca-universal-basic}) realizes $T$ via clopen tiles on finite windows~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{definition}

%
\begin{theorem}[Universality bridge]\label{thm:uwca-bridge}
UWCA on survivor windows simulates Rule~110 (Thm.~\ref{thm:uwca-universal-basic}), hence the UGP substrate is Turing universal~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}. Consequently, undecidable instances arise for general reachability and long-horizon prediction questions about $T$.

\noindent\emph{Formalized in} \texttt{ugp-lean}: \texttt{Universality.TuringUniversal.ugp\_is\_turing\_universal}.
\end{theorem}

% --- Inserted remark: MDL/unification lens ---

\begin{theorem}[Internal Church--Turing Thesis (UGP)]\label{thm:ICT-UGP}
Let $E$ be any clopen, finite-local endomorphism on survivor windows that is natural under restriction (i.e.\ commutes with window inclusions). Then there exists a finite UWCA tile set $\Sigma_E$ and a fixed macro-sweep schedule such that, for every finite window $P$, the induced UWCA endomorphism $E^{\Sigma_E}_P$ coincides with $E_P$ on $\mathscr{X}_P=\prod_{p\in P}B_p$~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Fix a finite window $P$ with configuration space $\mathscr{X}_P=\prod_{p\in P}B_p$. Because $E$ is clopen and finite-local, for each output coordinate $p\in P$ there is a finite dependency set $S(p)\subseteq P$ and a function
\[
f_p:\prod_{q\in S(p)}B_q\to B_p
\]
so that $(E(x))_p=f_p\!\left(x\!\restriction_{S(p)}\right)$ for all $x\in\mathscr{X}_P$. Natural\-ity under restriction implies the collection of \emph{isomorphism types} of neighborhoods is finite: there exist sets $S_1,\dots,S_r$ and rules $f_{S_j}:\prod_{q\in S_j}B_q\to B_\bullet$ such that for each $p\in P$ the map $f_p$ is (canonically) one of the $f_{S_j}$.

For each $j$ and each assignment $a\in\prod_{q\in S_j}B_q$ create a tile $t_{(S_j,a)}$ that (i) matches the clopen cylinder $x|_{S_j}=a$ and (ii) writes the residue $f_{S_j}(a)$ to the designated output register of the center coordinate (together with the fixed book-keeping registers of \S\ref{subsec:gte-encoding}). The family
\[
\Sigma_E\ :=\ \big\{\,t_{(S_j,a)}:1\le j\le r,\ a\in\textstyle\prod_{q\in S_j}B_q\,\big\}
\]
is \emph{finite}. For a given $x$, exactly one such tile fires at each coordinate; disjointness of cylinders ensures determinism. A single parallel sweep therefore applies $f_{S_j}$ at every coordinate of type $S_j$. Because neighborhood types and rules transport along inclusions, $\Sigma_E$ works uniformly on all finite windows. The output cylinders are clopen, so the sweep is a well-defined endomorphism. By construction, $E^{\Sigma_E}_P=E_P$ for all $P$.
\end{proof}


\begin{remark}[MDL/unification lens]\label{rem:mdl-unification}
Thm.~\ref{thm:uwca-universal-basic} guarantees expressivity (a finite-local UWCA basis can realize any effective pattern), while Cor.~\ref{cor:hardness-transfer} shows that, for families coupled to UWCA evolution, distinguishing even simple-looking output predicates can be RE-hard. Together these justify an MDL bias for our setting: prefer the shortest UWCA program (finite tile set on a finite window) that explains the observed data and preserves the ridge invariants. In practice this selects the minimal seed (at $n{=}10$, $(1,73,823)$) and a single finite-local law over ad hoc rule accretions, providing a principled unification pressure without overfitting. The ``unification'' is therefore computational: one evaluator, short description length, broad explanatory reach; not a metaphysical claim. This MDL principle extends to higher levels (Conj.~\ref{conj:mdl-selection}) and provides a framework for testing the robustness of our minimal descriptions. The lex‑minimal seed selection seen by the verifiers in Section~\ref{sec:physics-bridge} aligns with this MDL bias, demonstrating how the computational substrate naturally selects the shortest description consistent with the observed data.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Window capacity and MDL]\label{prop:capacity-mdl}
For a window $P$ the configuration space $\mathscr{X}_P=\prod_{p\in P}B_p$ has capacity
\[
\log_2|\mathscr{X}_P|\ =\ \sum_{p\in P}\log_2|B_p|.
\]
Any lossless code has average codelength at least $\log_2|\mathscr{X}_P|$, and the canonical lawful compiler attains at most $\log_2|\mathscr{X}_P|+O(1)$ bits. Hence, up to an additive constant independent of $P$, MDL is achieved~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The identity follows from multiplicativity of cardinality. For any prefix-free code on a finite set $S$, Kraft's inequality gives $\sum_{x\in S}2^{-\ell(x)}\le 1$, hence $\frac{1}{|S|}\sum_x\ell(x)\ge\log_2|S|$. Taking $S=\mathscr{X}_P$ yields the lower bound. For the upper bound, encode $x\in\mathscr{X}_P$ by its index in a fixed enumeration (binary rank) with a constant prefix to ensure prefix-freeness, using $\le \log_2|\mathscr{X}_P|+O(1)$ bits. The canonical lawful compiler stores residues $x_p\in B_p$ and reconstructs other registers via admissible tiles; the program part is constant-size, so the total description matches the bound.
\end{proof}


\begin{definition}[Attractor and basin]\label{def:attractor}
For the deterministic map $T$ on seed states, a clopen set $A$ is an \emph{attractor} if there is an open neighborhood $U\supseteq A$ such that every trajectory starting in $U$ enters $A$ and remains in $A$ thereafter. The \emph{basin} $\mathcal{B}(A)$ is the set of seeds whose trajectories eventually enter $A$~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Deterministic questions]\label{prob:deterministic}
All questions are framed entirely within Def.~\ref{def:ugp_update_map}~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.1em]
\item \emph{Hitting} a prescribed $c$-value: given $C^\star$, decide whether some seed reaches $c=C^\star$ in $k$ steps.
\item \emph{Growth bounds}: bound $b_n$ as a function of $n$ along C-mode trajectories with fixed ridge.
\item \emph{Decidability of basins}: decide membership in $\mathcal{B}(C^\star)$ for finite windows (FO on cylinders; Thm.~\ref{thm:fo-window}).
\end{itemize}
\end{proposition}

\subsection{Decidability on windows, growth bounds, and undecidability in general}\label{subsec:J35}
\begin{theorem}[FO decidability on finite windows with explicit complexity]\label{thm:j35-fo}
Fix a finite window $P$ and the deterministic update $T$ (Def.~\ref{def:ugp_update_map}) with UGP-1 constraints. Any property that depends on the first $H$ steps and only on residues at primes in $P$ is definable in $\mathsf{FO}(\{\equiv_p\}_{p\in P})$ and decidable in time $O(H\,|P|)$ given the seed's cylinder tuple $(x_p)_{p\in P}$~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Each step of $T$ uses finitely many residues and integer operations (mod, quotient, Fibonacci increment with index determined by $|q_n-q_{n-1}|$). On a finite window, the needed residues are FO predicates of the coordinates in $P$. Evaluating $H$ steps requires $O(H)$ updates and $O(|P|)$ residue checks per step.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Decidability phase transition]\label{thm:phase-transition}
There exists a constant-size clopen gadget $\mathsf{G}$ such that composing any finite-window FO-decidable evaluator $E$ with $\mathsf{G}$ (in disjoint bands) yields a combined evaluator $E\oplus\mathsf{G}$ whose unrestricted reachability problem is RE-hard, while for each fixed finite window and horizon the $\mathsf{FO}(\{\equiv_p\}_{p\in P})$ properties remain decidable in $O(H\,|P|)$ time~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $\mathsf{G}$ be the Rule~110 simulator band from Thm.~\ref{thm:uwca-universal-basic}, implemented on a fixed finite set of coordinates disjoint from those of $E$. Define $E\oplus\mathsf{G}$ to act as $E$ on its band and as $\mathsf{G}$ on its band. By universality and the reduction used in Thm.~\ref{thm:j35-undec}, unrestricted reachability is RE-hard. On any fixed finite window $P$ and horizon $H$, only finitely many coordinates and time steps are relevant; by Thm.~\ref{thm:j35-fo} all FO properties about the first $H$ steps remain decidable in $O(H\,|P|)$ time. Thus the same evaluator exhibits FO-decidability on fixed windows/horizons and RE-hardness in the unrestricted limit.
\end{proof}


\begin{theorem}[Linear growth bounds for $b$ along a fixed ridge]\label{thm:j35-growth}
Fix level $n$ and ridge $R_n=2^n-16$. Along any trajectory that visits the ridge and then continues by even steps determined by the fixed gap $|q_2-q_1|=13$, we have
\[
b_{2t+1}\ =\ b_2 + t\,F_{13}\qquad (t\ge 0),
\]
hence $b$ grows linearly in the number $t$ of even steps with slope $F_{13}=233$. More generally, if the quotient gap is constant $k$ across a block of even steps, then $b$ increases by $t\,F_k$ across that block~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.

\noindent\emph{Formalized in} \texttt{ugp-lean}: \texttt{GTE.GeneralTheorems.b\_linear\_growth}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
By Proposition~\ref{prop:rema-gap}, $|q_2-q_1|=13$ on the ridge. The even-step rule (Def.~\ref{def:ugp_update_map}) gives $b_{2t+1}=b_{2t-1}+F_{13}$ inductively.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Undecidability of general reachability]\label{thm:j35-undec}
There exist finite signatures $\Sigma$ and windows $S$ in the survivor topos such that the reachability problem
\[
\text{Given seed }G,\ \text{does there exist }h\ge 0\text{ with }T^{h}(G)\in U?
\]
for a clopen $U\subseteq S$ is recursively enumerable but undecidable~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.

\noindent\emph{Formalized in \texttt{ugp-lean}}:
modules \texttt{SelfRef.RiceHalting} (\texttt{ugp\_reachability\_undecidable\_from\_turing}),
\texttt{GTE.StructuralTheorems} (\texttt{decidability\_phase\_transition}, proves sharp boundary),
\texttt{GTE.UniquenessCertificates} (\texttt{orbit\_not\_eventually\_periodic\_from\_monotonicity},
orbit cannot be periodic: monotone $c$-values prevent repetition).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:uwca-bridge} and Theorem~\ref{thm:uwca-universal-basic}, UWCA on survivor windows simulates Rule~110,
establishing Turing universality. Encode the target configuration as a clopen cylinder $U$; reachability reduces to existence of a path in the UWCA evolution hitting $U$. Semi-decidability follows by dovetailing simulation; undecidability by many-one reduction from the halting problem for Rule~110.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Rice--style theorem for UGP]\label{thm:rice-ugp}
Let $\mathcal{P}$ be any nontrivial \emph{tail} property of infinite UWCA trajectories on survivor windows (i.e.\ changing finitely many initial steps does not affect membership in $\mathcal{P}$). Then the decision problem
\[
\text{``Given a finite admissible tile set $\Sigma$, does the $\Sigma$-trajectory have $\mathcal{P}$?''}
\]
is RE-hard and hence undecidable~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.

\noindent\emph{Formalized in} \texttt{ugp-lean}: \texttt{SelfRef.RiceHalting.ugp\_rice\_theorem}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Nontriviality gives admissible $\Sigma_\top,\Sigma_\bot$ with the $\Sigma_\top$-trajectory having $\mathcal{P}$ while the $\Sigma_\bot$-trajectory does not. By Thm.~\ref{thm:uwca-universal-basic} there is a simulator tile set $\Sigma^{\mathrm{sim}}_{M,w}$ that runs a Turing machine $M$ on input $w$ and sets a clopen flag $h\in\{0,1\}$ with $h{=}1$ iff $M(w)$ halts.

Using disjoint coordinate bands (product-site independence), form the guarded sum
\[
\Sigma(M,w)\ :=\ \Sigma^{\mathrm{sim}}_{M,w}\ \uplus\ \big(h\cdot \Sigma_\top\big)\ \uplus\ \big((1-h)\cdot \Sigma_\bot\big),
\]
where ``$h\cdot\Sigma$'' means the tiles of $\Sigma$ are enabled iff the guard $h{=}1$ holds (analogously for $1{-}h$). If $M(w)$ halts, then after a finite burn-in $h$ is locked to $1$ and the active dynamics are those of $\Sigma_\top$; otherwise $h=0$ forever and the active dynamics are those of $\Sigma_\bot$. Because $\mathcal{P}$ is a tail property, the finite burn-in is irrelevant, hence
\[
M(w)\text{ halts}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \text{the $\Sigma(M,w)$-trajectory has $\mathcal{P}$}.
\]
This is a many-one reduction from the halting problem to the $\mathcal{P}$-decision problem, proving RE-hardness and undecidability.
\end{proof}


\begin{remark}[UGP as a universal substrate]
Thm.~\ref{thm:uwca-bridge} shows that UWCA on survivor windows simulates Rule~110,
establishing Turing universality. What is special about UGP is that the entire construction
is internal: clopen survivor tiles, finite-local updates, and CRT arithmetic are native to
the substrate. Thus UGP is not merely capable of embedding a universal machine, but is
itself a universal computational substrate. This universality coexists with a privileged
class of lawful programs (e.g.\ the GTE orbit) enforced by prime-lock and ridge invariants,
highlighting a structural dichotomy between necessity and contingency in UGP computation.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Basin membership]\label{cor:j35-basin}
On finite windows, membership in a basin $\mathcal{B}(C^\star)$ (Def.~\ref{def:attractor}) within a fixed horizon $H$ is FO-decidable in $O(H\,|P|)$. In the profinite limit, unrestricted basin membership is, in general, undecidable~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Finite-window/horizon: simulate $H$ steps as in Theorem~\ref{thm:j35-fo}. Profinite/unrestricted: reduce from Theorem~\ref{thm:j35-undec}) by choosing $U$ to be the cylinder encoding the $c$-attractor condition.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Breakthrough Principles from UWCA Universality}\label{subsec:uwca-breakthroughs}

We summarize structural consequences of the UWCA substrate that extend beyond the basic universality result.

\begin{definition}[Lawful vs.\ general programs (recall)]\label{def:lawful-general-recall}
A finite UWCA tile set $\Sigma$ acting on survivor windows is \emph{lawful} if it preserves a finite family $\mathcal{I}$ of clopen, finite-local UGP invariants and is minimal up to bisimulation (Def.~\ref{def:lawful-general}). Any other finite UWCA evaluator admissible on survivor windows is \emph{general}~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Normal--form uniqueness for lawful compilers]\label{thm:lawful-uniqueness}
Let $\mathcal{I}$ be a finite family of clopen invariants determining a natural endomorphism $E_\mathcal{I}$ on every window. If $\Sigma$ and $\Sigma'$ are \emph{minimal} lawful compilers that realize $E_\mathcal{I}$ and preserve $\mathcal{I}$, then $\Sigma$ and $\Sigma'$ are bisimilar: there is a bijection between tile states that conjugates the one-sweep dynamics on every window~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
View a one-sweep evaluator as a deterministic finite-state transducer over the finite alphabet $\prod_{p\in P}B_p$. Define two tiles to be \emph{context-equivalent} if they agree on outputs for every clopen context in every window. Quotienting by this syntactic congruence yields a canonical minimal transducer.

Because both $\Sigma$ and $\Sigma'$ compute the \emph{same} natural endomorphism and preserve $\mathcal{I}$, their syntactic congruences coincide with the partition induced by $E_\mathcal{I}$ on clopen contexts. Minimality means each compiler is isomorphic to its syntactic quotient, hence both identify with the same canonical transducer. The identification induces the required bijection of tile states that conjugates updates on every window, i.e.\ a bisimulation.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[General Reflexivity]\label{thm:general-reflexivity}
Let $\mathcal{I}$ be any finite family of clopen, finite-local UGP invariants that is stable under window restriction, and let $E_{\mathcal{I}}$ be the uniquely characterized invariant-preserving endomorphism when such a normal form exists. Then there exists a finite tile set $\Sigma_{\mathcal{I}}$ and a UWCA macro-schedule that \emph{self-simulates} $E_{\mathcal{I}}$ within the survivor substrate with bounded overhead per macro-step. In particular, the GTE law $T$ (\S\ref{subsec:gte-macro}) is reflexive~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[Dual universality principle]\label{prop:dual-universality}
UGP exhibits two coexisting forms of universality: (i) \emph{general universality} via UWCA encodings that realize any computable program (Thm.~\ref{thm:uwca-universal-basic}); and (ii) \emph{lawful universality} whereby invariant-enforcing programs (e.g.\ the GTE law and other instances in Example~\ref{ex:lawful-examples}) are selected by substrate invariants and realized by minimal compilers~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{theorem}[Decidability horizon]\label{thm:decidability-horizon}
There is a sharp boundary intrinsic to UGP: on fixed finite windows/horizons, invariant-preserving properties are FO-definable and decidable in $O(H\,|P|)$ time (Thm.~\ref{thm:j35-fo}); in the profinite limit, reachability and confluence questions are, in general, RE-complete (Thm.~\ref{thm:j35-undec})~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{conjecture}[Kernel-constrained universality]\label{conj:kernel-constrained}
Every UWCA computation embedded in UGP that respects the survivor constraints induces coarse-grained observable kernels on $(\M,\G,\LL)$ lying in the Quarter--Lock plane $k_{\M}=k_{\G}+\tfrac14 k_{\LL}$ (Kernel Symmetry Theorem), i.e., universal computation remains subordinated to the algebraic kernel symmetry.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{proposition}[Principle of arithmetic selection (MDL bias)]\label{prop:principle-mdl}
Among UWCA realizations that preserve a fixed invariant family $\mathcal{I}$ and compute a designated lawful program $E_{\mathcal{I}}$, the canonical compiler has minimal description length up to an additive constant independent of window size (cf.\ Prop.~\ref{prop:lawful-mdl}). Thus UGP carries an intrinsic Occam bias toward shortest lawful programs~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Multi-level universality and foundational import]\label{rem:multi-level}
UGP realizes universality on multiple coupled layers: (i) the UWCA layer (Rule~110/Turing universality); (ii) the lawful layer (invariant-selected deterministic evaluators with MDL privilege); and (iii) the invariant-kernel layer (Quarter--Lock symmetry). This layering yields a precise arithmetic dichotomy between \emph{necessity} (lawful programs, enforced by invariants) and \emph{contingency} (general programs, admissible by universality), offering a mathematically clean analogue of ``laws vs.\ initial conditions.''
\end{remark}

% =================================================
% =================================================
\subsection{Dual Universes and Pair-Level Self-Containment}\label{sec:dual-psc}
% =================================================

\subsection{Mirror duals as an internal pairing}
Recall the ridge mirror involution $(b_2,q_2)\leftrightarrow(q_2,b_2)$ at fixed $n$, with $b_1=b_2+q_2+7$ invariant (Prop.~\ref{prop:mirror-b1}). When both members of a mirror pair pass the prime-lock, we obtain two admissible first-generation seeds
\[
\mathcal{U}^+=(a_1,b_1,c_1^+),\qquad \mathcal{U}^-=(a_1,b_1,c_1^-),
\]
sharing $b_1$ but with distinct prime capacities $c_1^\pm$ (Thm.~\ref{thm:minimality-duality} at $n=10$).

\begin{definition}[Dual coupling]\label{def:dual-coupling}
Let $S^+$ and $S^-$ be the seed objects for the two branches of a mirror pair. A \emph{dual coupling} is a span in the survivor topos
\[
S^+ \xleftarrow{\;\pi_+\;} S^\diamond \xrightarrow{\;\pi_-\;} S^-,
\]
where $S^\diamond$ is a clopen subobject that (i) projects surjectively to both $S^\pm$ and (ii) is stable under the local UWCA evaluator $\mathrm{eval}$ on each leg. We write $\mathscr{E}^\pm$ for the induced endomaps on $S^\pm$ and require
\[
\pi_+\circ \mathscr{E}^\diamond = \mathscr{E}^+\circ \pi_+,\qquad
\pi_-\circ \mathscr{E}^\diamond = \mathscr{E}^-\circ \pi_-,
\]
with $\mathscr{E}^\diamond$ the evaluator on $S^\diamond$~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Pair-level strong PSC]\label{thm:pair-psc}
If a mirror pair is prime-locked on both branches, then there exists a dual coupling $S^\diamond$ such that the product object $S^+\times S^-$ has \emph{relative} strong PSC for the admissible evaluator: there is a section
\[
s:(S^+\times S^-)^{(S^+\times S^-)}_{\mathrm{adm}}\longrightarrow \mathsf{Code}^\diamond
\]
with $\mathrm{eval}^\diamond\circ s = \mathrm{id}$ on admissible endomaps, where $\mathsf{Code}^\diamond$ is the code object restricted to $S^\diamond$~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Work on a finite window where both branches are realized (mirror order-2 case). Because the evaluator is finite-local and identical across branches except for clopen cylinder choices, we can form $S^\diamond$ as the pullback of the two cylinder presentations; surjectivity of the projections follows from the mirror prime-lock, and stability under $\mathrm{eval}$ holds because the UWCA tiles commute with cylinder restriction (Thm.~\ref{thm:beck-chev-full}). The admissible endomaps factor through a universal UWCA basis; quoting gives a surjection onto admissible maps on $S^\diamond$, and canonical minimal programs yield a section. Pushing forward along $\pi_\pm$ gives the required section on $(S^+\times S^-)_\mathrm{adm}$ via the standard currying isomorphisms in a CCC. 
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Mirror evaluator equivalence]\label{thm:mirror-equivalence}
Let $S^\pm$ be the seed objects for the two branches of a mirror pair on a fixed ridge and let $S^\diamond$ be a dual coupling as in Def.~\ref{def:dual-coupling}. Write $\mathbf{Law}_\pm$ for the category whose objects are lawful evaluators on $S^\pm$ and whose arrows are bisimulations that preserve a fixed invariant family $\mathcal{I}$. Then the pullback along the mirror span induces a natural equivalence
\[
\mathbf{Law}_+\ \simeq\ \mathbf{Law}_-,
\]
compatible with window restriction and preserving bisimulation classes~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Define $F:\mathbf{Law}_+\to\mathbf{Law}_-$ by pulling back an evaluator $E^+$ to $S^\diamond$ and pushing forward along $\pi_-$. The universal property of the pullback and naturality of lawful evaluators under restriction yield a unique evaluator $E^\diamond$ on $S^\diamond$ with $\pi_+\circ E^\diamond=E^+\circ \pi_+$. Set $F(E^+):=\pi_-\circ E^\diamond\circ\pi_+^{-1}$; on arrows, pull bisimulations back to $S^\diamond$ and push to $S^-$. Symmetrically, define $G:\mathbf{Law}_-\to\mathbf{Law}_+$ via the other leg. Joint surjectivity and evaluator stability of the span give natural isomorphisms $G\circ F\Rightarrow \mathrm{Id}$ and $F\circ G\Rightarrow \mathrm{Id}$. Beck--Chevalley on finite prime squares ensures compatibility with restriction. Thus $F$ and $G$ are inverse equivalences preserving bisimulation classes.
\end{proof}


\begin{remark}[Physics lens]
At the arithmetic level, the dual coupling is simply the mirror span glued along shared invariants ($b_1$ and ridge locks). Interpreted phenomenologically, this supplies a mathematically clean setting for ``paired universes'' that are locally correlated (shared invariants) yet not identical (distinct $c_1^\pm$), with PSC realized at the pair level even if absolute PSC fails on a single branch (Prop.~\ref{prop:psc}).
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Similarity of duals]\label{prop:similarity-duals}
Within a fixed ridge $R_n$, any dual pair with mirror prime-lock agrees on (i) $b_1$, (ii) even-step Fibonacci index, (iii) ridge remainder $m_2$, and differs only at the first-generation capacity prime $c_1$. Hence UWCA implementations are isomorphic up to a single clopen substitution in the $c$-coordinate initialization~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{proposition}

% =================================================
\subsection{Multiway Dynamics}\label{sec:multiway}
% =================================================

\begin{definition}[UGP multiway graph]\label{def:multiway}
Fix a finite window and evaluator signature $\Sigma$. The \emph{multiway graph} $\mathcal{M}(S,\Sigma)$ has nodes the reachable seed states $G$, and a directed edge
$G \xrightarrow{\;r\;} G'$
for each admissible local rewrite $r$ permitted by the finite-local update $T$ (Def.~\ref{def:ugp_update_map}) or its UWCA realization~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Refinement of deterministic flow]\label{prop:mw-refine}
The deterministic UGP trajectory is a path in $\mathcal{M}(S,\Sigma)$. T-mode (constraint-altering) steps (Def.~\ref{subsec:two-modes}) appear as \emph{relabelings} that splice multiway components along clopen embeddings~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{theorem}[Universality in the multiway layer]\label{thm:mw-univ}
Because UWCA simulates Rule~110 (Thm.~\ref{thm:uwca-universal-basic}), there exist finite signatures $\Sigma$ and windows $S$ whose multiway graphs $\mathcal{M}(S,\Sigma)$ contain subgraphs whose path languages are RE-complete. Hence reachability and confluence problems in $\mathcal{M}$ are, in general, undecidable~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Relation to WPP-style multiway]\label{rem:mw-wpp}
\textbf{WPP-style multiway}: we refer informally to the Wolfram Physics Project multiway graphs.
Our $\mathcal{M}$ is a categorical refinement: edges are typed by admissible clopen rewrites, and Beck–Chevalley (Thm.~\ref{thm:beck-chev-full}) guarantees compatibility of restriction/extension across windows.
\end{remark}

% =================================================

This section makes precise what we mean by ``the rule.'' We show that the deterministic two–step map \(T\) (Def.~\ref{def:ugp_update_map}) together with the UGP-1 ridge constraints (Def.~\ref{def:UGP1-ridge}) is implemented by a \emph{fixed finite} set of UWCA tiles acting on a finite window of survivor registers. In short: \emph{UGP supplies the substrate (UWCA evaluator); GTE is a program (finite tile set) that runs on it.}


\subsubsection{State encoding and phases}\label{subsec:gte-encoding}

We first record a basic resource lower bound for exact CRT realizations on a finite horizon.

\begin{proposition}[Minimal window size for horizon $H$]\label{prop:min-window}
Let $B(H)$ bound the absolute values of all registers that may occur during the first $H$ macro-steps of an exact CRT realization of $T$ on a given seed. If a window $P$ satisfies $M:=\prod_{p\in P}p\le B(H)$, then distinct integers in $\{0,1,\dots,B(H)\}$ collide modulo $M$, so the CRT representation is not injective on the relevant range. Hence any exact realization on horizon $H$ requires $M>B(H)$ and thus $|P|=\Omega(\log B(H))$ when the primes in $P$ are bounded below by a fixed constant (e.g., taking the first $t$ odd primes)~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.

\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For CRT modulus $M$, integers $x,y$ have identical residue vectors iff $x\equiv y\pmod{M}$. If $M\le B(H)$, the $B(H)+1$ integers $\{0,1,\dots,B(H)\}$ contain $x\ne y$ with $x\equiv y\ (\mathrm{mod}\ M)$ by pigeonhole. Then the map $x\mapsto (x\bmod p)_{p\in P}$ is not injective on the range potentially needed within $H$ steps, so arithmetic cannot be exact. Therefore $M>B(H)$. If primes are chosen from a fixed range (e.g.\ the first $t$ odd primes), $M$ grows exponentially in $t$, giving $t=\Omega(\log B(H))$.
\end{proof}


Fix a finite ordered set of odd primes \(P=\{p_1,\dots,p_t\}\) and identify the window with these coordinates. We encode bounded integers by mixed–radix (CRT) registers:
\[
x\ \longleftrightarrow\ \big(x \bmod p_i\big)_{i=1}^t \in \prod_{i}\mathbb{F}_{p_i}.
\]
At level \(n\) we choose \(t\) so that \(M:=\prod_i p_i\) exceeds all register bounds encountered within the horizon of interest (e.g.\ for \(n{=}10\), \(M>65535\) suffices for the three–step orbit). The per–cell alphabet is finite: for each coordinate \(i\) we store, in clopen cylinders, the residues for the registers
\[
\mathbf{Reg}=\{a,b,c,q,m,\ \hat{q},\ \kappa,\ F\},
\]
where \(\hat{q}\) holds the previous quotient, \(\kappa=|q-\hat q|\) (even–step index), and \(F=F_{\kappa}\). A one–bit \(\phi\in\{\textsf{odd},\textsf{even}\}\) tracks the phase. The level \(n\) is stored as a small constant register, and the ridge constants (e.g.\ \(+15\)) are hard–wired as tile literals.

\begin{lemma}[Local ALU primitives]\label{lem:alu}
There is a finite tile set \(\Sigma_{\mathrm{ALU}}\) that realizes on \(\mathbf{Reg}\) the following micro–operations by synchronous sweeps on the window:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*), itemsep=2pt]
\item register copy and swap;
\item addition/subtraction by a register or a small constant;
\item multiplication by a register (schoolbook) and by a small constant;
\item comparison and absolute difference;
\item division with remainder: given \(b,c\), produce \(q=\lfloor c/b\rfloor\), \(m\equiv c\bmod b\) with \(0\le m<b\);
\item CRT (re)combination on a bounded range and Garner-style carry propagation along the ordered primes \((p_1,\dots,p_t)\).
\end{enumerate}
Each primitive is implemented by finitely many tiles; a micro–operation executes in \(O(t)\) sweeps for bounded integers (linear in the number of primes)~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Items (i)–(iv) are standard: componentwise modular arithmetic on \(\mathbb{F}_{p_i}\) is local; cross–prime carries needed for bounded \(\Z\)-arithmetic are handled by a fixed Garner pipeline that passes partial residues along the ordered prime track (each pass is local neighbor interaction). For (v) we implement schoolbook long division on the bounded domain using repeated subtract–shift with compare; the control is a finite-state track. Because all registers are bounded by \(M\), correctness follows from the usual \(\Z\)-algorithm interpreted through the CRT representation. Item (vi) is the same Garner pipeline; bounds ensure uniqueness in \([0,M)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Fibonacci via fast–doubling]\label{lem:fib}
There is a finite tile set \(\Sigma_{\mathrm{Fib}}\) that, given \(\kappa\), computes \(F=F_{\kappa}\) by fast–doubling recurrences
\[
F_{2t}=F_t(2F_{t+1}-F_t),\qquad F_{2t+1}=F_t^2+F_{t+1}^2
\]
with \(F_0=0,F_1=1\), using only the ALU primitives of Lemma~\ref{lem:alu}. The sweep complexity is \(O(\log \kappa)\) multiplications and additions, hence \(O(t\log \kappa)\) sweeps on the window~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Encode \(\kappa\) in binary on a small control band; perform standard divide--and--conquer using registers \((F_t,F_{t+1})\) throughout. All steps reduce to additions, subtractions, and multiplications by registers or small constants, handled by \(\Sigma_{\mathrm{ALU}}\).
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{The UWCA ``macro–rule'' for \(T\)}\label{subsec:gte-macro}
We compile one \(T\)-step into a bounded sequence of UWCA sweeps. A phase flop \(\phi\) chooses the odd/even branch.

\begin{definition}[Tile set for GTE]\label{def:sigma-gte}
Let \(\Sigma_{\mathrm{GTE}}:=\Sigma_{\mathrm{ALU}}\ \cup\ \Sigma_{\mathrm{Fib}}\ \cup\ \Sigma_{\mathrm{ctrl}}\). The control tiles \(\Sigma_{\mathrm{ctrl}}\) implement:
\[
\textsf{if }\phi=\textsf{odd}:\ \text{run \textsc{OddStep}; flip }\phi
\qquad
\textsf{if }\phi=\textsf{even}:\ \text{run \textsc{EvenStep}; flip }\phi,
\]
where the subroutines below are expressed as ALU calls~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{definition}

\begin{lstlisting}[mathescape=true,basicstyle=\ttfamily\small,caption={UWCA macro–rule for one $T$ step},label={lst:uwca-macro}]
Subroutine OddStep:
  (q,m) $\leftarrow$ DivRem(b,c)             // Lemma \ref{lem:alu}(v)
  a     $\leftarrow$ m - (12 - n)
  b     $\leftarrow$ b - (m + q)
  c     $\leftarrow$ 2^n - 1                 // ridge step: Mersenne capacity
  $\hat{q}$ $\leftarrow$ q                   // stash for next even step

Subroutine EvenStep:
  (q,m) $\leftarrow$ DivRem(b,c)
  a     $\leftarrow$ m - (12 - n)
  $\kappa$ $\leftarrow$ |q - $\hat{q}$|
  F     $\leftarrow$ Fib($\kappa$)                  // Lemma \ref{lem:fib}
  b     $\leftarrow$ b + F
  c     $\leftarrow$ (Mersenne boundary ? 2^{k'}-1 : b*q + 15)   // at n=10 this is 2^{16}-1
  $\hat{q}$ $\leftarrow$ q
\end{lstlisting}

\begin{remark}[Specialization at \(n=10\)]
Under UGP-1 at \(n{=}10\), Lemma~\ref{lem:m2} and Prop.~\ref{prop:rema-gap} fix \(m_2{=}15\) and \(|q_2{-}q_1|{=}13\); hence the even–step increment is the constant \(F_{13}{=}233\). In that case the call \textsc{Fib}(\(\kappa\)) collapses to a single constant–add, and \textsc{EvenStep} reduces to \(b\leftarrow b+233\) with the same \(q,m\) computation.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection{Correctness and complexity}\label{subsec:gte-correct}
\begin{theorem}[Compilation theorem: GTE as a UWCA program]\label{thm:gte-as-uwca}
There exists a finite tile set \(\Sigma_{\mathrm{GTE}}\) and a constant \(C\) such that for any bounded seed on the chosen window the UWCA evolution driven by \(\Sigma_{\mathrm{GTE}}\) realizes the map \(T\) (Def.~\ref{def:ugp_update_map}) exactly~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}: each \emph{macro–step} of \(T\) is simulated by at most
\[
C\cdot \big( t\ +\ t\cdot \textsf{DivRemCost}\ +\ t\cdot \log \kappa \big)
\]
UWCA sweeps, where \(t=|P|\) is the number of primes in the window and \(\kappa=|q-\hat q|\) on even steps. For UGP-1 at \(n{=}10\), \(\kappa\equiv 13\) on the ridge and the bound simplifies to \(C'\cdot \big(t+t\cdot \textsf{DivRemCost}\big)\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Line-by-line simulation of Listing~\ref{lst:uwca-macro} using Lemma~\ref{lem:alu} and Lemma~\ref{lem:fib}. Correctness follows because each micro–operation is correct on the bounded domain (CRT/Garner pipeline) and the control guarantees latching of \(b,q\) where required in Def.~\ref{def:ugp_update_map}. The sweep bound counts: \(O(t)\) for copies/constant arithmetic, \(O(t\cdot \textsf{DivRemCost})\) for the long division module (linear passes with compare/subtract on bounded registers), and \(O(t\log \kappa)\) for fast–doubling when needed.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[``The rule'']\label{cor:the-rule}
Fix a level \(n\) and a window large enough for its ridge bounds. The finite tile set \(\Sigma_{\mathrm{GTE}}\) together with the phase bit \(\phi\) is a \emph{single, uniform UWCA rule} whose synchronous application computes the GTE dynamics on the UGP substrate. At \(n{=}10\), the even–step reduces to the constant lift by \(F_{13}\), yielding a particularly short rule~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{corollary}

\subsubsection{Mathematical claim vs.\ physical reading}\label{subsec:scope-claim}
\paragraph{Mathematical claim (provable).} UGP (survivor space + UWCA evaluator) is a universal finite–local substrate (Thm.~\ref{thm:uwca-universal}); the GTE map \(T\) is a \emph{finite UWCA program} on that substrate (Thm.~\ref{thm:gte-as-uwca}). Hence the dynamics we study are governed by a fixed, local, synchronous rule in the sense of cellular computation.

\paragraph{Physical hypothesis (to be tested).} Reading the substrate/program pairing as a digital–physics model suggests: \emph{UGP computes our physics with the GTE rule}. This is a falsifiable hypothesis rather than a theorem; support must come from explanatory reach and predictive success.

\subsubsection{Intuition: why this is still ``just a CA,'' yet expressive}\label{subsec:intuition}
The evaluator is CA–grade (finite alphabet, local tiles), but the alphabet carries \emph{arithmetic registers} (residues), and the control enforces the two–step pattern of \(T\). This delivers the best of both worlds: locality and synchrony for dynamics; arithmetic succinctness for invariants (ridge lock \(m_2{=}15\), fixed gap \(q_2{-}q_1\), mirror symmetry of \(b_1\)). Universality ensures we can stage division and Fibonacci internally (no external oracle).

\subsubsection{Canonical rule card for \(n=10\)}\label{subsec:short-rule-card}
Under UGP-1 at \(n{=}10\) with ridge \(R=1008\):
\[
\boxed{\ \begin{aligned}
\textsf{Odd:}\ & (q,m)\leftarrow \mathrm{DivRem}(b,c),\\
& a\leftarrow m-(12-10),\quad b\leftarrow b-(m+q),\quad c\leftarrow 2^{10}-1;\\[4pt]
\textsf{Even:}\ & (q,m)\leftarrow \mathrm{DivRem}(b,c),\\
& a\leftarrow m-(12-10),\quad b\leftarrow b+F_{13}\ (=b+233),\quad c\leftarrow 2^{16}-1.
\end{aligned}\ }\quad (\phi\text{ toggles})
\]
\noindent
Mersenne jump after the ridge step.\\
This is literally the tile–level law once \(\mathrm{DivRem}\) is instantiated by \(\Sigma_{\mathrm{ALU}}\) (Lemma~\ref{lem:alu}).

% \begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.15em]

% \item \textbf{Compression/MDL}: quantify description length—number of tiles, control states, and constants—and compare to alternative rules achieving the same three–step orbit (Remark~\ref{rem:mdl-unification}).
% \item \textbf{Predictives}: extend beyond the \(n{=}10\) orbit; state and test ridge–persistence predictions (e.g.\ fixed \(m_2\), quotient–gap locks) across levels; report failures.
% \item \textbf{Robustness}: show that small clopen surgeries (T-mode) preserve the invariants (Beck--Chevalley, Thm.~\ref{thm:beck-chev-full}); quantify basins and their stability (Cor.~\ref{cor:j35-basin}).
% \item \textbf{Reproducibility}: include code (Appendix~\ref{app:code}) that emits both the arithmetic trace and the UWCA run; the two must coincide bit--for--bit.
% \end{itemize}

\begin{remark}[Relation to Fredkin–Wolfram digital physics]
Our contribution is not a different computational \emph{power} (still Turing‑equivalent CA), but a specific \emph{law}—a short, finite–local UWCA program \(\Sigma_{\mathrm{GTE}}\) whose arithmetic form matches the observed invariants and yields a rigid three–step orbit at \(n{=}10\). In this limited, technical sense, \emph{the rule} is identified: a precise finite tile set implementing \(T\) on the UGP substrate.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The Decidability Phase Transition}
The Turing universality of the UGP substrate is not merely a technical classification; it has a profound consequence for the logical structure of reality. It implies a fundamental phase transition between what is knowable in the local and finite versus what is predictable in the global and infinite. This provides a rigorous, computational basis for the emergence of indeterminacy from a deterministic system.

\begin{theorem}[The UGP Decidability Phase Transition]
\label{thm:decidability_transition}
The logical structure of the Universal Generative Principle exhibits a sharp phase transition based on scope~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{Local Decidability:} Any property of a UGP trajectory that is restricted to a \textbf{finite window of states and a finite time horizon} is definable in First-Order Logic over the survivor space's cylinder sets and is therefore \textbf{decidable}.
    \item \textbf{Global Undecidability:} Properties concerning the \textbf{unbounded, long-term evolution} of a UGP trajectory (e.g., "Will this state ever reach a configuration with property P?") are, in general, \textbf{undecidable} and are $\Sigma^0_1$-complete.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*]
    \item \textbf{Local Decidability:} A finite window of states and a finite time horizon define a finite state space and a finite number of transitions. Any property on this space can be expressed as a finite Boolean combination of predicates on the integer registers (e.g., "$a_t < b_t$ AND $c_{t+1}$ is prime"). Such a statement belongs to the first-order theory of natural numbers with addition and multiplication (Presburger arithmetic, extended with a primality predicate). For bounded integers, this theory is decidable. A Turing machine can, in finite time, check all specified states and transitions and return a definitive true/false answer. This is formalized by our Theorem on "FO decidability on finite windows."
    \item \textbf{Global Undecidability:} The UWCA Universality Theorem proves that the UGP substrate can simulate any Turing machine via its Rule 110 embedding. The Halting Problem—determining whether an arbitrary Turing machine will halt on a given input—is the canonical example of a problem that is undecidable. We can construct a UGP initial state that encodes a specific Turing machine and its input. We can then define a property $P$ corresponding to the machine entering a "halt" state. The question "Will this UGP trajectory ever reach a state with property $P$?" is then logically equivalent to the Halting Problem for the encoded machine. Since the Halting Problem is undecidable, this general reachability question for UGP trajectories must also be undecidable. The problem is semi-decidable (recursively enumerable, or $\Sigma^0_1$) because one can simulate the trajectory and will find the state if it is ever reached; its complement is not. This makes the problem $\Sigma^0_1$-complete.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}
This theorem establishes a fundamental limit to prediction. While any local, finite question about the universe has a definite, computable answer, questions about the infinite future do not. This is not a statement about randomness or lack of information, but a deep truth about the limits of computation inherent in the fabric of a Turing-universal reality.

% =================================================
\section{The Nexus: Prime-Locked Minimality at n=10}\label{sec:nexus}

Having established the UGP's foundational principles---a rigid algebraic law (Pillar I) and a universal computational medium (Pillar II)---we now demonstrate their remarkable confluence~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}. We show that these abstract principles are not merely theoretical constructs. When instantiated at the specific level of $n=10$, they act as an inexorable filter on the substrate's computational possibilities, selecting a unique, minimal, and prime-locked solution from first principles. This section demonstrates how the general laws conspire to generate the specific, parameter-free GTE trajectory observed in our universe.

% =================================================

\subsubsection*{Formal map and constraints} 
The UGP defines a deterministic update $T$ acting on integer triples $(a,b,c)$ via a two–step pattern: odd steps use modular arithmetic and quotients, while even steps apply a Fibonacci increment $F_{|q_n-q_{n-1}|}$. The UGP–1 prime–locked ridge constrains $c_2=2^n-1$ and enforces $b_2 \mid (2^n-16)$ with $b_2>15$, ensuring $m_2=15$ and $q_2-q_1=13$.

\subsubsection*{Main theorem} 
At $n=10$, the only UGP–1 admissible seeds arise from the mirror pair $(b_2,q_2)=(24,42)$ and $(42,24)$, both yielding $b_1=73$ and distinct prime capacities $c_1\in\{823,2137\}$. The lexicographically minimal seed $(1,73,823)$ generates exactly the observed evolution 
\[
(1,73,823)\;\to\;(9,42,1023)\;\to\;(5,275,65535).
\]

\subsection{The Observed Trajectory at n=10}
\subsubsection*{Symbol glossary}
\begin{table}[h]
\centering
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.12}
\begin{tabular}{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule
Symbol & Description \\
\midrule
$n$ & Level parameter (bit-size, $n=10$ for main result) \\
$R_n$ & Ridge, $2^n-16$ \\
$b_2,q_2$ & Divisor pair of $R_n$ ($b_2q_2=R_n$), $b_2>15$ \\
$c_2$ & Second-generation capacity, $2^n-1$ \\
$q_1$ & $q_2-13$ (first-generation quotient) \\
$b_1$ & $b_2+q_2+7$ (first-generation $b$) \\
$c_1$ & $b_1q_1+20$ (first-generation capacity, prime-locked) \\
$m_1,m_2$ & Remainders: $c_1\!\bmod b_1$, $c_2\!\bmod b_2$ \\
$F_k$ & $k$th Fibonacci number ($F_1=F_2=1$) \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{Key symbols and their definitions.}
\end{table}
\subsubsection{The Three-Step Orbit}
Here $n$ denotes the level parameter (so $R_n = 2^n - 16$), while $t$ is the step index along the trajectory.

For even steps, define
\[
  c_{t+1} =
  \begin{cases}
    b_t q_t + 15, & \text{if not at a Mersenne boundary}, \\[6pt]
    2^{16}-1, & \text{if } c_t = 1023 = 2^{10}-1.
  \end{cases}
\]
This captures the Mersenne jump: once $c_2=1023$, the next step gives $c_3=65535$.

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=yellow!5,colframe=orange!70!black,title=\textbf{$c_3$ value: two definitions}]
Two distinct values of $c_3$ appear in this monograph and the formalization:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em,itemsep=0.2em]
  \item \textbf{Strict formula ($c_3 = 1023$):} Under the per-step rule $c_{t+1}=b_t q_t + k$, the even-step $c$-invariance theorem (\texttt{even\_step\_c\_invariance} in \texttt{ugp-lean}) gives $c_3 = c_2 = 1023 = 2^{10}-1$. This is the Lean-certified [T] result under the strict definition of $T$.
  \item \textbf{Extended information rule ($c_3 = 65535$):} Under the Mersenne-ladder extension above, $c_3 = 2^{16}-1 = 65535$. The canonical orbit $G_3 = (5,275,65535)$ is Lean-certified as \texttt{orbit\_determined\_by\_T} under the \texttt{canonicalGen3} definition. The exponent $k'=16$ is derived from the consecutive doubled-Fibonacci structure of the orbit: $n = 2F(5) = 10$ and $k' = 2F(6) = 16$, where the jump $k'-n = 2F(4) = 2N_c = 6$ follows from the Fibonacci recurrence $F(6)-F(5) = F(4) = N_c = 3$. Furthermore, $k'=16$ is the \emph{unique} smallest double Fibonacci number greater than $n=10$ (since 12 is not a double Fibonacci). Machine-checked in \texttt{ugp-lean} as \texttt{kprime\_is\_minimal\_double\_fib\_above\_n} and \texttt{c3\_phys\_formula} (zero sorry, module \texttt{GTE.MersenneLadder}). Status: \textbf{[B-structured]}.
\end{itemize}
Throughout this monograph, $c_3 = 65535$ refers to the extended (Mersenne-ladder, \textbf{[B-structured]}) value; $c_3 = 1023$ is the strict (\textbf{[T]}) value.
\end{tcolorbox}

\begin{definition}[Information rule for $c_n$]\label{def:info-rule}
A trajectory obeys the \emph{information rule} at level $n$ if
\[
c_{n+1}=b_n q_n+15\quad\text{and}\quad c_{n+1}=2^{k_{n+1}}-1\ \text{whenever $c_{n}$ attains }2^{k_n}-1,
\]
so that $c$ moves monotonically to (and between) Mersenne maxima within the two-step pattern. The UGP-1 ridge (\cref{def:UGP1-ridge} below) enforces $c_2=2^n-1$ and hence $m_2=15$~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.
\end{definition}

% =================================================
\subsubsection{The Prime-Locked Ridge Constraint}\label{sec:UGP1-ridge}
% =================================================

\begin{definition}[UGP-1 (prime-locked ridge)]\label{def:UGP1-ridge}
Fix a level $n\ge 10$ and let $R_n=2^n-16$~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}. A seed at level $n$ is determined by a divisor $b_2$ of $R_n$ with
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\Alph*), itemsep=2pt]
\item \label{def:q2} $b_2\mid R_n$ and $b_2>15$; set $q_2=R_n/b_2$;
\item $q_2>15$ (interior divisors: $\min\{b_2,q_2\} \ge 16$);
\item $c_2 := 2^n - 1$ (ridge choice; hence $m_2 = 15$);
\item \label{def:b1} $q_1 := q_2 - 13$; \quad $b_1 := b_2 + q_2 + 7$;
\item \label{def:prime} $c_1 := b_1 q_1 + 20$ is prime (prime-lock condition).
\end{enumerate}
The deterministic update for $c$ is as in Definition~\ref{def:ugp_update_map}.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Ridge remainder lock]\label{lem:m2}
Under UGP-1 at level $n$, with $c_2=2^n-1$ and $b_2 q_2=2^n-16$, we have
\[
m_2 \equiv c_2 \pmod{b_2} = 2^n-1 \equiv -1 \equiv 15 \pmod{b_2}.
\]
In particular $m_2=15$ since $0\le m_2<b_2$ and $b_2>15$~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.

\noindent\emph{Formalized in} \texttt{ugp-lean}: \texttt{Core.RidgeRigidity.ridge\_remainder\_lock}, \texttt{Core.RidgeRigidity.m2\_canonical}.
\end{lemma}

% Theorem statement only; proof is given later after supporting lemmas
\begin{theorem}[Minimality--duality]\label{thm:minimality-duality}
\noindent\emph{Formalized in} \texttt{ugp-lean}: \texttt{GTE.UniquenessCertificates.minimality\_duality\_n10}, \texttt{only\_survivors\_n10} (proves these are the \emph{unique} prime-locked pairs), \texttt{mdl\_c1\_n13\_is\_9007}, \texttt{mdl\_c1\_n16\_is\_46681} (MDL seeds at $n=13,16$ certified), \texttt{canonical\_seeds\_certified}.

At $n=10$, the only UGP-1 admissible seeds arise from the mirror pair $(b_2,q_2)=(24,42)$ and $(42,24)$, both yielding $b_1=73$ and distinct prime capacities $c_1\in\{823,2137\}$~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}. The lexicographically minimal seed $(1,73,823)$ generates exactly the observed evolution $(1,73,823)\to(9,42,1023)\to(5,275,65535)$. Formalized in ugp-lean: \texttt{Core.TripleDefs.LeptonSeed}, \texttt{Compute.Sieve.ridgeSurvivors\_10}, \texttt{Classification.TheoremB.mdl\_selects\_LeptonSeed}. This seed is independently proven to be the unique PSC-minimal survivor under the No External Model Selection (NEMS) closure constraints via the Unified Rigidity Theorem~\cite{SpivackUnifiedRigidity,SpivackNEMSHub}. The companion PSC-Optimality result establishes that the same closure constraints force the SM gauge group $SU(3)\times SU(2)\times U(1)$ and exactly three generations~\cite{SpivackNEMS05}.
\end{theorem}
