\caption{Schematic space--time diagram for the UWCA rolling update. Each cell on row $t{+}1$ is determined by the Rule~110 triple on row $t$ via the local gadget $e_i{=}0$.}
\label{fig:uwca-spacetime}
\end{figure}


\begin{lemma}[Local determinism]\label{lem:uwca-localdet}
Fix $i\in[-L,L]$ and freeze the $t{=}0$ neighbors $(x_{i-1,0},x_{i,0},x_{i+1,0})$. For the penalty $e_i$ of Definition~\ref{subsec:uwca} there exists a \emph{unique} $y\in B_{p_{i,1}}$ such that $e_i$ evaluates to $0$\\ when $x_{i,1}=y$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
By definition, $e_i$ is $0$ iff $x_{i,1}=\mathcal{R}(x_{i-1,0},x_{i,0},x_{i+1,0})$. The rule $\mathcal{R}$ is a function $\{0,1\}^3\to\{0,1\}$, hence outputs a single bit for any fixed triple. Since $B_{p_{i,1}}$ contains exactly two symbols $\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}$, there is exactly one $y$ giving $e_i=0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Sweep correctness]\label{lem:uwca-sweep}
With frozen $t{=}0$ layer, a left-to-right sweep over $i=-L,\dots,L$ that updates each $x_{i,1}$ to the minimizer in Lemma~\ref{lem:uwca-localdet} produces a configuration with $e_i=0$ for all $i$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Induction on $i$. At $i=-L$, neighbors are fixed ($x_{-L-1,0}$ is a boundary symbol), so Lemma~\ref{lem:uwca-localdet} sets $x_{-L,1}$ correctly. Assume $x_{j,1}$ is correct for all $j<i$; updating $x_{i,1}$ uses only $(x_{i-1,0},x_{i,0},x_{i+1,0})$, all fixed during the sweep, so the choice matches $\mathcal{R}$. Proceed to $i=L$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Boundary handling]\label{lem:uwca-boundary}
If $x_{-L,0}$ and $x_{L,0}$ are fixed boundaries, then the sweep of Lemma~\ref{lem:uwca-sweep} yields the correct next-row values for all $i\in[-L,L]$ under the intended CA boundary conditions.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Boundaries provide the missing neighbor symbols for $i=-L$ and $i=L$ in the local triples. Correctness follows by the same inductive argument.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Rolling-window simulation]\label{prop:uwca-roll}
Let $\mathrm{CA}_t$ denote the CA configuration at time $t$ on cells $[-L,L]$. Initialize the $t{=}0$ layer with $\mathrm{CA}_0$. For $s=0,\dots,T-1$, run the UWCA sweep to fill the $t{=}1$ layer with $\mathrm{CA}_{s+1}$, then copy it down as the new $t{=}0$ layer. After $T$ sweeps, the $t{=}1$ layer holds $\mathrm{CA}_T$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
By Lemmas~\ref{lem:uwca-localdet}–\ref{lem:uwca-boundary}, each sweep produces the correct CA next state. Copy-down preserves values exactly, so the process iterates without error.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[FO/clopen definability of $e_i$]\label{prop:uwca-clopen}
For fixed neighbors $(x_{i-1,0},x_{i,0},x_{i+1,0})$, the predicate $e_i=0$ is definable by a finite Boolean combination of coordinate predicates and hence is clopen in the Stone topology on $E$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The condition $x_{i,1}=\mathbf{\mathcal{R}}(x_{i-1,0},x_{i,0},x_{i+1,0})$ involves only finitely many coordinates and a fixed truth table. By Theorem~\ref{thm:clopen-fo}, any finite-support Boolean combination of residue predicates is clopen.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Global $e_i=0$ set is clopen]
The set $\{x\in\mathscr{X}: e_i(x)=0\ \forall i\}$ is the finite intersection of the clopens from Proposition~\ref{prop:uwca-clopen} and is thus clopen.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proposition}[No conflicting constraints]\label{prop:uwca-noconflict}
On a fixed two-layer window, the constraints $\{e_i=0\}$ have a unique satisfying assignment for the $t{=}1$ layer given the $t{=}0$ layer.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:uwca-localdet}, each $x_{i,1}$ is uniquely determined by its $t{=}0$ neighbors. Constraints on different $i$ do not interfere because they share at most one $t{=}1$ coordinate, which is fixed consistently by the shared neighbor triple.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Formal universality of UWCA]\label{thm:uwca-formal}
Let $\mathcal{R}$ be Rule~110. The UWCA defined in Definition~\ref{subsec:uwca} simulates $\mathcal{R}$ for any finite number of steps and hence is Turing-universal.

\noindent\emph{Formalized in} \texttt{ugp-lean}: \texttt{Universality.TuringUniversal.ugp\_is\_turing\_universal}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:uwca-roll} shows UWCA exactly reproduces the CA space–time evolution. Cook's theorem states Rule~110 is Turing-universal. Therefore UWCA can simulate any Turing machine.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[$\Sigma^0_1$-completeness of reachability]\label{thm:uwca-sigma1}
Let $P$ be a pattern over $\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}$. The decision problem\\
``$\exists\, T\ \exists$ initial row $w$ such that UWCA evolution from $w$ reaches a configuration containing $P$ at time $T$'' is $\Sigma^0_1$-complete.

\noindent\emph{Formalized in \texttt{ugp-lean}}: module \texttt{SelfRef.RiceHalting} (\texttt{ugp\_reachability\_undecidable\_from\_turing}).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Reduction from the halting problem for Turing machines simulated by Rule~110. Encode the machine and input as the CA's initial row, choose $P$ to be a designated halting marker. If the TM halts, the UWCA reaches $P$; if not, it never does. Hardness follows; membership in $\Sigma^0_1$ is by direct existential search.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Complexity preservation]\label{thm:uwca-overhead}
Let a CA of width $n$ run for $T$ steps. The UWCA simulation uses $T$ sweeps with exactly $O(n)$ local updates per sweep. Thus time and work overhead are $O(1)$ factors.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Each sweep updates each of the $n$ sites once. The rolling-window scheme uses $T$ sweeps, so total updates = $nT$. There is no blow-up in state size beyond the constant factor for two layers.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Reversible/energy-based variant]
By embedding $\mathcal{R}$ into a reversible CA or adding ancilla bits, one can define a UWCA with reversible local gadgets. At zero temperature in the Metropolis chain of Section~\ref{subsec:B6-zeroT}, energy minima then correspond to valid space–time diagrams of the reversible CA, providing an alternative universality proof in an energy minimization setting.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Undecidability via reachability]
Fix a computable family of windows with increasing $L$ and frozen boundaries. The predicate “there exists a UWCA trajectory from $t{=}0$ to a configuration satisfying property $P$ at time $T$” is $\Sigma_1^0$-complete for suitable $P$ (e.g., an encoded halting marker).
\end{corollary}

\begin{corollary}[Complexity preservation]
For circuit families embedded as $O(1)$-radius UWCA gadgets on width-$n$ windows, one sweep simulates a circuit layer with $O(n)$ local updates. Hence UWCA time overhead is linear in CA (or circuit) time.
\end{corollary}

\subsubsection{Complexity and reductions}
The $n$-level ridge scan is in $\mathbf{P}$ for fixed-size arithmetic; the search over $(n,b_2)$ pairs up to $n_{\max}$ is $O(n_{\max}\,\tau(R_n))$ where $\tau$ is the divisor-count function. Extensions to larger $n$ with unbounded $c_1$ primality fall into $\mathbf{NP}\cap\mathbf{coNP}$ under Pratt certificate verification.

\begin{remark}[Computational universality]
Encoding survivor dynamics as a transition system on $\mathscr{X}_P$ with local update rules yields a finite-state machine for each window. Direct product limits over $P$ produce a profinite automaton. Embedding arbitrary finite automata into suitable survivor subsystems suggests the possibility of universal computation within UGP-configured dynamics; formal universality proofs would require constructing reversible or Turing-complete subsystems.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Halting-problem analogy]
While UGP admissibility is decidable for fixed $n$, the question of whether there exist infinitely many $n$ with a given order-$k$ property (e.g., order-2 mirrors) may be undecidable in general, paralleling open problems in number theory (e.g., infinitude of twin primes).
\end{remark}

\subsection{Quantum Information and Mechanics}\label{sec:quantum-bridge}
% =================================================
We record a purely mathematical bridge: survivor cylinders support internal probability objects and phase maps; under mild compatibility one obtains Kolmogorov extensions and Born-type evaluation functionals, all stated within the topos $\mathbf{Sh}(E)$. The companion NEMS programme (Papers~45--47~\cite{SpivackNEMSHub}) further shows that nonlocality arises from closure constraints on the survivor algebra — specifically that no compiler internal to the UGP substrate can convert nonlocal correlations into superluminal signals; this is machine-checked in \texttt{nems-lean}.

\subsubsection{Cylinder probability objects and Kolmogorov extension}
Let $\mathcal{C}$ be the Boolean algebra generated by basic cylinders on a finite window $P$. A \emph{cylinder pre-measure} is a map $\mu_P: \mathcal{C}\to [0,1]$ that is finitely additive and compatible under restriction $I\subset P$.

\begin{theorem}[Kolmogorov extension on survivor cylinders]\label{thm:kolmogorov}
If for every inclusion $I\subset J$ the pre-measures satisfy $\mu_J\circ \pi_{I\leftarrow J}^{-1}=\mu_I$ and each $\mu_P$ is countably additive on $\mathcal{C}$, then there exists a unique Borel probability measure $\mu$ on the Stone space $E$ such that $\mu\big(\pi_P^{-1}(C)\big)=\mu_P(C)$ for all cylinder events $C\in\mathcal{C}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Standard Kolmogorov extension on inverse limits of finite discrete spaces; the Stone topology ensures regularity. Compatibility is precisely the projective consistency required by the theorem.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Phase presheaves and Born-type evaluations}
For each window $P$, let $\Phi_P$ be the abelian group of phase functions $\phi_P: \mathscr{X}_P\to S^1$. The assignments $P\mapsto \Phi_P$ form a presheaf $\underline{\Phi}$ on the CRT site with pullback along restrictions. Write $\mathcal{H}_P:=\ell^2(\mathscr{X}_P)$ and denote by $\langle \cdot,\cdot\rangle_P$ its inner product.

\begin{proposition}[Born-type evaluation on windows]\label{prop:born}
Given a family of unit vectors $\psi_P\in \mathcal{H}_P$ that is compatible under restriction, the rule $A\subseteq \mathscr{X}_P\mapsto \|\mathbf{1}_A\psi_P\|^2$ defines a finitely additive probability on $\mathcal{C}$, extending uniquely to a measure on $E$ by Theorem~\ref{thm:kolmogorov}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Noncontextuality domain]
Because cylinders are products of classical coordinates, the resulting valuations are noncontextual on the CRT site. Contextual phenomena would require adding incompatible observables; our current bridge remains within the classical (commutative) survivor algebra.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection{Gleason-type constraint (finite)}
Let $\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{H}_P)$ be the set of rank-one orthoprojectors on $\mathcal{H}_P$.

\begin{theorem}[Finite Gleason on survivor windows]\label{thm:gleason-finite}
Any frame function $w: \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{H}_P)\to [0,1]$ that is additive on orthonormal bases and compatible under cylinder restriction arises from a unique density operator $\rho_P$ via $w(\Pi)=\operatorname{tr}(\rho_P\Pi)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Finite-dimensional Gleason (dimension $\ge 3$) holds on $\mathcal{H}_P$; compatibility across $P$ ensures naturality of the family $\{\rho_P\}$ and descent to $E$ at the level of cylinders.
\end{proof}

% =================================================
\subsection{Cosmology and the Multiverse Landscape}\label{sec:cosmo-bridge}
% =================================================
We formalize the atlas of admissible seeds as a parameter space with natural measures and large-deviation estimates. The NEMS programme (Papers~79--80~\cite{SpivackNEMSHub}) establishes the formal Foundational Admissibility condition and the Classification Cascade that selects our universe from the admissible landscape; the present section provides the measure-theoretic substrate on which that classification operates.

\subsubsection{Parameter space and sigma-compactness}
Let $\mathfrak{A}:=\{(n,b_2): n\ge 10,\ b_2\mid (2^n-16),\ b_2>15\}$ with the discrete topology in $b_2$ and the usual topology in $n$ (identified with $\N$). For each $(n,b_2)$ define the indicator $\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{prime}}(n,b_2)$ for the prime-lock property.

\begin{proposition}[Sigma-compactness and projection]\label{prop:sigmacompact}
The subspace $\mathfrak{A}_{\mathrm{prime}}=\{(n,b_2)\in\mathfrak{A}: \mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{prime}}=1\}$ is $\sigma$-compact, and the projection $\pi(n,b_2)=n$ has finite fibers. Consequently, any locally finite measure $\nu$ on $\mathfrak{A}_{\mathrm{prime}}$ admits disintegration along $n$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
For fixed $n$ the fiber is finite (divisors of $2^n-16$ above 15). Countable union over $n$ of finite discrete sets gives $\sigma$-compactness.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Large deviations along ridges}
Let $X_n$ be the number of prime-locked $b_2$ on ridge $R_n$ and set $\delta(n)=X_n/\#\{b_2>15: b_2\mid R_n\}$.

\begin{theorem}[Exponential tail under independence ansatz]\label{thm:ldp}
Assume the prime-lock events across distinct divisor pairs on a fixed ridge are $\varepsilon$-weakly dependent with bounded correlations. Then there exist $c_1,c_2>0$ such that for any $t>0$,
\[
\Pr\big(|\delta(n)-\mathbb{E}[\delta(n)]|>t\big)\ \le\ c_1\, e^{-c_2 t^2 D(n)},
\]
where $D(n)=\#\{b_2>15: b_2\mid R_n\}$. In particular, order-2 mirrors are exponentially rare in $D(n)$ unless structurally enforced.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Apply Bernstein/Hoeffding inequalities under weak dependence (e.g., Janson’s inequality on dependency graphs) with $D(n)$ trials; rarity of simultaneous mirror survival follows by a union bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Selection principles]
Measures that weight lexicographically minimal $(c_1,b_1)$ concentrate on minimal branches when multiple survivors occur; this gives a mathematically clean selection prior on $\mathfrak{A}_{\mathrm{prime}}$ without physical interpretation.
\end{remark}

% =================================================
\subsection{Encryption and Pseudorandomness}\label{sec:crypto-bridge}
% =================================================
We outline cryptographic primitives suggested by the survivor product structure and spectral bounds of Section~\ref{sec:spectral-internal}.

 \subsection{One-way predicates from ridge facts}
 Fix $n\ge 10$ and let $R_n=2^n-16$. For any divisor $b_2>15$ of $R_n$, write $q_2:=R_n/b_2$ and set
 \[
 b_1:=b_2+q_2+7,\qquad q_1:=q_2-13,\qquad c_1:=b_1\,q_1+20.
 \]
 Define the map
 \[
 H_n:\ \{b_2>15:\ b_2\mid R_n\}\longrightarrow \mathbb{N},\qquad H_n(b_2):=c_1=b_1(q_2-13)+20.
 \]
 Deciding primality of $H_n(b_2)$ is easy for 64-bit ranges (Section~\ref{sec:comp-bridge}), but—given only $c_1=H_n(b_2)$ and $n$—recovering a preimage $b_2$ appears hard on average when the ridge $R_n=2^n-16$ has many divisors and the prime-lock filters are sparse. We formalize a family of \emph{ridge one-way predicates}:
 \begin{align*}
   \mathsf{ROP}_n(b_2) &:= \mathbf{1}\big[\ b_2\mid (2^n-16),\ b_2>15,\ \text{$b_1(b_2)(\tfrac{2^n-16}{b_2}-13)+20$ is prime}\ \big],\\
   H_n(b_2) &:= b_1(b_2)\Big(\frac{2^n-16}{b_2}-13\Big)+20,\qquad b_1(b_2):=b_2+\frac{2^n-16}{b_2}+7.
 \end{align*}
 \noindent\textbf{Assumption (Ridge preimage hardness).} For suitable $n$ (and random choice of a survivor window on the ridge), given $c_1=H_n(b_2)$ it is computationally infeasible to recover any valid preimage $b_2$ with nonnegligible probability over the ridge distribution. Under this assumption, $H_n$ is a preimage-resistant map on the survivor domain; combined with the mirror-invariance of $b_1$, this yields a natural 2-to-1 ambiguity when a canonical mirror exists (order-2 case), which we treat as intended pseudocollision resistance in that regime.

\subsection{PRG from survivor windows}\label{subsec:prg}
Fix a finite window $P$ of odd primes and let $\mathscr{X}_P=\prod_{p\in P} B_p$ be the survivor cylinder (Section~\ref{subsec:C1-FO}). Let $E_P:\mathscr{X}_P\to\{0,1\}^r$ be any extractor whose coordinate functions have nontrivial multiplicative Fourier support (Section~\ref{subsec:C3-ADH}). Define the keyed map
\[
  G_{n,P}(b_2;\,s)\ :=\ E_P\!\big(\pi_P(b_2,s)\big),
\]
where $\pi_P$ deterministically encodes $(b_2,s)$ into residues in $B_p$ via CRT with a collision-free schedule and $s$ is a short seed. Then, by Corollary~\ref{cor:extractor-bias}, for any affine test $L$ we have
\[
  \operatorname{bias}\big(L\circ G_{n,P}(b_2;\,s)\big)\ \le\ C^{|P|}\, b_\ast^{-|P|/2},
\]
with $b_\ast=\min_{p\in P}|B_p|$. Assuming ridge preimage hardness and a seed-hiding encoder $\pi_P$, distinguishing outputs of $G_{n,P}$ from uniform requires either inverting $H_n$ with nonnegligible probability or breaking the ADH bias bound, yielding a simple PRG heuristic on survivor windows.

\subsection{Commitment and coin flipping (sketch)}\label{subsec:commit}
\textbf{Commitment.} To commit to a message $M$, pick a fresh seed $s$, compute $c_1=H_n(b_2)$ and $y=G_{n,P}(b_2;s)$; publish $(c_1,y)$ as the commitment. \emph{Hiding} follows from extractor bias and seed entropy; \emph{binding} reduces to preimage hardness for $H_n$, up to the explicit mirror ambiguity when order-2 holds (which can be disambiguated by including $q_1\bmod 2$ or a hash of $(n,b_1)$ in the commitment). \textbf{Coin flipping.} Parties exchange commitments $(c_1^{(A)},y^{(A)})$, $(c_1^{(B)},y^{(B)})$; upon opening, the coin is $\bigoplus$ of a designated output bit of $y^{(A)},y^{(B)}$. Soundness inherits from hiding/binding and the ADH bias control.

\paragraph{Limitations and falsifiability.}
All number-theoretic results are unconditional and proved within the deterministic UGP core.  
Bridges to physics (e.g., mapping invariants to observed quantities) are conjectural and intended as testable hypotheses; they can be falsified by finding counterexamples in the arithmetic layer or by empirical physical data inconsistent with the mapped invariants.
