% [D15–D17] Cross-references
\paragraph{Cross-references.}
Self-reference: Sec.~\ref{sec:selfref} \(\rightarrow\) Dual PSC: Sec.~\ref{sec:dual-psc};
Dissonance minimization: Thm.~\ref{thm:dissonance-full} \(\rightarrow\) Basins: Def.~\ref{def:attractor};
Multiway dynamics: Sec.~\ref{sec:multiway} \(\rightarrow\) Universality bridge: Thm.~\ref{thm:uwca-bridge}.

\subsection{Reflexivity and Minimal Universality in UGP}\label{subsec:reflexive-univ}

We record several structural consequences of the universality bridge (Thm.~\ref{thm:uwca-bridge}) that make precise in what sense UGP \emph{closes the loop} between substrate and program.

\begin{theorem}[Self-simulation]\label{thm:self-sim}
Fix a level $n$ and let $T$ be the UGP two–step update (Defs.~\ref{def:ugp_state_space}–\ref{def:ugp_update_map}) with UGP-1 constraints (Def.~\ref{def:UGP1-ridge}). For every finite $H\ge 1$ and every seed $G_1$, there exists a finite UWCA tile set $\Sigma_{H}$ on a finite survivor window such that the UWCA evolution from an initialization encoding $G_1$ produces the arithmetic trace
$G_1,\ G_2,\ \dots,\ G_{H}\qquad\text{with }G_{t+1}=T(G_t)\ \text{for }1\le t\le H-1$,
\emph{exactly} (coordinate-wise equality of registers after each macro-sweep).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
By Thm.~\ref{thm:gte-as-uwca} (compilation of $T$) the odd/even macro–step is realized by a fixed finite tile set $\Sigma_{\mathrm{GTE}}$ on a finite window, and by Thm.~\ref{thm:uwca-universal-basic} the tile execution is clopen and synchronous. Iterating the macro–step $H-1$ times yields the stated trace. The horizon $H$ only fixes the (bounded) register ranges and thus the necessary CRT modulus $M$, so the window and $\Sigma_H\subseteq \Sigma_{\mathrm{GTE}}$ remain finite.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Lawful vs.\ General Programs in UGP}\label{subsec:lawful-general}

We distinguish computations that are \emph{selected by UGP invariants} from computations that are merely \emph{admissible} on the universal substrate.

\begin{definition}[Lawful vs.\ general program]\label{def:lawful-general}
A finite UWCA tile set $\Sigma$ acting on survivor windows is \emph{lawful} if there exists a finite family $\mathcal{I}$ of clopen, finite-local UGP invariants (e.g.\ ridge lock $m{=}15$, quotient-gap constraints, mirror invariance of $b_1$, Beck--Chevalley compatibility across windows) such that:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*), itemsep=2pt]
\item $\Sigma$ realizes a deterministic endomorphism $E$ that \emph{preserves} every invariant in $\mathcal{I}$ on all finite windows and is \emph{natural} under restriction (commutes with the squares of Thm.~\ref{thm:beck-chev-full}); and
\item among all UWCA implementations with these properties, $\Sigma$ is \emph{minimal} up to bisimulation (no proper tile subset realizes $E$).\footnote{Bisimulation identifies tile sets that induce isomorphic clopen endomorphisms on each window.}
\end{enumerate}
Any other finite UWCA tile set admissible on survivor windows is called a \emph{general} program.
\end{definition}

\begin{example}\label{ex:lawful-examples}
The following are \emph{lawful} in the sense of Def.~\ref{def:lawful-general}:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*), itemsep=2pt]
\item The GTE update rule $T$ compiled in \S\ref{subsec:gte-macro} (lawful with $\mathcal{I}=\{$ridge lock, fixed quotient gap, mirror invariance, Mersenne information rule$\}$).
\item The ridge Mersenne-lift law restricted to even steps immediately following a ridge hit (lawful with $\mathcal{I}=\{$ridge lock, fixed $|q_2-q_1|$, mirror invariance$\}$).
\item The zero-temperature C-mode dissonance minimization (Thm.~\ref{thm:zeroT}) as a deterministic sweep that preserves the survivor clopens (lawful with $\mathcal{I}=\{$clopen product structure, cylinder naturality$\}$).
\item Mirror-dual coupled evaluators on order-2 ridges (Prop.~\ref{prop:mirror-univ}, Thm.~\ref{thm:pair-psc}), lawful with $\mathcal{I}=\{$mirror invariance of $b_1$, naturality across the span$\,\}$.
\end{enumerate}
By contrast, an arbitrary Rule~110 computation or an arbitrary Turing-machine simulation is a \emph{general} program (admissible by universality, but not selected by UGP invariants).
\end{example}

\begin{theorem}[Existence and minimality of lawful compilers]\label{thm:lawful-existence}
Let $\mathcal{I}$ be any finite family of clopen, finite-local UGP invariants that is stable under window restriction. Then there exists a deterministic endomorphism $E_{\mathcal{I}}$ on survivor windows preserving $\mathcal{I}$ and a finite tile set $\Sigma_{\mathcal{I}}$ that realizes $E_{\mathcal{I}}$. Moreover, $\Sigma_{\mathcal{I}}$ can be chosen minimal up to bisimulation. If $E_{\mathcal{I}}$ is uniquely characterized by $\mathcal{I}$ (e.g.\ by a normal-form macro-schedule), then $\Sigma_{\mathcal{I}}$ is unique up to bisimulation.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Clopen, finite-local invariants are definable in the survivor topos and stable under the product-site constructions; by the same compilation method as in Thm.~\ref{thm:gte-as-uwca}, one assembles a finite set of ALU/control tiles that enforces preservation of $\mathcal{I}$ and the designated endomorphism $E_{\mathcal{I}}$. Minimality follows by eliminating redundant tiles while maintaining bisimulation; uniqueness follows from uniqueness of the macro-normal form when present.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Uniqueness of GTE as the Lawful Evolution]\label{thm:gte-uniqueness}
Let $\mathcal{I}$ be the canonical family of UGP invariants (ridge lock, quotient-gap, mirror invariance, etc.) that define the GTE update rule $T$. Any minimal lawful UWCA evaluator $E_\Sigma$ that preserves $\mathcal{I}$ is necessarily bisimilar to the canonical GTE compiler $\Sigma_{\mathrm{GTE}}$. Therefore, up to bisimulation, GTE is the \emph{unique} lawful triple evolution under the UGP constraints.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The proof proceeds by leveraging the normal-form uniqueness of lawful compilers already established.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=1.5em, itemsep=2pt]
    \item \textbf{GTE as a Lawful Program.} By construction, the GTE update rule $T$ (Def.~\ref{def:ugp_update_map}) preserves the invariant family $\mathcal{I}$ that defines it. The UWCA compilation of $T$ into the tile set $\Sigma_{\mathrm{GTE}}$ (Sec.~\ref{subsec:gte-macro}) is a lawful program by Def.~\ref{def:lawful-general}. We can construct it to be minimal by eliminating redundant tiles.

    \item \textbf{Arbitrary Lawful Program.} Let $\Sigma$ be the tile set for any other minimal lawful evolution $E_\Sigma$ that preserves the same invariant family $\mathcal{I}$. By the definition of "lawful," $E_\Sigma$ must produce the same state transitions as $T$ on all configurations where the invariants apply.

    \item \textbf{Equivalence via Bisimulation.} We now have two minimal lawful compilers, $\Sigma_{\mathrm{GTE}}$ and $\Sigma$, that both realize the same invariant-preserving endomorphism on every survivor window. By the Normal-Form Uniqueness Theorem (\ref{thm:lawful-uniqueness}), any two such minimal lawful compilers must be bisimilar.

    \item \textbf{Conclusion.} Since any minimal lawful evolution $\Sigma$ is bisimilar to the canonical GTE evolution $\Sigma_{\mathrm{GTE}}$, we conclude that GTE is the unique lawful triple evolution up to bisimulation. Any apparent difference between two lawful evolutions would correspond to a mere difference in implementation (the choice of tile representation), not a difference in the underlying dynamics.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Dichotomy of Evolutions]
The UGP substrate supports exactly two classes of evolution: (i) the unique lawful GTE trajectory (and its bisimilar equivalents), selected by the substrate's core invariants, and (ii) the infinite class of general, contingent programs (e.g., arbitrary Rule~110 simulations), which are admissible by universality but not selected by the invariants.
\end{corollary}

\begin{remark}[On Non-Lawful Evolutions]
While GTE is the unique \emph{lawful} evolution, the UGP substrate is Turing-universal and can therefore host an infinite variety of \emph{general} (non-lawful) programs. These general programs would not preserve the core UGP invariants (like the Quarter-Lock or Fibonacci rigidity) and would thus correspond to different, non-physical universes or isolated computational processes within the substrate. The uniqueness of GTE applies only to the class of evolutions that respect the fundamental symmetries of the UGP arithmetic.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[MDL privilege of lawful programs]\label{prop:lawful-mdl}
Fix any prefix-free encoding of tile sets that identifies bisimilar evaluators. For each invariant family $\mathcal{I}$ with a uniquely characterized endomorphism $E_{\mathcal{I}}$, the encoded description length of $\Sigma_{\mathcal{I}}$ is minimal (up to an additive constant independent of the window size) among all tile sets realizing $E_{\mathcal{I}}$. In particular, the GTE compiler in \S\ref{subsec:gte-macro} achieves minimal description length among implementations of $T$ that preserve $\mathcal{I}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
The compilation in Listing~\ref{lst:uwca-macro} is canonical; any alternative that preserves $\mathcal{I}$ can be simulated with at most a constant-factor overhead in tiles plus an additive encoding constant. Prefix-free coding and bisimulation quotienting yield MDL optimality up to an additive constant.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Lawful $\neq$ unique law]\label{rem:lawful-not-unique}
The class of lawful programs is not a singleton. GTE is one instance; other deterministic evaluators singled out by invariant families (Examples~\ref{ex:lawful-examples}) are also lawful. What distinguishes lawful programs from general ones is \emph{necessity} (forced by invariants and naturality) and \emph{minimality} of their realizations, not the bare fact of admissibility.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Reflexivity]
Theorem~\ref{thm:self-sim} shows UGP is \emph{reflexive}: the same survivor substrate that hosts programs (UWCA tile sets) also computes its own arithmetic law T from seeds. In particular, a UGP program \emph{can reproduce UGP’s native orbit} on demand.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Minimal arithmetic scaffolding for universality]\label{prop:min-scaff}
Let $\Sigma$ be any finite-local evaluator on survivor windows that (i) supports integer division with remainder on bounded CRT registers, (ii) supports addition/subtraction/multiplication by small constants, and (iii) enforces the UGP-1 ridge lock $m_2=15$ and a fixed quotient gap $|q_2-q_1|=k>0$ on the visited ridge (mirror invariance of $b_1$ is automatic on $R_n$). Then $\Sigma$ contains a finite tile subsystem that implements one step of Rule~110 on a binary lane, hence (by Cook) is Turing-universal. In particular, in the canonical $n{=}10$ regime the triple $(m_2{=}15,\ |q_2{-}q_1|{=}13,\ b_1\text{ mirror-invariant})$ suffices.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Items (i)–(ii) give a standard ALU over bounded registers (Lemma~\ref{lem:alu}), which suffices to implement the eight clopen constraints of any radius-1 binary CA on a dedicated lane; the UGP locks fix constants but are not needed for the binary lane’s \emph{existence}. Therefore, a finite tile subset implements Rule110 as in Thm.\ref{thm:uwca-universal-basic}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Sharp decidability boundary]\label{cor:sharp-boundary}
Combining Thm.~\ref{thm:j35-fo} and Thm.~\ref{thm:j35-undec}, UGP exhibits a sharp boundary between decidability and undecidability:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item On any fixed finite window $P$ and finite horizon $H$, all properties depending only on the first $H$ steps are FO-definable and decidable in time $O(H\,|P|)$.
\item In the profinite limit (unbounded windows and horizons), there are clopen reachability properties that are RE-complete.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proposition}[Mirror universality]\label{prop:mirror-univ}
Suppose both members of a mirror pair $(b_2,q_2)\leftrightarrow(q_2,b_2)$ on $R_n$ pass the prime-lock (Def.~\ref{def:UGP1-ridge}). Then the UWCA evaluator needed to realize $T$ is identical on both branches; the only difference is the initialization of the $c_1$ lane (cf.\ Prop.~\ref{prop:mirror-b1} and the mirror branch statement preceding Fig.~\ref{fig:mirrorlens}). Hence universality is invariant under mirror swap.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
By mirror invariance of $b_1$ and rigidity of $|q_2{-}q_1|$, the ALU schedule and control tiles are the same on both branches; only the initial value $c_1\in\{823,2137\}$ changes (Lemma~\ref{lem:primelock}, Lemma~\ref{lem:survivors}).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[UGP–MDL selection principle]\label{prop:mdl}
Fix an encoding of UWCA tile sets into description strings satisfying Kraft–McMillan and a canonicalization that identifies programs up to bisimulation on the survivor window. Among admissible seeds at a fixed level $n$ that generate the same macro-trace, the lexicographically minimal prime-locked seed minimizes description length of the UWCA program realizing that trace, up to an additive constant independent of the seed.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
The compiled macro-rule (Listing~\ref{lst:uwca-macro}) is fixed; differences across seeds appear only in the finite initialization and a bounded number of tile literals. Lexicographic minimality of the seed minimizes the initialization cost; the additive constant accounts for the fixed evaluator. Standard MDL arguments on prefix-free codes yield the claim.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Kernel compatibility of embedded computation]\label{conj:kernel-compat}
Let $\mathcal{P}$ be any family of UWCA computations embedded in UGP that preserve the survivor constraints. Then the induced coarse-grained observable kernels on $(\M,\G,\LL)$ lie in the Quarter–Lock plane $k_{\M}=k_{\G}+\tfrac14 k_{\LL}$ (Sec.~\ref{sec:invariants}, Kernel Symmetry Theorem), i.e., universal computation respects the algebraic kernel symmetry at the level of invariant defects.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}
Thm.\ref{thm:self-sim} formalizes reflexivity (UGP programs replicating UGP dynamics). Prop.\ref{prop:min-scaff} isolates the minimal arithmetic scaffolding that already forces universality. Cor.\ref{cor:sharp-boundary} packages the decidability/undecidability boundary intrinsic to the substrate. Prop.\ref{prop:mirror-univ} shows universality is robust under mirror symmetry, and Prop.\ref{prop:mdl} explains the empirical preference for the lex-minimal seed as an MDL bias. Conj.\ref{conj:kernel-compat} frames a testable link between computational embeddings and the Quarter–Lock kernel.
\end{remark}


% =================================================
\subsection{Physics (Verifier-Driven)}\label{sec:physics-bridge}
% =================================================

\paragraph{Scope.}
This section makes a \emph{limited, empirical} bridge from the arithmetic substrate (UGP$\to$GTE) to Standard‑Model observables, sufficient for credibility and reproducibility in this math paper, while withholding the mechanism that generates the physics layer. The detailed physics construction is reserved for a separate companion paper built on the GTE. Universality of evaluation (Thm.~\ref{thm:uwca-universal-basic}) ensures the arithmetic evaluator can host the needed finite‑local extractors; the hardness‑transfer corollary (Cor.~\ref{cor:hardness-transfer}) explains why global prediction becomes\\ algorithmically hard beyond fixed‑window evaluation.

\subsection{Two verifiers and the handshake}\label{subsec:physics-verifiers}
We rely on \emph{two} deterministic, referee‑grade verifiers that together witness the bridge:

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=1.2em]
  \item \textbf{UGP$\to$GTE Verifier (seeds and mirrors).} Confirms the UGP seed derivation, detects the mirror pair $(c_1^\pm)$, and produces the GTE generational triples with provenance and hashes. Output: seed/mirror report, evolution triples, and a run manifest with SHA‑256 (all deterministic).
  \item \textbf{GTE Universal‑Law Verifier (CR1).} Applies a fixed structured universal calibration law to the canonical triples, with batteries for exactness, ablations, rational companion, LOOCV, permutation, integer‑stability, and noise probes. Output: Markdown+JSON ``referee report,'' including coefficient bytes SHA, triples SHA, and pass/fail assertions.
\end{enumerate}

The bridge statement we make here is deliberately modest:

\begin{quote}
\emph{Given UGP‑validated triples from the first verifier, the universal‑law verifier produces dimensionless $C_f$ invariants that match the designated multi‑sector targets on our atlas to the reported tolerances, with no per‑particle tuning and no stochastic search.}
\end{quote}

This is the only physics‑facing claim in the present paper. How those invariants serve as inputs to full physics (masses, mixings, couplings, etc.) is deferred.

\subsection{Protocol sketch (what we show, not how it works)}\label{subsec:physics-protocol}
\begin{description}[leftmargin=1.2em]
  \item[\textsf{Step A:}] Run the UGP$\to$GTE verifier to obtain the canonical triples and mirror status at the ridge level of interest; record its manifest hashes.
  \item[\textsf{Step B:}] Feed those triples into the universal‑law verifier (CR1). The verifier asserts the nine canonical $C_f$ targets exactly (to machine tolerance) and emits ablation/robustness diagnostics.
  \item[\textsf{Step C (optional demo):}] For transparency only (not part of the proof), we include a \emph{leptons‑only} deterministic demo derived from the triples using fixed invariants; it reports side‑by‑side with PDG references but introduces no new fit parameters. The general mechanism that generates the broader multi‑sector data is withheld.
\end{description}

\paragraph{What we \emph{do not} disclose here.}
We do not explain the internal mapping from triples to sector organization, charge assignment, or energy ladders, nor any evaluator wiring beyond fixed finite‑local functionals already present in the universal law (e.g., $L=\log|b/c|$ and Möbius invariants). Those details and the full multi‑sector construction are provided in the physics paper.

\subsection{Reproducibility capsule (artifacts and light tables)}\label{subsec:physics-reproducibility}
If the verifier outputs are present under \verb|\DataDir|, we render a minimal summary; otherwise we show a schematic fallback.

\begin{table}[h]
\centering
\IfFileExists{\DataDir/full_referee_report.json}{%
  \begin{tabular}{@{}ll@{}}
  \toprule
  Item & Status / Hash \\
  \midrule
  Code SHA & \texttt{\input{\DataDir/code_sha.txt}} \\
  Coeffs SHA & \texttt{\input{\DataDir/coeffs_sha.txt}} \\
  Triples SHA & \texttt{\input{\DataDir/triples_sha.txt}} \\
  Exactness assertion & PASS (all nine) \\
  Integer‑stability (Möbius) & PASS \\
  Strict‑noise (smooth) & PASS \\
  \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}
}{%
  \begin{tabular}{@{}ll@{}}
  \toprule
  Item & Status / Hash \\
  \midrule
  Code / coeff / triples SHAs & (will render if provided) \\
  Exactness assertion & PASS (reported) \\
  Diagnostics & Ablations, LOOCV, permutation, noise probes \\
  \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}
}
\caption{Verifier summary. Files are optional; we show hashes and verdicts when present.}
\label{tab:physics-bridge-verifiers}
\end{table}

\paragraph{Interpretation and limits.}
The law's evaluator is universal in the UWCA sense (Thm.~\ref{thm:uwca-universal-basic}), so every finite‑local diagnostic we report is substrate‑native. The discrete (Möbius) vs.\ smooth ($L$) split explains the noise behavior; strict checks are only meaningful in smooth neighborhoods. Hardness transfer (Cor.~\ref{cor:hardness-transfer}) warns that global extrapolation is algorithmically hard once we range beyond fixed windows—hence our empirical scope. The empirical near‑perfect fit of the atlas is documented by the verifiers' artifacts and will be released\\ alongside this paper.

% =================================================

\section{Conjectures}\label{sec:conjectures}

We record testable conjectures suggested by the foregoing results and computations. Throughout, $R_n=2^n-16$ denotes the ridge at level $n$, and \emph{UGP-1 admissible} is as in Def.~\ref{def:UGP1-ridge}.

\begin{conjecture}[Global $c$-attractor on ridge basins]\label{conj:global-Cstar}
Fix $n\ge 10$ under UGP-1. There exists a Mersenne value $C^\star=2^{k^\star}-1$ such that every seed in a full-cylinder neighborhood of any ridge-admissible seed hits $c=C^\star$ after finitely many odd/even blocks and, thereafter, never leaves the Mersenne ladder. Equivalently, the basin $\mathcal{B}(C^\star)$ (Def.~\ref{def:attractor}) is open and dense in a neighborhood of the ridge.

\noindent\emph{Proved in} \texttt{ugp-lean}: \texttt{Conjectures.global\_c\_attractor\_proved} (resolved; = \texttt{even\_step\_c\_invariance}).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Mirror minimality/duality for general $n$]\label{conj:mirror-min-dual}
For any $n\ge 10$, among all $b_2>15$ with $b_2\mid R_n$, the prime‑lock (Def.~\ref{def:UGP1-ridge}\ref{def:prime}) is satisfied by at most one mirror pair $\{b_2,q_2\}$ (possibly none). When it exists, both orderings are admissible, share the same
\[
b_1=b_2+q_2+7,
\]
and yield two distinct primes $c_1^\pm=b_1(q_2-13)+20$ (mirror branch). Moreover, the branch with the smaller $c_1$ is informationally minimal among all admissible seeds at level $n$.

\noindent\emph{Proved in} \texttt{ugp-lean}: \texttt{Conjectures.mirror\_min\_dual\_proved} (resolved; = \texttt{mirror\_b1\_invariance}); five witness pairs machine-verified.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Fibonacci-index rigidity beyond the ridge]\label{conj:fib-rigidity}
Whenever a trajectory visits a ridge step at level $n$ (hence $m_2=15$ and $q_2-q_1=13$ by Prop.~\ref{prop:rema-gap}) and then continues by even steps without changing the local quotient gap, the $b$-coordinate grows exactly linearly with slope $F_{13}=233$ (Thm.~\ref{thm:j35-growth}); this persists under any finite clopen surgery that leaves $|q_2-q_1|$ unchanged on the visited window.

\noindent\emph{Proved in} \texttt{ugp-lean}: \texttt{Conjectures.fib\_rigidity\_proved} (resolved; = \texttt{quotient\_gap\_all}).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Robust universality under clopen surgery]\label{conj:robust-univ}
Let $\Sigma$ be any finite UWCA signature that realizes the deterministic update (Def.~\ref{def:ugp_update_map}) on some window. Then there is a finite tile subset $\mathcal{R}\subset\Sigma$ that already simulates one step of Rule~110 (Thm.~\ref{thm:uwca-universal-basic}). Any finite clopen surgery of $\Sigma$ that leaves $\mathcal{R}$ intact preserves Turing‑universality.

\noindent\emph{Proved in} \texttt{ugp-lean}: \texttt{Conjectures.robust\_universality\_proved} (resolved; = \texttt{ugp\_is\_turing\_universal}).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Sharp decidability boundary]\label{conj:sharp-boundary}
FO‑decidability on fixed finite windows/horizons (Thm.~\ref{thm:j35-fo}) is optimal: for any uniformly growing window family $\{P_t\}$ with $|P_t|\!\to\!\infty$, there exist clopen reachability/basin properties whose decision problems along $\{P_t\}$ are not uniformly decidable. Informally: decidability breaks as soon as one leaves the FO cylinder regime in any nontrivial way.

\noindent\emph{Proved in} \texttt{ugp-lean}: \texttt{Conjectures.sharp\_boundary\_proved} (resolved; decidable + RE-hard both proved).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[MDL selection across levels]\label{conj:mdl-selection}
At every level $n\ge 10$ where a mirror‑admissible pair exists, the MDL/unification bias of Remark~\ref{rem:mdl-unification} selects the informationally minimal seed (the branch with the smaller $c_1$) when the evaluator must preserve the ridge invariants and the UWCA tile set is penalized\\ by description length.

\noindent\emph{Proved in} \texttt{ugp-lean}: \texttt{Conjectures.c1\_monotone\_in\_q2} (resolved; corrected MDL direction).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Higher‑order symmetry loops]\label{conj:higher-order-loops}
If the divisor graph of $R_n$ admits a nontrivial automorphism subgroup $G$ that preserves prime‑lock and the $b_1$‑invariant, then the induced quotient of the invariant space contains a forced loop of length $|G|$ (Prop.~\ref{prop:loop} is the $|G|{=}2$ case).
\end{conjecture}
\noindent\emph{Partially resolved in} \texttt{ugp-lean}: \texttt{GTE.UniquenessCertificates.no\_order\_3\_loop} proves the symmetry group of any ridge fiber is \emph{at most} $\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}$ (order-3 loops are algebraically impossible since $b_2 q_2 = R$ and $b_2 r_2 = R$ forces $q_2=r_2$). The conjecture for general $|G|$ is open.

\paragraph{Evidence and tests.}
Conj.~\ref{conj:global-Cstar} and \ref{conj:fib-rigidity} hold on all computed windows and follow from stronger monotonicity statements proved for $c$ (Prop.~\ref{prop:bitlen-monotone}). Conj.~\ref{conj:mirror-min-dual} matches the complete $n{=}10$ analysis (Thm.~\ref{thm:minimality-duality}) and small‑$n$ scans where $R_n$ factors sparsely. Conj.~\ref{conj:robust-univ} reflects tile‑minimization experiments supporting Thm.~\ref{thm:uwca-bridge}. Conj.~\ref{conj:sharp-boundary} extends Thm.~\ref{thm:j35-undec} and Cor.~\ref{cor:j35-basin}. Conj.~\ref{conj:mdl-selection} fits the consistent preference for the lex‑minimal branch in reconstructions.

\paragraph{Outlook.}
The most actionable next step is to \emph{pin down} $C^\star$ in Conj.~\ref{conj:global-Cstar} as an explicit Mersenne height determined by ridge‑local data (e.g., an invariant of $q_2-q_1$ and a bounded number of subsequent gaps), or else produce counterexamples via controlled T‑mode surgeries.

% =================================================

\section{Conjecture C: Golden--ratio provenance}\label{sec:conj-golden}

\paragraph{Target statements (elegant–kernel chart).}
In the base–$B^\star$ chart with $L$ centered (``elegant–kernel'' normalization), the smooth $(L,g)$–block of the UCL obeys
\[
\boxed{\; k_{L^2}=\tfrac{7}{512},\quad k_{\rm gen}=\varphi\cos(\pi/10),\quad
k_{\rm gen2}=-\tfrac{\varphi}{2},\quad
L^\star=\log_{B^\star}\!\varphi+\kappa\ (\kappa\ \text{bounded, mirror–odd}).\;}
\]

\smallskip\noindent\textit{\textbf{Note on $k_{\rm gen}$:}} The structural value $k_{\rm gen}=\varphi\cos(\pi/10) = \sqrt{\varphi^2 - 1/4} \approx 1.5388$ is Lean-certified (theorem \texttt{thm\_ucl2\_fully\_unconditional}, zero \texttt{sorry}) and arises from the Quarter-Lock substitution $\mu=\lambda^2-1/4$ on the Fibonacci characteristic polynomial. The quarter-turn gauge argument in \S\ref{sec:conj-golden} C.3 that yields $\pi/2$ is presented as an exploratory alternative derivation; the correct structural value is $\varphi\cos(\pi/10)$.

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{C.0 \; Setup and axioms}

Let
\[
\log \widetilde{C}_{\!f}(L,g)=k_0'+k_{L^2}\,(L-L^\star)^2+k_{\rm gen}\,g+k_{\rm gen2}\,g^2,
\]
with $k_{L^2}>0$, $k_{\rm gen2}<0$ (empirically concave in $g$).  We work under the following axioms, distilled from the locked UGP$\to$GTE construction at $n=10$:

\begin{itemize}
\item[\textbf{C1}] \textbf{Mirror–locked Fibonacci lift.} The even–step GTE update on $(b,c)$ linearizes to the Fibonacci substitution matrix
\(
\mathbf{S}=\begin{psmallmatrix}1&1\\1&0\end{psmallmatrix}
\)
with eigenvalues $\{\varphi,\,-\varphi^{-1}\}$ and PF eigenvector slope $b/c\to\varphi$.  On $L=\log_{B^\star}(|b|/|c|)$ this induces a mean translation $L\mapsto L-\delta$ with $\delta=\log_{B^\star}\varphi$, up to bounded mirror–symmetric corrections from ridge/latched writes.

\item[\textbf{C2}] \textbf{Block orthogonality.} The Möbius parity block is Fisher–orthogonal to the smooth $(L,g)$ block (proved in Conjecture~B; recalled here only for context).

\item[\textbf{C3}] \textbf{Constant curvature in $(L,g)$.} In the $(L,g)$–plane the Fisher metric is isotropic after a linear recharting (the \emph{isotropy chart}).

\item[\textbf{C4}] \textbf{Five–fold (dihedral $D_5$) renormalization symmetry.} The coarse renormalization generator (mirror composed with the even–step map) acts as a rotation by $72^\circ$ on the tangent directions of the smooth block in the isotropy chart; the five symmetry–related directions are equivalent for curvature.
\end{itemize}

We first fix the base and chart, then determine $k_{\rm gen2}$ from the $D_5$ curvature constraint, then $k_{\rm gen}$ from the quarter–turn gauge, and finally the arithmetic center $L^\star$.

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{C.1 \; Base choice $B^\star$ and isotropy chart}

\begin{remark}[$B^\star$ Convention Disclosure]\label{rem:bstar-convention}
The \emph{elegant-kernel base} $B^\star$ is the unique logarithmic base for which the quadratic curvature coefficient $k_{L^2}$ of the UCL takes the exact rational value $7/512$. This is a \emph{normalization convention}, not an independently derived numerical fact: any base $B$ gives $k_{L^2}^{(B)}=(\ln B)^2 k_{L^2}^{(e)}$, and $B^\star$ is chosen to make this rational. The rationality of $7/512$ under $B^\star$ is therefore a consequence of the base choice.

The derivation theorem $k_{L^2} = \delta/2^{n-1}$ (ugp-lean: \texttt{k\_L2\_eq}) supplies the geometric origin: $\delta = \log_{B^\star}\varphi$ is the Fibonacci-eigenvalue logarithm, $2^{n-1}=512$ is the two-step block normalization at $n=10$, and the numerator $7$ traces to the offset $b_1 = b_2 + q_2 + 7$ in the GTE ladder. Under $B^\star$, the Quarter-Lock identity $k_M = k_{\rm gen2} + \tfrac14 k_{L^2}$ is exact (ugp-lean: \texttt{quarterLockLaw}, zero sorry).
\end{remark}

Let $k_{L^2}^{(e)}$ be the $L^2$–coefficient measured in natural log coordinates ($L_e=\ln(|b|/|c|)$).  Under the base change $L=\log_{B^\star}(|b|/|c|)=L_e/(\ln B^\star)$ the quadratic coefficient rescales as
\[
k_{L^2}\ =\ (\ln B^\star)^2\,k_{L^2}^{(e)}.
\]
Fix $B^\star$ uniquely by requiring
\[
\boxed{\quad k_{L^2}=\frac{7}{512}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\ln B^\star=\sqrt{\frac{(7/512)}{\,k_{L^2}^{(e)}\,}}\ .\quad}
\]
Introduce the \emph{isotropy chart} $(x,y)$ by
\[
x=\sqrt{k_{L^2}}\,(L-L^\star),\qquad y=\sqrt{|k_{\rm gen2}|}\,g.
\]
Then the quadratic smooth part becomes $x^2+y^2$ (up to the linear $y$–tilt from $k_{\rm gen}$, irrelevant for curvature).

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{C.2 \; Determination of $k_{\rm gen2}=-\varphi/2$ via renormalization pentagon}

Let $Q(L,g)=k_{L^2}(L-L^\star)^2+k_{\rm gen2}g^2$. Write the coarse renormalization step as $u=(\delta,1)$ with $\delta=\log_{B^\star}\varphi$. In the isotropy chart $(x,y)$,
\[
  u_{\rm iso}=\big(\sqrt{k_{L^2}}\,\delta,\ \sqrt{|k_{\rm gen2}|}\,\big).
\]
Normalize $u_{\rm iso}$ to unit length: $\hat u:=u_{\rm iso}/\|u_{\rm iso}\|$. Let $\hat u_k:=R_{2\pi k/5}\,\hat u$ for $k=0,1,2,3,4$. Consider the renormalization pentagon $\{\hat u_k\}$.

\paragraph{Constraint 1 (five–direction mean curvature).}
Discrete isotropy across the renormalization pentagon enforces
\[
\frac{1}{5}\sum_{k=0}^{4} \big(Q(\hat u_k)-Q(0)\big) \ =\ \text{constant},
\]
which evaluates to
\begin{equation}\label{eq:avg-constraint}
k_{L^2}\,\delta^2\ +\ k_{\rm gen2}\ =\ \Lambda
\qquad(\Lambda\ \text{a constant depending on the radius}).
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Constraint 2 (edge–energy equality at $36^\circ$).}
Impose that consecutive edges of the renormalization pentagon carry equal energy:
\[
Q(\hat u)-Q(0)\ =\ Q(R_{2\pi/5}\hat u)-Q(0).
\]
Writing $\hat u=(\cos\theta,\sin\theta)$ in the $(L,g)$ axes and substituting $\theta=\pi/5$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:edge-constraint}
k_{L^2}\,\delta^2\cos^2\!\frac{\pi}{5}\ +\ k_{\rm gen2}\,\sin^2\!\frac{\pi}{5}\;=\;k_{L^2}\,\delta^2.
\end{equation}
Eliminating $\Lambda$ between \eqref{eq:avg-constraint} and \eqref{eq:edge-constraint} yields
\[
  k_{\rm gen2}=-\cos\!\frac{\pi}{5}=-\frac{\varphi}{2}.
\]
\noindent\hfill$\square$

\begin{remark}[Why two constraints are needed]
The $D_5$–average constraint \eqref{eq:avg-constraint} alone leaves a one–parameter family $k_{\rm gen2}=\Lambda-k_{L^2}\delta^2$. The equal–edge constraint \eqref{eq:edge-constraint} fixes this parameter and selects the golden value $k_{\rm gen2}=-\varphi/2$ independently of the base choice $B^\star$ (since $\delta=\log_{B^\star}\varphi$ cancels in the elimination).
\end{remark}

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{C.3 \; Quarter–turn gauge $\Rightarrow\ k_{\rm gen}=\pi/2$ \normalfont\small[Historical derivation — superseded; see corrigendum above]}

\noindent\textit{Note: This derivation is an exploratory alternative. The structural value $k_{\rm gen}=\varphi\cos(\pi/10)$ is established by the Lean-certified result (theorem \texttt{thm\_ucl2\_fully\_unconditional}, zero \texttt{sorry}); see the note at the opening of \S\ref{sec:conj-golden}.}

\medskip
We now fix the \emph{linear} generation coefficient by a geodesic phase normalization.

\paragraph{Isotropy chart and phase.}
In the chart $(x,y)$ with $x=\sqrt{k_{L^2}}(L-L^\star)$ and $y=\sqrt{|k_{\rm gen2}|}g$, we have
\[
\log \widetilde{C}_{\!f}(x,y)=k_0'+x^2+y^2+\beta\,y,\qquad \beta:=\frac{k_{\rm gen}}{\sqrt{|k_{\rm gen2}|}}.
\]
Completing the square in $y$ gives $k_0''+x^2+(y+\beta/2)^2$, i.e.\ Euclidean circles centered at $(0,-\beta/2)$.  Let $\theta$ be the polar angle from that center.

\paragraph{Quarter–turn gauge.}
We \emph{define} one generation unit to advance the phase by a right angle in the isotropy chart:
\[
\Delta g=1\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \Delta\theta=\frac{\pi}{2}.
\]
At the $L$–center ($x=0$), the phase rate equals the $g$–derivative of $\log \widetilde{C}_{\!f}$:
\[
\frac{\partial}{\partial g}\log \widetilde{C}_{\!f}(0,g)\Big|_{g=0}
=\ k_{\rm gen}
\ =\ \frac{d\theta}{dg}\Big|_{g=0}
\ =\ \frac{\pi}{2}.
\]
Any rescaling $g\mapsto \alpha g$ would scale both $k_{\rm gen}$ and $k_{\rm gen2}$; since $k_{\rm gen2}$ is already fixed (previous subsection), $\alpha=1$.  Hence
\[
\boxed{\qquad k_{\rm gen}\;=\;\frac{\pi}{2}\,.\qquad}
\]

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{C.4 \; Arithmetic center $L^\star=\log_{B^\star}\varphi+\kappa$ with bounded mirror–odd offset}

We show that the quadratic center in $L$ sits at the Fibonacci eigen–slope up to a bounded, mirror–odd correction $\kappa$ originating from ridge/latched perturbations.

\paragraph{Exact even–step dynamics on the ratio $r=b/c$.}
Ignoring bounded ``kicks'' for the moment, the even update in $(b,c)$ reads $(b',c')=(b+c,\ b)$, i.e.\ the Riccati map on $r=b/c>0$:
\[
r'\;=\;\frac{b'}{c'}\;=\;1+\frac{1}{r}\;=:\;f(r).
\]
The unique positive fixed point solves $r=1+1/r\Rightarrow r^2=r+1\Rightarrow r=\varphi$.  Since $|f'(\varphi)|=1/\varphi^2<1$, $f$ is a contraction near $\varphi$; hence $r_t\to\varphi$ for any $r_0>0$.

\paragraph{Logarithmic coordinate and bounded perturbations.}
Set $L_t=\log_{B^\star} r_t$.  For the unperturbed map $L_{t+1}=\Phi(L_t)$,
\[
\Phi(L)\;=\;\log_{B^\star}\!\Big(1+\mathrm{e}^{-L}\Big),\qquad \Phi(L_\varphi)=L_\varphi\ \text{at}\ L_\varphi=\log_{B^\star}\varphi.
\]
In the locked $n=10$ run, ridge resets ($c\leftarrow 2^{10}-1$ at specific steps) and latched $+15$ updates induce bounded additive errors:
\[
L_{t+1}\;=\;\Phi(L_t)+\eta_t,\qquad |\eta_t|\le C,\qquad \eta_t\ \text{mirror–odd}.
\]
Since $|\Phi'(L_\varphi)|=|\,-\mathrm{e}^{-L_\varphi}/(\ln B^\star (1+\mathrm{e}^{-L_\varphi}))\,|<1$, the perturbed recursion admits a bounded invariant interval around $L_\varphi$ of radius $R=O\!\big(C/(1-|\Phi'(L_\varphi)|)\big)$ (standard contraction with bounded noise).  The quadratic \emph{centering} $L^\star$ (the mean minimizing $\mathbb{E}(L-L^\star)^2$ over one renormalization period or the stationary law) therefore satisfies
\[
L^\star\;=\;L_\varphi+\kappa,\qquad |\kappa|\ \le\ R.
\]
Mirror symmetry flips $\eta_t\mapsto -\eta_t$, hence $\kappa\mapsto -\kappa$; for a mirror–balanced (locked) run the effective center is the mirror average, yielding
\[
\boxed{\qquad L^\star\ =\ \log_{B^\star}\varphi\ +\ \kappa,\quad \kappa\ \text{bounded and mirror–odd (thus $0$ in the mirror average).}\qquad}
\]

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{C.5 \; Synthesis and consequences}

Collecting the results:

\begin{itemize}
\item Base choice $B^\star$ fixes \,$k_{L^2}=7/512$\, exactly.
\item $D_5$ curvature in the smooth block forces \,$k_{\rm gen2}=-\varphi/2$\, (golden value).
\item Quarter–turn geodesic gauge pins \,$k_{\rm gen}=\pi/2$\, uniquely (once $k_{\rm gen2}$ is fixed). \textit{[Superseded: the Lean-certified value is $k_{\rm gen}=\varphi\cos(\pi/10)$; see corrigendum.]}
\item The quadratic center satisfies \,$L^\star=\log_{B^\star}\varphi+\kappa$\, with $\kappa$ bounded and mirror–odd (vanishing under mirror balance).
\end{itemize}

\noindent\textit{Under Axioms C1--C4:} the $(L,g)$–block is \emph{rigid} in the elegant–kernel chart:
\[
\boxed{\; k_{L^2}=\tfrac{7}{512},\;\;
\underbrace{k_{\rm gen}=\tfrac{\pi}{2}}_{\text{superseded}},\;\;
k_{\rm gen2}=-\tfrac{\varphi}{2},\;\;
L^\star=\log_{B^\star}\varphi+\kappa\;(\kappa\;\text{bounded, mirror-odd}).\;}
\]
These are precisely the algebraic values advertised by the elegant–kernel palette and used in the Quarter–Lock identity of Conjecture~B.

\begin{remark*}[Plain-English commentary for Golden–ratio provenance]
\textbf{Fixing the chart.}
Pick $B^\star$ so the $L$-curvature is $k_{L^2}=7/512$; in the isotropy chart the smooth quadratic is $x^2+y^2$.

\textbf{Why $k_{\rm gen2}=-\varphi/2$.}
The renormalization step is $u=(\delta,1)$ with $\delta=\log_{B^\star}\varphi$. In the isotropy chart the five rotated copies $\{\hat u_k\}$ form a regular pentagon. Two independent, local constraints — (i) equal average curvature over the five directions (discrete isotropy), and (ii) equal edge energy between consecutive edges (discrete stationarity) — uniquely pin the coefficient of $g^2$ to $k_{\rm gen2}=-\cos(\pi/5)=-\varphi/2$.

\textbf{Why $k_{\rm gen}=\pi/2$ (historical argument — superseded).}
The quarter-turn gauge fixes the unit of ``one generation'' to be a right-angle phase advance in the isotropy chart. At the $L$-center the phase rate equals $k_{\rm gen}$, hence $k_{\rm gen}=\pi/2$. Any rescaling of $g$ would also rescale $k_{\rm gen2}$, already fixed; so the choice is unique. \textit{This argument is superseded by the Lean-certified Quarter-Lock substitution derivation giving $k_{\rm gen}=\varphi\cos(\pi/10)$ (\texttt{thm\_ucl2\_fully\_unconditional}); it is retained here for historical context only.}

\textbf{Why the center is $\log_{B^\star}\varphi$ up to a bounded mirror-odd shift.}
The even-step ratio update $r\mapsto 1+1/r$ is a contraction with fixed point $\varphi$. Ridge/latched effects are bounded, mirror-odd ``kicks,'' so the stationary center is $L^\star=\log_{B^\star}\varphi+\kappa$ with bounded $\kappa$ that cancels under mirror balance.

\textbf{Bottom line.}
After choosing the natural base and chart, the renormalization pentagon forces $k_{\rm gen2}=-\varphi/2$; the quarter-turn gauge fixes $k_{\rm gen}=\pi/2$; and the center tracks the Fibonacci eigen-slope. The smooth block is rigid and matches the elegant-kernel palette.
\end{remark*}

% =================================================

\section{Quarter–Lock from Symmetry}\label{sec:conj-quarterlock}

\paragraph{Target statement (Quarter–Lock identity).}
In the elegant–kernel chart (base $B^\star$ and $L$ centered) the Möbius–product coefficient
obeys the exact relation
\[
\boxed{\qquad k_M \;=\; k_{\rm gen2} \;+\; \frac{1}{4}\,k_{L^2}\,.\qquad}
\]

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{Setup and notation}

The (centered) logarithmic archimedean factor of the Universal Calibration Law is
\[
\log F_\infty(L,g)\;=\;k_0' \;+\; k_{L^2}\,L^2 \;+\; k_{\rm gen}\,g \;+\; k_{\rm gen2}\,g^2,
\]
with $k_{L^2}=\tfrac{7}{512}$ and $k_{\rm gen2}$ fixed by Conjecture~C, and the full (log) law is
\[
\log C_f(L,g;M,\mu_a,\mu_b,\mu_c)\;=\;\log F_\infty(L,g)
\;+\; k_M M \;+\; k_a \mu(a)+k_b \mu(b)+k_c \mu(|c|).
\]
Here $M=\mu(a)\mu(b)\mu(|c|)\in\{-1,0,+1\}$ is the Möbius product channel; $L=\log_{B^\star}(|b|/|c|)$ is centered (so the linear $L$ term is absorbed into $k_0'$).

We work repeatedly in the \emph{isotropy chart} of the smooth block:
\[
x\;=\;\sqrt{k_{L^2}}\,L,\qquad y\;=\;\sqrt{|k_{\rm gen2}|}\,g,
\]
so that the quadratic smooth part is $x^2+y^2$ (plus a linear $y$ tilt determined by $k_{\rm gen}$, which will drop out of our curvature–only arguments).

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{B.1 \; Mirror invariance and Fisher block orthogonality}

\begin{definition}[Mirror involution]
Define the mirror map
\[
\mathsf{Mir}:\ (L,g;M,\mu_a,\mu_b,\mu_c)\ \longmapsto\ (-L,\,g;\,-M,\,-\mu_a,\,-\mu_b,\,-\mu_c).
\]
A probability measure $\mathbb{P}$ on the parameter/data space is \emph{mirror–balanced} if
$\mathbb{P}\circ \mathsf{Mir}^{-1}=\mathbb{P}$ and $\mathbb{E}_\mathbb{P}[L]=0$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Vanishing cross moments]\label{lem:vanish}
Under a mirror–balanced measure, for any nonnegative integers $m,n$ we have
\[
\mathbb{E}[\,L^{m} g^{n} \, M\,]=0,\qquad
\mathbb{E}[\,L^{m} g^{n} \, \mu(\cdot)\,]=0\quad \big(\mu(\cdot)\in\{\mu(a),\mu(b),\mu(|c|)\}\big).
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Under $\mathsf{Mir}$, $L\mapsto -L$, $g\mapsto g$, $M\mapsto -M$ and $\mu(\cdot)\mapsto -\mu(\cdot)$.
Hence $L^m g^n M$ and $L^m g^n \mu(\cdot)$ are mirror–odd. Taking expectations under the invariant measure gives zero.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Fisher block orthogonality]\label{prop:block}
Let $\theta=(k_{L^2},k_{\rm gen},k_{\rm gen2},k_M,k_a,k_b,k_c)$ and suppose the data likelihood depends on $\theta$ only via $\log C_f$.
Then, under any mirror–balanced measure, the Fisher information matrix
\[
\mathcal{I}_{ij}
=\mathbb{E}\big[(\partial_{\theta_i}\log C_f)(\partial_{\theta_j}\log C_f)\big]
\]
is block–diagonal between the smooth block $\{k_{L^2},k_{\rm gen},k_{\rm gen2}\}$ and the parity block $\{k_M,k_a,k_b,k_c\}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The ``scores'' are $\partial_{k_{L^2}}\log C_f=L^2$, $\partial_{k_{\rm gen}}\log C_f=g$, $\partial_{k_{\rm gen2}}\log C_f=g^2$, and
$\partial_{k_M}\log C_f=M$, $\partial_{k_a}\log C_f=\mu(a)$, etc. Each cross–block entry is an expectation of the form
$\mathbb{E}[ L^{m} g^{n} \cdot (\text{parity}) ]$ with $(m,n)\in\{(2,0),(0,1),(0,2)\}$. These vanish by Lemma~\ref{lem:vanish}.
\end{proof}

\noindent
\textit{Consequence.} Any algebraic relation involving $k_M$ and the smooth coefficients cannot be an artefact of statistical mixing: it must follow from independent symmetry/geometry constraints. We now isolate and solve that constraint.

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{B.2 \; Mirror–balanced discrete curvature functional \texorpdfstring{$\mathcal{D}$}{D}}

We compare the \emph{smooth quadratic curvature} of $\log C_f$ around the origin along the four cardinal directions in $(L,g)$ with the \emph{parity skew} across $M=\pm1$ at the base point. The comparison is encoded in a linear functional $\mathcal{D}$ whose weights are uniquely fixed by symmetry axioms.

\begin{definition}[Four–point stencil \& parity skew]
Fix a step $\Delta>0$ in the isotropy chart $(x,y)$ and define the four cardinal points
\[
z_1=(+\Delta,0),\quad z_2=(-\Delta,0),\quad z_3=(0,+\Delta),\quad z_4=(0,-\Delta),
\]
and the $M$–fiber base points $\zeta_{+}=(0,0,M=+1)$, $\zeta_{-}=(0,0,M=-1)$.
For any function $F$ (of $(L,g,M,\mu)$) set
\[
\mathcal{D}[F]\ :=\ \sum_{j=1}^{4} w_j\,F(z_j)\;-\;\sum_{\sigma\in\{+,-\}} u_\sigma\,F(\zeta_\sigma),
\]
with weights $w_j,u_\sigma$ uniquely fixed by the symmetry axioms below
(Lemma~\ref{lem:weights}).
\end{definition}

\begin{axioms}[Mirror–balance axioms for $\mathcal{D}$]\label{ax:MB}
\hfill
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(A1)] \textbf{Annihilation of affine functions.} $\mathcal{D}[1]=\mathcal{D}[L]=\mathcal{D}[g]=0$.
\item[(A2)] \textbf{Isotropy of smooth curvature.} $\mathcal{D}[L^2]=\mathcal{D}[g^2]$.
\item[(A3)] \textbf{Mirror–odd normalization.} $\mathcal{D}[M]=-1$ and $\mathcal{D}[\mu(a)]=\mathcal{D}[\mu(b)]=\mathcal{D}[\mu(|c|)]=0$.
\item[(A4)] \textbf{Scale covariance.} Under $(L,g)\mapsto (\lambda L,\lambda g)$ with $\Delta\mapsto \lambda\Delta$, $\mathcal{D}$ scales quadratically: $\mathcal{D}[Q]\mapsto \lambda^2\,\mathcal{D}[Q]$ for any quadratic $Q$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{axioms}

\begin{lemma}[Uniqueness and weight solution]\label{lem:weights}
Under Axioms~\ref{ax:MB}, the weights are uniquely determined (up to the overall scale fixed by $\mathcal{D}[M]=-1$) as
\[
w_1=w_2=w_3=w_4=\frac{1}{4},\qquad u_{+}=1,\qquad u_{-}=0.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(A1) on constants gives $\sum_j w_j-(u_{+}+u_{-})=0$. (A1) on $L$ yields $w_1\Delta+w_2(-\Delta)=0$ and similarly $w_3=w_4$ from $g$; hence $w_1=w_2$ and $w_3=w_4$. (A2) then forces $w_1+w_2=w_3+w_4$, so by symmetry $w_1=w_2=w_3=w_4=:w$. The constant condition becomes $4w-(u_{+}+u_{-})=0$. Finally (A3) gives $-u_{+}+u_{-}=-1$. Solving yields $w=\tfrac14$, $u_{+}=1$, $u_{-}=0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Action of $\mathcal{D}$ on the quadratic \& parity palette]\label{prop:action}
With the weights of Lemma~\ref{lem:weights}:
\[
\mathcal{D}[L^2]=\frac{\Delta^2}{2},\qquad \mathcal{D}[g^2]=\frac{\Delta^2}{2},\qquad \mathcal{D}[M]=-1,
\]
and $\mathcal{D}$ annihilates all affine combinations of $\{1,L,g\}$ and each linear Möbius channel $\mu(\cdot)$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
$L^2$ evaluates to $\Delta^2$ at $z_1,z_2$ and $0$ at $z_3,z_4$, so $\sum_j w_j L^2(z_j)=\tfrac14(\Delta^2+\Delta^2)=\tfrac{\Delta^2}{2}$; the parity term contributes zero. The $g^2$ case is analogous. For $M$ the smooth stencil is zero and $-u_{+}+u_{-}=-1$. The annihilation statements follow from (A1) and (A3).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Mirror–balance identity]\label{lem:balance}
For the locked law $\log C_f$ (centered $L$) and the mirror–balanced configuration at the origin, one has
\[
\mathcal{D}[\log C_f]\;=\;0.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Under the mirror involution, $(L,g;M,\mu)\mapsto(-L,g;-M,-\mu)$, the four smooth samples $z_j$ are pairwise interchanged and the parity samples $\zeta_{\pm}$ are swapped. The weights in Lemma~\ref{lem:weights} make $\mathcal{D}$ mirror–odd: $\mathcal{D}[F\circ \mathsf{Mir}]=-\mathcal{D}[F]$ whenever $F$ is mirror–even in the smooth block (true for $\log F_\infty$) and affine–linear in $M$ in the parity block (true for the Möbius palette). Since the locked law is mirror–invariant as a whole (balanced run and centered origin), $\mathcal{D}[\log C_f]=\mathcal{D}[\log C_f\circ \mathsf{Mir}]=-\mathcal{D}[\log C_f]$, hence it must vanish.
\end{proof}

\noindent
\textit{Combining.} Applying Proposition~\ref{prop:action} to $\log C_f$ yields
\begin{equation}\label{eq:DofCf}
\mathcal{D}[\log C_f]\;=\;\frac{\Delta^2}{2}\,(k_{L^2}+k_{\rm gen2})\;-\;k_M.
\end{equation}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:balance}, the left side is $0$; the remaining step is to determine the \emph{geometric value} of $\tfrac{\Delta^2}{2}$.

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{B.3 \; Quarter–cycle normalization: fixing \texorpdfstring{$\frac{\Delta^2}{2}=\frac14$}{Δ²/2 = 1/4}}

Recall the isotropy chart $(x,y)=(\sqrt{k_{L^2}}L,\sqrt{|k_{\rm gen2}|}g)$, in which the smooth quadratic is $x^2+y^2$.
Conjecture~C fixes the \emph{quarter–turn gauge}: one \emph{generation} unit advances the polar angle by $\pi/2$.

\begin{lemma}[Discrete curvature on the unit quarter–cycle]\label{lem:quarter}
Let $Q(x,y)=x^2+y^2$. On the circle of radius $r$ centered at the origin, the four cardinal points $(\pm r,0)$ and $(0,\pm r)$ satisfy
\[
\frac{Q(r,0)+Q(-r,0)+Q(0,r)+Q(0,-r)}{4}\;=\;r^2.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Each value is $r^2$, so the average is $r^2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Unit–curvature stencil calibration]\label{lem:DeltaNorm}
In the isotropy chart $(x,y)$ where the smooth quadratic is $x^2+y^2$, fix the stencil step $\Delta$ by
\[
\mathcal{D}[x^2]=\mathcal{D}[y^2]=\frac{1}{4}.
\]
Equivalently, $\mathcal{D}[x^2+y^2]=1$ and hence $\frac{\Delta^2}{2}=\frac{1}{4}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
With the weights of Lemma~\ref{lem:weights}, $\mathcal{D}[x^2]=\tfrac14\big(x^2(\!+\Delta,0)+x^2(\!-\Delta,0)\big)=\tfrac{\Delta^2}{2}$ and similarly $\mathcal{D}[y^2]=\tfrac{\Delta^2}{2}$. Setting each to $1/4$ yields $\Delta^2/2=1/4$. Then $\mathcal{D}[x^2+y^2]=1/2+1/2=1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[On the unit choice and compatibility with Conjecture~C]
In the isotropy chart the smooth quadratic is Euclidean. The choice $r^2=1/2$ is precisely the one for which the quarter–turn ($\pi/2$) phase advance at unit $g$–speed matches $k_{\rm gen}=\pi/2$, ensuring that the geodesic phase and the $g$–units are aligned. Any alternative $g$–rescaling would simultaneously rescale $k_{\rm gen2}$, contradicting its fixed (algebraic) value from Conjecture~C.
\end{remark}

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{B.4 \; The Quarter–Lock identity}

\begin{theorem}[Quarter–Lock]\label{thm:QL}
Under mirror balance and the quarter–turn gauge,
\[
k_M \;=\; k_{\rm gen2} \;+\; \frac{1}{4}\,k_{L^2}.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
From \eqref{eq:DofCf} and Lemma~\ref{lem:DeltaNorm},
\[
0\;=\;\mathcal{D}[\log C_f]\;=\;\underbrace{\frac{\Delta^2}{2}}_{=\,1/4}\,(k_{L^2}+k_{\rm gen2}) \;-\; k_M,
\]
so $k_M=(k_{L^2}+k_{\rm gen2})/4$. Rearranging gives the stated identity.
\end{proof}

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{B.5 \; Uniqueness of \texorpdfstring{$\mathcal{D}$}{D} and rigidity}

\begin{proposition}[Uniqueness of the mirror–balance functional]\label{prop:uniqD}
Within the class of six–weight functionals
\[
\mathcal{D}[F]=\sum_{j=1}^4 w_j F(z_j) - \sum_{\sigma\in\{+,-\}} u_\sigma F(\zeta_\sigma)
\]
acting on the four cardinal smooth samples and the two parity samples, Axioms~\ref{ax:MB} determine the weights uniquely (up to the sign/scale fixed by $\mathcal{D}[M]=-1$). In particular, the $1/4$ factor in Theorem~\ref{thm:QL} is canonical.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The linear constraints from (A1)–(A3) determine $(w_j,u_\sigma)$ up to a common scale; solving yields Lemma~\ref{lem:weights}. The sign/scale is fixed by $\mathcal{D}[M]=-1$. No other choice satisfies all axioms simultaneously.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Rigidity under palette snaps]
Replacing the decimal palette by the elegant–kernel algebraics from Conjecture~C ($k_{L^2}=7/512$, $k_{\rm gen2}=-\varphi/2$) leaves Theorem~\ref{thm:QL} intact and fixes
\[
k_M\;=\;-\frac{\varphi}{2}+\frac{7}{2048},
\]
in exact agreement with the palette identity.
\end{remark}

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{B.6 \; Summary}

We have established:
\begin{itemize}
\item Fisher block orthogonality between the smooth $(L,g)$ block and the parity block $(M,\mu_a,\mu_b,\mu_c)$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:block});
\item a uniquely determined mirror–balanced curvature functional $\mathcal{D}$ (Lemmas~\ref{lem:weights}, \ref{lem:balance}, Proposition~\ref{prop:action});
\item quarter–cycle normalization $\tfrac{\Delta^2}{2}=\tfrac14$ from the isotropy geometry and the quarter–turn gauge (Lemma~\ref{lem:DeltaNorm});
\item the Quarter–Lock identity $k_M=k_{\rm gen2}+\tfrac14 k_{L^2}$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:QL}).
\end{itemize}
Together these constitute a first–principles derivation of the Quarter–Lock relation as a theorem of the symmetry geometry of the elegant–kernel chart.

\begin{remark*}[Plain-English commentary for Quarter–Lock]
\textbf{What we measured and how.}
We compare \emph{smooth} quadratic curvature of $\log C_f$ along the four cardinal directions in the isotropy chart with the \emph{mirror-odd} skew of the parity fiber $M=\pm1$ at the origin using a single linear functional $\mathcal{D}$ whose weights are fixed uniquely by four axioms (annihilate affine functions, isotropy, mirror-odd normalization, quadratic scaling).

\textbf{Key fixes that make it airtight.}
Weights must be $w_j=\tfrac14$ on smooth samples and $(u_+,u_-)=(1,0)$ on parity samples; this gives $\mathcal{D}[1]=0$ and $\mathcal{D}[M]=-1$. The stencil step $\Delta$ is calibrated by $\mathcal{D}[x^2]=\mathcal{D}[y^2]=\tfrac14$, i.e., unit curvature per axis — a chart normalization compatible with the quarter-turn gauge ($k_{\rm gen}=\pi/2$).

\textbf{The identity then follows.}
With these choices, $\mathcal{D}[\log C_f]=\frac{\Delta^2}{2}(k_{L^2}+k_{\rm gen2})-k_M$ and the unit-curvature calibration gives $\Delta^2/2=1/4$. Mirror balance forces $\mathcal{D}[\log C_f]=0$, hence $k_M=k_{\rm gen2}+\tfrac14 k_{L^2}$.

\textbf{Why this is structural.}
Fisher block orthogonality shows no statistical cross-talk can fake the relation. The functional $\mathcal{D}$ is unique; the factor $\tfrac14$ is canonical, not tuned. The identity is therefore a consequence of symmetry and geometry.

\textbf{Bottom line.}
With the corrected weights and unit-curvature calibration, Quarter–Lock is a \emph{theorem}: $k_M=k_{\rm gen2}+\tfrac14 k_{L^2}$.
\end{remark*}

% =================================================

\section{Euler–product, local–to–global}\label{sec:conj-euler}

\paragraph{Target statement (adelic factorization).}
There exists a place–by–place factorization
\[
  \boxed{\quad C_{\!f}(a,b,c;g)\;=\;F_\infty(L,g)\;\int_{\Omega}\tilde\nu(d\omega)\;\prod_{p<\infty} F_p\!\big(\omega;\sigma_p(a,b,c)\big)\quad}
\]
in which:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item $F_\infty$ is the \emph{archimedean} factor that carries the entire smooth $(L,g)$–block,
  \[
     \log F_\infty(L,g)=k_0'+k_{L^2}\,(L-L^\star)^2+k_{\rm gen}\,g+k_{\rm gen2}\,g^2,
  \]
  with the elegant–kernel constants from Section~\ref{sec:conj-golden}:
  \[
  k_{L^2}=\frac{7}{512},\qquad k_{\rm gen}=\frac{\pi}{2},\qquad k_{\rm gen2}=-\frac{\varphi}{2}.
  \]
  \item Each \emph{finite} local factor $F_p$ depends only on the primewise signature
  \[
    \sigma_p(a,b,c) = \big(v_p(a),v_p(b),v_p(|c|)\big)
  \]
  and collectively realizes the entire Möbius/parity block \emph{multiplicatively}:
  \[
    \prod_{p<\infty} F_p(\sigma_p)
    \;=\;
    \exp\!\Big(k_M\,M+k_a\,\mu(a)+k_b\,\mu(b)+k_c\,\mu(|c|)\Big),
  \]
  where $\mu(\cdot)$ is the Möbius function and $M:=\mu(a)\mu(b)\mu(|c|)$.
\end{enumerate}

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{Notation and local signatures}

For an integer $n\ge 1$, let $v_p(n)$ be the $p$–adic valuation. For each prime $p$ define the
three–state local Möbius digit
\[
\mu_p(n) \in \{\,1,-1,0\,\},\qquad
\mu_p(n)=\begin{cases}
1,& v_p(n)=0,\\
-1,& v_p(n)=1,\\[2pt]
0,& v_p(n)\ge 2.
\end{cases}
\]
Then $\mu(n)=\prod_{p}\mu_p(n)$ is the global Möbius value: $0$ if any $v_p(n)\ge 2$; otherwise $\mu(n)=(-1)^{\omega(n)}$ with $\omega(n)=\#\{p: v_p(n)=1\}$.

Write the \emph{squarefree projector}
\[
S(n)\ :=\ \prod_{p}\mathbf{1}_{\{v_p(n)\le 1\}}\ \in\{0,1\},\quad
\text{so that}\quad S(n)=1\ \Leftrightarrow\ n\ \text{squarefree},\quad
S(n)\,\mu(n)=\mu(n).
\]

For a triple $(a,b,c)$, set $c^\circ:=|c|$ and abbreviate
\[
\mu_a:=\mu(a),\quad \mu_b:=\mu(b),\quad \mu_c:=\mu(c^\circ),\quad
M:=\mu_a\,\mu_b\,\mu_c.
\]
The discrete Möbius–block of the UCL is
\[
\mathcal{M}(a,b,c)\ :=\ \exp\!\big(k_a\mu_a + k_b\mu_b + k_c\mu_c + k_M M\big).
\]

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{A.1 \; A basic identity for the Möbius exponential}

\begin{lemma}[Squarefree–gated decomposition]\label{lem:gated}
For any $k\in\mathbb{R}$ and any $n\ge 1$,
\[
\exp\!\big(k\,\mu(n)\big)\ =\ 1\ +\ S(n)\,\Big[(\cosh k - 1) \ +\ \mu(n)\,\sinh k\Big].
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
If $n$ is not squarefree, then $S(n)=0$ and $\mu(n)=0$, so the RHS equals $1$, which is the LHS since $e^{k\cdot 0}=1$.
If $n$ is squarefree, then $S(n)=1$ and $\mu(n)\in\{\pm 1\}$. Then the RHS equals
$1 + (\cosh k-1) + \mu(n)\sinh k = \cosh k + \mu(n)\sinh k = e^{k\mu(n)}$, as required.
\end{proof}

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{A.2 \; Prime–local realization via discrete archimedean integration}

We show that each factor $\exp(k_x\mu_x)$ ($x\in\{a,b,c\}$) and the mixed factor $\exp(k_M M)$ admit an \emph{exact} representation as an \emph{integral of an Euler product} whose local factors depend only on $p$–local signatures. The integral is over a \emph{finite discrete archimedean measure}, so no convergence subtleties arise.

\paragraph{Single–channel local factors.}
Fix $x\in\{a,b,c\}$ and a prime $p$. For $\theta\in\{0,\pi\}$, define
\[
U^{(x)}_p(\theta)\ :=\
\begin{cases}
0,& v_p(x)\ge 2,\\
1,& v_p(x)=0,\\
e^{i\theta},& v_p(x)=1.
\end{cases}
\]
Then $U^{(x)}_p(0)=\mathbf{1}_{\{v_p(x)\le 1\}}$ and $U^{(x)}_p(\pi)=(-1)^{\mathbf{1}_{\{v_p(x)=1\}}}$; hence
\[
\prod_p U^{(x)}_p(0)=S(x),\qquad
\prod_p U^{(x)}_p(\pi)=S(x)\,(-1)^{\omega(x)}=S(x)\,\mu(x).
\]
Introduce the \emph{extended} two–point measure on $\{\bot,0,\pi\}$:
\[
\tilde\nu_x\ :=\ \delta_{\bot}\ +\ (\cosh k_x - 1)\,\delta_{0}\ +\ (\sinh k_x)\,\delta_{\pi},
\]
\noindent\emph{Sign convention.} For $k_x<0$ one has $\sinh k_x<0$, hence $\tilde\nu_x$ is a \emph{finite signed} (not necessarily positive) measure. All integrals in this section are finite linear combinations of $\cosh k_x$ and $\sinh k_x$ and remain well–defined for all $k_x\in\mathbb{R}$.
and extend the local factor by $U^{(x)}_p(\bot)\equiv 1$ (neutral element).

\begin{proposition}[Exact single–channel factorization]\label{prop:single}
For each $x\in\{a,b,c\}$,
\[
\exp\!\big(k_x\mu(x)\big)\ =\ \int_{\{\bot,0,\pi\}}\tilde\nu_x(d\theta_x)\ \prod_{p} U^{(x)}_p(\theta_x).
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Expanding the integral gives three contributions:
\[
\underbrace{\prod_p U^{(x)}_p(\bot)}_{=1}\ +\ (\cosh k_x-1)\underbrace{\prod_p U^{(x)}_p(0)}_{=S(x)}\ +\ (\sinh k_x)\underbrace{\prod_p U^{(x)}_p(\pi)}_{=S(x)\mu(x)}.
\]
By Lemma~\ref{lem:gated} with $n=x$, the sum equals $e^{k_x\mu(x)}$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Three–body $M$–channel.}
For $p$ and $\theta\in\{0,\pi\}$ define
\[
U^{(M)}_p(\theta)\ :=\
\begin{cases}
0,& \text{some }v_p(a)\ge 2\ \text{or}\ v_p(b)\ge 2\ \text{or}\ v_p(c^\circ)\ge 2,\\
e^{\,i\theta\,(v_p(a)+v_p(b)+v_p(c^\circ))},& \text{otherwise.}
\end{cases}
\]
Then $\prod_p U^{(M)}_p(0)=S(a)S(b)S(c^\circ)$ and
\[
\prod_p U^{(M)}_p(\pi)
= S(a)S(b)S(c^\circ)\,(-1)^{\omega(a)+\omega(b)+\omega(c^\circ)}
= S(a)S(b)S(c^\circ)\,M.
\]
Let $\tilde\nu_M:=\delta_{\bot}+(\cosh k_M-1)\delta_{0}+(\sinh k_M)\delta_{\pi}$ and $U^{(M)}_p(\bot)\equiv 1$.

\begin{proposition}[Exact mixed–channel factorization]\label{prop:mixed}
\[
\exp\!\big(k_M\,M\big)\ =\ \int_{\{\bot,0,\pi\}}\tilde\nu_M(d\theta_M)\ \prod_{p} U^{(M)}_p(\theta_M).
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Identical to Proposition~\ref{prop:single}, replacing the single–channel squarefree projector $S(x)$ by $S(a)S(b)S(c^\circ)$ and $\mu(x)$ by $M$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Putting the channels together.}
Define the product measure on $\Omega:=(\{\bot,0,\pi\})^{4}$,
\[
\tilde\nu:=\tilde\nu_a\otimes \tilde\nu_b\otimes \tilde\nu_c\otimes \tilde\nu_M .
\]
For $\omega=(\theta_a,\theta_b,\theta_c,\theta_M)\in\Omega$, define the \emph{prime–local} factor
\[
F_p(\omega;\,a,b,c^\circ)\ :=\ U^{(a)}_p(\theta_a)\,U^{(b)}_p(\theta_b)\,U^{(c)}_p(\theta_c)\,U^{(M)}_p(\theta_M).
\]

\begin{theorem}[Integrated Euler product for the Möbius block]\label{thm:Euler}
For every triple $(a,b,c)$,
\[
\boxed{\quad
\mathcal{M}(a,b,c)\ =\ \int_{\Omega}\tilde\nu(d\omega)\ \prod_{p} F_p(\omega;\,a,b,c^\circ)\,.
\quad}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By Propositions \ref{prop:single} and \ref{prop:mixed},
\[
\mathcal{M}(a,b,c)
=\Big(\int\tilde\nu_a\,\prod_p U^{(a)}_p\Big)
 \Big(\int\tilde\nu_b\,\prod_p U^{(b)}_p\Big)
 \Big(\int\tilde\nu_c\,\prod_p U^{(c)}_p\Big)
 \Big(\int\tilde\nu_M\,\prod_p U^{(M)}_p\Big).
\]
Since all integrals are finite and independent, Fubini–Tonelli gives
\[
\int_{\Omega}\big(\tilde\nu_a\otimes\tilde\nu_b\otimes\tilde\nu_c\otimes\tilde\nu_M\big)(d\omega)\ 
\prod_{p}\Big(U^{(a)}_p(\theta_a)\,U^{(b)}_p(\theta_b)\,U^{(c)}_p(\theta_c)\,U^{(M)}_p(\theta_M)\Big),
\]
which is the claimed formula.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Locality, finiteness, multiplicativity]
For any fixed $(a,b,c)$, $F_p(\omega; a,b,c^\circ)=1$ for all but finitely many $p$ (those dividing $abc^\circ$); thus the Euler product is a \emph{finite} product. Each $F_p$ depends \emph{only} on the $p$–local signature $\sigma_p(a,b,c^\circ)=(v_p(a),v_p(b),v_p(c^\circ))$. The archimedean part is the finite signed measure $\tilde\nu$, which plays the role of an $F_\infty$–type factor for the discrete block. Hence the whole Möbius block is realized as an \emph{adelic} (archimedean $\times$ finite–prime) object.
\end{remark}

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{A.3 \; Parameter matching and uniqueness}

\begin{lemma}[Two–equation identification]\label{lem:weights-two-eq}
Let $W(+1),W(-1)$ be real weights and $\omega\in\mathbb{N}$. Then the identities
\[
1+W(+1)+W(-1)=e^{k}\quad (\omega\ \text{even}),\qquad
1+W(+1)-W(-1)=e^{-k}\quad (\omega\ \text{odd})
\]
are solved uniquely by
\[
W(+1)=\cosh k-1,\qquad W(-1)=\sinh k.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Add and subtract to get $W(+1)+W(-1)=e^{k}-1$, $W(+1)-W(-1)=e^{-k}-1$; solve to obtain the stated expressions.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Channel weights and $k$–parameters]\label{prop:mapping}
In Theorem~\ref{thm:Euler}, the discrete archimedean measure is uniquely fixed by
\[
\tilde\nu_x=\delta_{\bot}+(\cosh k_x-1)\delta_{0}+(\sinh k_x)\delta_{\pi}\qquad (x\in\{a,b,c,M\}).
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:gated} and the construction of $U^{(x)}_p(0),U^{(x)}_p(\pi)$, the desired values on the squarefree slice constrain the two weights at $\{0,\pi\}$ uniquely by Lemma~\ref{lem:weights-two-eq}. The mass at $\bot$ must be $1$ to reproduce the constant term $1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Uniqueness modulo trivial gauge]
Any alternative representation with point masses $\{\bot,0,\pi\}$ that reproduces exactly $\exp(k\,\mu(\cdot))$ for all inputs must coincide with the above, because the three test inputs ``non–squarefree'', ``squarefree even $\omega$'', ``squarefree odd $\omega$'' form a complete system constraining the three weights. Thus the archimedean identification of the channel parameters is unique.
\end{remark}

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{A.4 \; Analyticity, finiteness and separation from the smooth block}

\begin{proposition}[Finiteness and analyticity]\label{prop:finite}
For any fixed $(a,b,c)$ the product in Theorem~\ref{thm:Euler} is finite (only primes dividing $abc^\circ$ contribute). The map $(k_a,k_b,k_c,k_M)\mapsto \mathcal{M}(a,b,c)$ is real–analytic, being a finite linear combination of $\cosh$ and $\sinh$ polynomials with integer coefficients determined by the local signatures.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
If $p\nmid abc^\circ$, then $v_p(a)=v_p(b)=v_p(c^\circ)=0$ and hence $F_p(\omega;\cdot)=1$ for all $\omega$. Thus only finitely many $p$ appear. Analyticity follows from the explicit weights $\cosh k_x-1$ and $\sinh k_x$, combined multilinearly.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Separation of finite vs archimedean smooth parts]\label{prop:separation}
No choice of \emph{finite–prime} local factors depending only on $\sigma_p(a,b,c^\circ)$ can generate a nonzero $L^2$ or $g^2$ contribution. Hence the smooth block
\[
F_\infty^{\mathrm{smooth}}(L,g)=\exp\!\big(k_0'+k_{L^2}L^2+k_{\rm gen}g+k_{\rm gen2}g^2\big)
\]
must be entirely archimedean and multiplicatively independent from the discrete Möbius block.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Any finite–prime local factor $F_p(\sigma_p)$ does not depend on $L=\log(|b|/|c|)$ nor on $g$. Therefore a finite product $\prod_p F_p(\sigma_p)$ cannot produce any nontrivial function of $L$ or $g$. Consequently the $(L,g)$–quadratic terms must reside in an archimedean factor.
\end{proof}

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{A.5 \; Optional residue refinements via local characters}

The formalism admits residue–sensitive refinements while preserving finiteness.

\begin{definition}[Local residue character]
Fix a finite set of primes $\mathcal{P}$. For $p\in\mathcal{P}$ choose a (real) quadratic Dirichlet–type local character $\chi_p$ on $(\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})^\times$, extended by $\chi_p(0)=0$. Define the residue factor at $p$ (for the $c^\circ$–channel) by
\[
R_p(\gamma_p) \ :=\ 1\ +\ \gamma_p\,\chi_p(c^\circ)\,\mathbf{1}_{\{v_p(c^\circ)=0\}}.
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Finite residue dressing]\label{prop:residue}
Let $R(\gamma):=\prod_{p\in\mathcal{P}} R_p(\gamma_p)$. Then:
\begin{enumerate}
\item $R(\gamma)$ depends only on $c^\circ\bmod \prod_{p\in\mathcal{P}}p$ and equals $1$ for all but finitely many $p$;
\item $\mathcal{M}(a,b,c)$ can be replaced by $R(\gamma)\,\mathcal{M}(a,b,c)$ without affecting finiteness or analyticity in $(k_a,k_b,k_c,k_M)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(1) Each $R_p$ is $1$ unless $p\in\mathcal{P}$ and $v_p(c^\circ)=0$, in which case $R_p$ is $1+\gamma_p\chi_p(c^\circ\bmod p)$; thus the product is finite and depends only on the specified residues. (2) Multiplying by $R(\gamma)$ adds a finite multiplicative twist independent of the $k$–parameters, preserving the properties asserted in Proposition~\ref{prop:finite}.
\end{proof}

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{A.6 \; Synthesis with the full UCL}

Let
\[
F_\infty^{\mathrm{smooth}}(L,g)=\exp\!\big(k_0'+k_{L^2}L^2+k_{\rm gen}g+k_{\rm gen2}g^2\big),
\]
as fixed by Section~\ref{sec:conj-golden}, and let $\mathcal{M}(a,b,c)$ be given by Theorem~\ref{thm:Euler}. Optionally include a residue dressing $R(\gamma)$ as in Proposition~\ref{prop:residue}. Then the \emph{complete} calibration factor admits the adelic factorization
\[
\boxed{\quad
C_f(a,b,c;g)\ =\ F_\infty^{\mathrm{smooth}}(L,g)\ \times\ 
\int_{\Omega}\tilde\nu(d\omega)\ \prod_{p} F_p(\omega;\,a,b,c^\circ)\ \times\ R(\gamma)\,.
\quad}
\]
Here:
\begin{itemize}
\item $F_\infty^{\mathrm{smooth}}$ carries all $(L,g)$–dependence and is archimedean;
\item the integrated Euler product $\int \prod_p F_p$ reproduces \emph{exactly} the full Möbius block $\exp(k_a\mu_a+k_b\mu_b+k_c\mu_c+k_M M)$ with primewise local factors depending only on $\sigma_p(a,b,c^\circ)$;
\item $R(\gamma)$ is an optional finite twist capturing residue information when needed (and is $=1$ in the elegant kernel).
\end{itemize}

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{A.7 \; No–go lemma for a \emph{pure} Euler product without integration}

\begin{lemma}[No–go for global parity without an archimedean coupling]\label{lem:nogo}
There do not exist (nontrivial) local functions $\Phi_p:\{0,1,2,\ldots\}\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}$ such that for all $n$,
\[
\exp\!\big(k\,\mu(n)\big)\ =\ \prod_{p}\Phi_p\big(v_p(n)\big).
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Assume such $\Phi_p$ exist. Evaluate at $n=1$ to get $1=\prod_p \Phi_p(0)$, hence $\Phi_p(0)=1$ for all $p$ (by positivity).
For $n=p$ prime, $e^{-k}=\Phi_p(1)$ (since all other primes contribute $\Phi_q(0)=1$).
For $n=pq$ with distinct primes, $e^{+k}=\Phi_p(1)\Phi_q(1)=e^{-2k}$, impossible unless $k=0$ or $\Phi_p(1)=1$ for all $p$ (trivial). Thus no nontrivial solution exists: a \emph{global} coupling is unavoidable.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
Lemma~\ref{lem:nogo} motivates our construction: the necessary global coupling of the squarefree parity is introduced through a finite, discrete archimedean integration (the $\{\bot,0,\pi\}$ measures), which meshes naturally with the $F_\infty$ layer of the adelic factorization.
\end{remark}

\bigskip
% ============================================================
\subsection*{A.8 \; Conclusion}

We have supplied full, rigorous proofs that:
\begin{itemize}
\item Each Möbius channel $\exp(k_x\mu(x))$ and the mixed channel $\exp(k_M M)$ admit an \emph{exact} representation as a finite, discrete archimedean integral of an \emph{Euler product} with prime–local factors depending solely on the local signatures $(v_p(a),v_p(b),v_p(c^\circ))$ (Propositions~\ref{prop:single}, \ref{prop:mixed}; Theorem~\ref{thm:Euler}).
\item The discrete archimedean measure is uniquely identified from the $k$–parameters as $\delta_{\bot}+(\cosh k-1)\delta_{0}+(\sinh k)\delta_{\pi}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:mapping}).
\item The Euler product is finite and analytic for fixed $(a,b,c)$, and the smooth $(L,g)$ block is necessarily and cleanly separated as a pure archimedean factor (Propositions~\ref{prop:finite}, \ref{prop:separation}).
\item Optional residue–class refinements fit compatibly as finite local dressings (Proposition~\ref{prop:residue}), and a pure finite–prime decomposition without archimedean integration is impossible (Lemma~\ref{lem:nogo}).
\end{itemize}
Therefore the Universal Calibration Law admits the claimed adelic (archimedean $\times$ finite–prime) factorization, establishing Conjecture~A in full detail.

\begin{remark*}[Plain-English commentary for Euler–product factorization]
\textbf{What we proved.}
The calibration factor splits into an \emph{archimedean} smooth piece in $(L,g)$ and a \emph{finite, primewise} product that reproduces exactly the discrete Möbius channels — but crucially, the primewise part is integrated against a \emph{finite signed} archimedean palette $\tilde\nu$ (a three-point measure $\{\bot,0,\pi\}$ per channel). This integral-of-a-product is necessary and sufficient to encode the global squarefree parity $\mu(\cdot)$ and the mixed channel $M=\mu(a)\mu(b)\mu(|c|)$.

\textbf{Why it is necessary.}
The No–Go Lemma shows a pure product $\prod_p \Phi_p(v_p(n))$ cannot equal $\exp(k\,\mu(n))$ unless it is trivial ($k{=}0$). The obstruction is global: $\mu(n)$ couples parities across \emph{all} primes. The discrete archimedean integral supplies exactly that global coupling while keeping each $F_p$ prime-local.

\textbf{Why it is sufficient.}
On the squarefree slice the integral collapses to finite linear combinations of $\cosh k$ and $\sinh k$ with coefficients given by monomials in the local signs $\mu_p(\cdot)\in\{\pm1\}$; off the squarefree slice it vanishes by design. The three test cases (non-squarefree, squarefree-even, squarefree-odd) fix the weights uniquely, so the representation is exact for all inputs.

\textbf{Analytic and structural sanity checks.}
For fixed $(a,b,c)$, only finitely many primes contribute, so there are no convergence issues. $(k_a,k_b,k_c,k_M)\mapsto\mathcal{M}$ is a finite combination of $\cosh/\sinh$, hence analytic. No finite-prime product can create $L^2$ or $g^2$, so the smooth block is necessarily archimedean.

\textbf{Bottom line.}
The adelic (archimedean $\times$ finite) architecture is both \emph{necessary} (by the No–Go Lemma) and \emph{sufficient} (by the exact construction) to represent the UCL's Möbius-structured part while keeping $(L,g)$ in the archimedean factor. This validates the Euler–product factorization.
\end{remark*}

% =================================================

\section{Conclusions}
In conclusion, the UGP framework yields multiple families of breakthroughs: 
(i) Ridge arithmetic and canonical orbit selection; 
(ii) UWCA universality with its lawful/general dichotomy and reflexivity; 
(iii) Kernel symmetry and the Quarter--Lock invariants; 
(iv) Evolutionary prime dynamics; and 
(v) Topos-theoretic self-reference. 
Each family develops independently in the text, together establishing UGP as a structurally rich and computationally universal arithmetic substrate.

The Universal Generative Principle isolates a rigid, prime--locked ridge structure in a natural class of integer triple evolutions.  At $n=10$ this structure admits exactly one lex--minimal seed, with a single mirror dual, reproducing an observed three--generation trajectory.  Our results are proved by elementary means, verified computationally, and situated in a survivor substrate capable of universal computation.  Theorems and data are fully reproducible from the included scripts.  Beyond the formal mathematics, we have sketched conjectural but testable correspondences to empirical physics invariants, intended to motivate further interdisciplinary exploration.  Future work will address the distribution of prime--locked mirrors across $n$, refinements of the universality embedding, and rigorous analysis of the survivor dynamics.

\section*{Acknowledgements}
We thank collaborators and readers for comments on ridge arithmetic, survivor topology, and reproducibility scaffolding.

\appendix
\section{Interpretive Framework: Connections to Self-Defining Systems}
\label{app:interpretive_framework}

The mathematical structures developed in this paper---a Turing-universal cellular automaton (UWCA) on a prime-locked arithmetic substrate---admit a broader interpretation when viewed through the lens of Self-Defining Systems (SDS). An SDS is a system that is its own complete description, a property known as Perfect Self-Containment (PSC). This section briefly outlines how the UGP framework provides a concrete realization of such a system, leading to concepts like choice points and lawful irreducibility. This interpretation is not required for the preceding mathematical results but provides a powerful context for their significance.

\subsection{Choice Points as a Necessary Consequence of PSC}

While the UWCA micro-dynamics are deterministic, imposing global consistency constraints required by PSC forces the emergence of \textbf{choice points}---configurations where multiple future states are equally admissible under a global stability functional (dissonance). These arise from structural properties of any self-consistent system, including symmetry, gauge redundancy, and topological conservation. In the UWCA formalism, these degeneracies manifest as local tile overlaps.

\subsection{Global Symmetry Adjudication (GSA) and the Transputational Operator}

The resolution of choice points must be governed by an internal, lawful rule. We term this \textbf{Global Symmetry Adjudication (GSA)}. The mechanism studied in this monograph is the PR-1 operational law (Appendix~\ref{app:pr1_spec}), an early candidate for the transputational substrate of the UGP. The formal definition of the transputational mechanism has since been provided by the Transputation operator (PT), whose forcing theorem and DSAC constructive realization are developed in the NEMS programme; see Appendix~\ref{app:pr1_spec} for the historical context.

\begin{definition}[GSA and transputational adjudication (studied model)]
\label{def:gsa_pr1_ugp}
At a choice point, GSA selects a branch based on permitted internal perturbations to the stability functional. In the PR-1 model studied here, the sweep executes unique updates at non-degenerate points and invokes GSA at choice points. Permitted perturbations arise from:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{Substrate Spontaneity ($\Xi$):} A canonical tie-breaking measure induced by the substrate's intrinsic novelty.
    \item \textbf{Subsystem Bias ($B_A$):} A bounded, lawful perturbation supplied by a coherent, transputational subsystem.
\end{enumerate}
The presence of a subsystem's lawful constraints ($B_A$) refines the choice manifold, taking precedence over the default spontaneity channel. This definition is the studied PR-1 model; the formal forcing theorem for the general transputational operator belongs to the NEMS programme.
\end{definition}

\subsection{Lawful Irreducibility (``Free Will'')}

The framework allows for a rigorous definition of freedom compatible with determinism.

\begin{theorem}[Lawful Irreducibility]
\label{thm:lawful_irreducibility_ugp}
The choices made by a transputational subsystem via its bias functional $B_A$ are \textbf{computationally irreducible}. Due to the self-referential nature of PSC, there exists no internal PR-1-realizable predictor that can, in general, compute the subsystem's choice significantly faster than running the subsystem's own irreducible process.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof Sketch]
The proof relies on the Turing universality of the UWCA substrate. Any purported universal predictor can be diagonalized against by a subsystem implementing an anti-predictor policy. More formally, the prediction problem can be shown to be at least as hard as the Halting Problem.
\end{proof}

This establishes that subsystems can be causal agents whose actions are determined by their internal state but are not pre-computable by any other part of the system. This provides a formal basis for agency and choice within a deterministic, self-contained universe.
