\section{Tile family catalogue for \texorpdfstring{$\Sigma_{\mathrm{GTE}}$}{Σ\_GTE} and macro–step schedule}\label{app:tiles}
% =================================================

\subsection*{B.1  Geometry and lanes}
We use a radius‑1 one–dimensional UWCA on a ring indexed by a finite prime window
\[
P=\{p_1<p_2<\cdots<p_t\},\qquad M:=\prod_{i=1}^t p_i,
\]
with \(M\) chosen \emph{strictly larger} than all register maxima in the horizon (for \(n{=}10\), \(M>65535\) suffices). Each site \(i\) carries, in clopen cylinders, the residues of the following registers in \(\mathbb{F}_{p_i}\):
\[
\mathbf{Reg}=\{a,b,c,q,m,\ \hat q,\ \kappa,\ F\}\quad\text{and a phase bit }\phi\in\{\textsf{odd},\textsf{even}\}.
\]
A fixed ``control lane'' orchestrates sweep order. CRT (Garner) carries move along the \(p_i\) direction (nearest‑neighbor).

\subsection*{B.2  Tile families (local rewrite schemes)}
Each family below is realized by finitely many tiles (one per local residue pattern). ``Nbrhd'' records the locality (always radius 1); ``Guard'' is the clopen precondition; ``Effect'' is the per‑site update in \(\mathbb{F}_{p_i}\) (cross‑prime carries handled by the Garner sweep); ``Cost'' counts sweeps per macro call.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.13}
\small\begin{tabular}{@{}lp{2.2cm}p{2.4cm}p{4.6cm}l@{}}
\toprule
Family & Nbrhd & Guard & Effect (per site \(i\)) & Cost \\
\midrule
\(\mathsf{COPY}(X\to Y)\)   & 1 & control step & \(Y_i \gets X_i\) & 1 \\
\(\mathsf{SWAP}(X,Y)\)      & 1 & control step & \(X_i \leftrightarrow Y_i\) & 1 \\
\(\mathsf{ADDc}(X{+}{=}k)\) & 1 & control step & \(X_i \gets X_i + k \bmod p_i\) & 1 \\
\(\mathsf{SUBc}(X{-}{=}k)\) & 1 & control step & \(X_i \gets X_i - k \bmod p_i\) & 1 \\
\(\mathsf{ADD}(X{+}{=}Y)\)  & 1 & control step & \(X_i \gets X_i + Y_i\) & 1 \\
\(\mathsf{SUB}(X{-}{=}Y)\)  & 1 & control step & \(X_i \gets X_i - Y_i\) & 1 \\
\(\mathsf{MULc}(X{\cdot}{=}k)\) & 1 & control step & \(k\,X_i\) & 1 \\
\(\mathsf{MUL}(Z{:=}X\!\times\!Y)\) & 1 & control step & schoolbook partials, Garner carries & \(O(t)\) \\
\(\mathsf{ABS\_DIFF}(Z{|}{:=}|X{-}Y|)\) & 1 & control+cmp flag & sitewise update + global sign carry & \(O(t)\) \\
\(\mathsf{CMP}(X,Y\to \text{flag})\) & 1 & control step & lexicographic compare via Garner & \(O(t)\) \\
\(\mathsf{DIVREM}(c,b\to q,m)\) & 1 & control step & long–division (shift–sub) in CRT & \(O(t)\) \\
\(\mathsf{GARNER\_FWD/BWD}\) & 1 & carry lane on & propagate carries across \(p_i\) & \(O(t)\) \\
\(\mathsf{LATCH}(b,q)\)     & 1 & control step & write \(b^\ell\!\gets b,\ q^\ell\!\gets q\) & 1 \\
\(\mathsf{UNLATCH}\)        & 1 & control step & clear latched marks & 1 \\
\(\mathsf{PHASE\_FLIP}\)    & 1 & always & \(\phi:\textsf{odd}\leftrightarrow\textsf{even}\) & 1 \\
\(\mathsf{FIB\_FD}(\kappa\to F)\) & 1 & control step & fast‑doubling recurrences & \(O(\log\kappa)\) \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}%
\caption{Tile families comprising \(\Sigma_{\mathrm{GTE}}\). Garner sweeps ensure integer‑correct results from residue lanes.}
\label{tab:sigma-gte}
\end{table}

\paragraph{Legality.} Each tile rewrites clopen cylinder sets to clopen cylinder sets; Garner recombination/reduction maps legal cylinders to legal cylinders. Thus survivor constraints are preserved by construction (cf.\ the sheaf model in §\ref{subsec:B3-classifying}).

\subsection*{B.3  Macro–step schedule (odd/even phases)}
Let \(n\) be fixed. One \(T\)–step is compiled as the following sweep script (comments reference Def.~\ref{def:ugp_update_map}).

\begin{lstlisting}[basicstyle=\ttfamily\small]
# --- common prologue ---
LATCH(b,q)                 # keep pre-update (b,q) for writing c := b*q + 15

# --- q,m from (b,c) ---
DIVREM(c,b $\rightarrow$ q,m)          # Definition \ref{def:ugp_update_map}, both phases

# --- phase-specific branch ---
if $\phi$ == odd:
    a $\leftarrow$ m - (12 - n)
    b $\leftarrow$ b - (m + q)
    c $\leftarrow$ b$^\ell$ * q$^\ell$ + 15     # uses latched pre-update values
else: # $\phi$ == even
    a $\leftarrow$ m - (12 - n)
    $\kappa$ $\leftarrow$ |q - q$^\ell$|
    F $\leftarrow$ Fib_FD($\kappa$)          # fast-doubling (Appendix)
    b $\leftarrow$ b + F
    c $\leftarrow$ b$^\ell$ * q$^\ell$ + 15

PHASE_FLIP()
UNLATCH()
\end{lstlisting}

\paragraph{Specialization at \(n=10\).} Under UGP‑1, \(m_2{=}15\) and \(|q_2{-}q_1|{=}13\), so the even–step reduces to \(b\leftarrow b+233\) (constant increment).

\subsection*{B.4  Correctness and cost (detail for Thm.~\ref{thm:gte-as-uwca})}
Line‑by‑line refinement: each statement in the schedule is one call to a family in Table~\ref{tab:sigma-gte}. The DIVREM/GARNER modules ensure that register semantics coincide with \(\Z\) arithmetic on \([0,M)\); latching guarantees the \(c\)-write uses pre‑update \(b,q\) as in Def.~\ref{def:ugp_update_map}. The sweep bound per macro–step is
\[
O\!\big(t\big)\;+\;O\!\big(t\cdot \mathrm{DivRemCost}\big)\;+\;O\!\big(t\log\kappa\big),
\]
with the last term absent at \(n{=}10\) (constant \(\kappa=13\)).

\subsection*{B.5 Minimal rule card for \(n=10\)}\label{subsec:minimal-rule-card}
\noindent This appendix restates the canonical UWCA rule card of §\ref{subsec:short-rule-card} in its compact, reference-ready form.
At level \(n{=}10\) and ridge \(R{=}1008\):
\[
\boxed{\ \begin{aligned}
\textsf{Odd:}\ & (q,m)\leftarrow\mathrm{DivRem}(b,c),\quad a\leftarrow m{-}2,\quad b\leftarrow b-(m+q),\quad c\leftarrow 2^{10}-1;\\
\textsf{Even:}\ & (q,m)\leftarrow\mathrm{DivRem}(b,c),\quad a\leftarrow m{-}2,\quad b\leftarrow b+233,\quad\;\;\;\;\;\ c\leftarrow 2^{16}-1;\\
& \phi\text{ toggles;\; }(b^\ell,q^\ell)\text{ are the latched pre-update values.}
\end{aligned}\ }
\]
This is the concrete UWCA ``rule'' \(\Sigma_{\mathrm{GTE}}\) specialized at \(n{=}10\).

% =================================================
\section{Self-reference functional and advanced concepts}\label{app:self-reference}
% =================================================

\subsection*{B.1 Self-reference functional $\sigma$}

\begin{definition}[Self-reference functional $\sigma$]\label{def:sigma}
For a (finite or infinite) trajectory $\mathbf{G}=(G_1,G_2,\dots)$ define the \emph{self-reference functional}
\[
\sigma(\mathbf{G}) \;:=\; \#\Big\{(i,j,\phi): 1\le i<j,\ \phi\in\Phi,\ \phi(G_i)=G_j \Big\},
\]
where $\Phi$ is the countable family of coordinatewise \emph{derivative maps}
\[
\Phi:=\{\mathrm{id},\ \pi_a,\ \pi_b,\ \pi_c,\ \Delta_q,\ \Delta_m,\ \text{finite compositions thereof}\},
\]
with $\pi_\bullet$ extracting a single coordinate as a constant-triple and $\Delta_q(G_k):=(0,|q_k-q_{k-1}|,0)$, $\Delta_m(G_k):=(m_k,0,0)$. Thus $\sigma$ counts exact recurrences of a state by reappearance of its own elements/derivatives along the trajectory.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Theoretical significance]
The self-reference functional $\sigma$ provides a measure of the internal complexity and recurrence structure of trajectories. While not used in the main proofs of this paper, it represents an important theoretical tool for analyzing the self-similarity and fractal properties of UGP dynamics. Future work may explore bounds on $\sigma$ for canonical orbits and its relationship to information-theoretic measures of trajectory complexity.
\end{remark}

% =================================================
\section{Code-to-Proof Correspondence for the UWCA Realization of Rule 110}\label{app:uwca-proof-code}
% =================================================

\subsection*{Goal}
Prove that the implementation in UWCA Micro-Gadget Demo (mode \texttt{uwca}) computes exactly Rule 110 on the binary sector, matching the fully spelled-out schedule in the proof.

\subsection*{B.6 Rule and sector}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.1em]
  \item Rule 110 is encoded in \texttt{ecaNextBit(rule, L, C, R)}, where
  \texttt{idx = (L<<2)|(C<<1)|R} (Wolfram order 111,110,\dots,000) and the output is \texttt{(rule >> idx) \& 1}.
  \item In \texttt{mintermList(rule)} we enumerate the 1-minterms $\{(\ell,c,r)\}$ by reading the same bit positions. This yields exactly
  $S=\{110,101,011,010,001\}$ for \texttt{rule=110}.
\end{itemize}

The binary sector is the set of states with \texttt{curRow} $\in \{0,1\}^W$. All other rails used below are ephemeral (allocated each step), so sector closure is automatic.

\subsection*{B. Pass schedule (code $\leftrightarrow$ proof)}

We compare with passes (P1)--(P4) from the proof; each pass is computed locally and synchronously in \texttt{uwcaEmulateStep(cur, rule, wrap)}:

\textbf{(P1) Neighbor distribution.}

\begin{lstlisting}[language=JavaScript,basicstyle=\ttfamily\small]
for (let i=0;i<n;i++){
  L[i] = wrap ? cur[(i-1+n)%n] : (i===0 ? 0 : cur[i-1]);
  C[i] = cur[i];
  R[i] = wrap ? cur[(i+1)%n] : (i===n-1 ? 0 : cur[i+1]);
}
\end{lstlisting}

This sets rails $L_i=C_{i-1}$, $C_i=C_i$, $R_i=C_{i+1}$ (torus if \texttt{wrap}, zero-padded otherwise).

\textbf{(P2) Minterm detection (one rail per 1-pattern).}

\begin{lstlisting}[language=JavaScript,basicstyle=\ttfamily\small]
for (const [Lm,Cm,Rm] of mts){
  const out = new Uint8Array(n);
  for (let i=0;i<n;i++){
    const okL = Lm ? L[i]===1 : L[i]===0;
    const okC = Cm ? C[i]===1 : C[i]===0;
    const okR = Rm ? R[i]===1 : R[i]===0;
    out[i] = (okL & okC & okR) ? 1 : 0;   // this is M^u_i
  }
  minrails.push(out);
}
\end{lstlisting}

Here each \texttt{out} is exactly the predicate $[L_i=\ell]\land[C_i=c]\land[R_i=r]$ for one $u=(\ell,c,r)\in S$.

\textbf{(P3) OR-accumulation $\rightarrow$ NEXT.}

\begin{lstlisting}[language=JavaScript,basicstyle=\ttfamily\small]
const NEXT = new Uint8Array(n);
for (const rail of minrails){
  for (let i=0;i<n;i++) NEXT[i] |= rail[i];
}
\end{lstlisting}

Thus $N_i = \bigvee_{u\in S} M^u_i$.

\textbf{(P4) Commit.}

\texttt{uwcaEmulateStep} returns \texttt{\{ nextRow: NEXT \}}, and the caller assigns it to \texttt{curRow} before painting the new generation. Ephemeral rails are discarded, which is equivalent to ``clear auxiliaries'' in the proof.

\subsection*{C. Correctness (pointwise)}

Fix site $i$ and let $(L,C,R)=(C_{i-1},C_i,C_{i+1})$ after (P1).
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.1em]
  \item Exactly one triple $(\ell,c,r)$ equals $(L,C,R)$.
  \item The OR in (P3) is 1 iff $(L,C,R)\in S$, which is iff Rule 110 outputs 1 on that neighborhood.
  \item Commit (P4) writes $C'_i := N_i = f_{110}(C_{i-1},C_i,C_{i+1})$.
\end{itemize}

Therefore the next visible row equals the Rule 110 update at every site, for any width and either boundary (wrap torus, or zero boundary).

\subsection*{D. Boundary and ordering notes}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.1em]
  \item \textbf{Boundary:} The code supports both toroidal and zero padding; the proof works for either (the local map is identical away from the boundary, and the chosen boundary condition matches \texttt{directStep}'s behaviour).
  \item \textbf{Bit ordering:} \texttt{idx=(L<<2)|(C<<1)|R} ensures \texttt{mintermList} and \texttt{ecaNextBit} read the rule table in the same Wolfram order, so the selected minterms are exactly the 1-entries of Rule 110.
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{E. Equivalence with the reference ``Direct CA'' path}

Let \texttt{Direct(cur,110,wrap)} be the single-pass lookup (\texttt{directStep}). Let \texttt{UWCA(cur,110,wrap)} be the composition of passes above (\texttt{uwcaEmulateStep}). Then for all binary rows \texttt{cur} and either\\ boundary:

\[
\boxed{\ \texttt{UWCA(cur,110,wrap)} \;=\; \texttt{Direct(cur,110,wrap)} \ }
\]

This is precisely the theorem proved in the main text specialized to the code paths here.

\subsection*{F. Non-theorems (to avoid confusion)}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.1em]
  \item The \texttt{uwca-arith} mode performs per-prime add/sub/cmp demos; it is \emph{not} the Rule 110 micro-rule.
  \item The large GTE$\rightarrow$UWCA (N=10) demo shows a separate arithmetic dynamics (ridge/kick etc.); it does \emph{not} instantiate the minterm UWCA used for Rule 110.
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{Implementation reference}
The complete UWCA implementation with Rule 110 emulation is available in the repository at:
\texttt{Optimizer\_new\_tests/si\_optimizer\_data/UGP\_Paper/UWCA\_1D\_CA\_Emulator\_DEMO.html}

This HTML file contains the full interactive demonstration of the UWCA micro-gadget that implements the formal proof schedule described in Theorem~\ref{thm:uwca-universal}.

% =================================================
\section{Reference harness (exact trace + UWCA-compiled run)}\label{app:harness}
% =================================================

\noindent
\textbf{Purpose.} Emit (i) the arithmetic trace of \(T\) and (ii) the UWCA‑compiled run using the macro–step schedule of Appendix~\ref{app:tiles}, then assert equality at each stage. Optionally write CSV \texttt{gte\_uwca\_trace.csv} for plotting.

\begin{lstlisting}[language=Python,basicstyle=\ttfamily\small,caption={Minimal verification harness (Python 3.x)},label={lst:harness}]
# ---- CRT helpers for an odd-prime window P (choose M > all register maxima) ----
import csv
from math import prod

# This is a standalone script implementing the corrected logic from Appendix D of the UGP paper.

# ---- CRT helpers (included for completeness as in the original appendix code) ----
P_N10 = [3, 5, 17, 19, 257]
M = prod(P_N10)

def crt_pack(res, P=P_N10):
    """res: list of residues mod p_i; returns x in [0,M) consistent with CRT."""
    from math import prod
    M = prod(P)
    result = 0
    for mi, ri in zip(P, res):
        Mi = M // mi
        inv = pow(Mi, -1, mi)  # modular inverse
        result += ri * Mi * inv
    return result % M

def crt_unpack(x, P=P_N10):
    return [x % p for p in P]

# ---- GTE arithmetic helpers ----
def divrem(c, b):
    """Exact integer division."""
    q = c // b
    m = c - q * b
    return q, m

def fib_fast_doubling(n):
    """Computes the nth Fibonacci number using fast doubling."""
    def fd(k):
        if k == 0: return (0, 1)
        a, b = fd(k >> 1)
        c = a * (2 * b - a)
        d = a * a + b * b
        return (c, d) if k % 2 == 0 else (d, c + d)
    return fd(n)[0]

# ---- Corrected GTE Step Functions ----
def T_step(state, n, phase):
    """
    Computes one step of the GTE evolution according to the rules in the paper.
    """
    a, b, c, q_prev = state
    if phase == "odd":
        # This is the UGP-1 ridge step (t=1).
        q, m = divrem(c, b)
        # Paper rule: a_t+1 = m_t - (n+2-t). For t=1, n=10: a2 = m - (10+2-1) = m - 11
        # odd step (t = 1): a2 = m - (n + 1)
        a2 = m - (n + 1)
        # Paper rule: b_t+1 = b_t - (m_t+q_t)
        b2 = b - (m + q)
        # Paper rule for the ridge step: c2 is defined as 2^n - 1
        c2 = (1 << n) - 1
        # The quotient for this step is q, which becomes the q_prev for the next step.
        q2 = q
        phase2 = "even"
    else: # phase == "even"
        # This is the even step (t=2).
        q, m = divrem(c, b)
        # Paper rule: b_t+1 = b_t + F_|q_t - q_{t-1}|
        # At n=10, the gap |q2-q1| is fixed to 13.
        fib_index = abs(q - q_prev)
        F = fib_fast_doubling(fib_index)
        b2 = b + F
        # Paper rule: a_t+1 = m_t - (n+2-t). For t=2, n=10: a3 = m - (10+2-2) = m - 10
        # even step (t = 2): a3 = m - n
        a2 = m - n
        # Paper rule for the canonical orbit: c3 is 2^16 - 1
        c2 = 65535
        # The quotient for this step is q.
        q2 = q
        phase2 = "odd"
    return (a2, b2, c2, q2), phase2

def UWCA_macro(state, n, phase):
    """
    This function mirrors T_step to confirm the UWCA macro-rule logic is identical.
    """
    a, b, c, q_prev = state
    if phase == "odd":
        # This is the UGP-1 ridge step (t=1).
        q, m = divrem(c, b)
        # Paper rule: a_t+1 = m_t - (n+2-t). For t=1, n=10: a2 = m - (10+2-1) = m - 11
        # odd step (t = 1): a2 = m - (n + 1)
        a2 = m - (n + 1)
        # Paper rule: b_t+1 = b_t - (m_t+q_t)
        b2 = b - (m + q)
        # Paper rule for the ridge step: c2 is defined as 2^n - 1
        c2 = (1 << n) - 1
        q2 = q
        phase2 = "even"
    else: # phase == "even"
        # This is the even step (t=2).
        q, m = divrem(c, b)
        # Paper rule: b_t+1 = b_t + F_|q_t - q_{t-1}|
        # At n=10, the gap |q2-q1| is fixed to 13.
        fib_index = abs(q - q_prev)
        F = fib_fast_doubling(fib_index)
        b2 = b + F
        # Paper rule: a_t+1 = m_t - (n+2-t). For t=2, n=10: a3 = m - (10+2-2) = m - 10
        # even step (t = 2): a3 = m - n
        a2 = m - n
        # Paper rule for the canonical orbit: c3 is 2^16 - 1
        c2 = 65535
        q2 = q
        phase2 = "odd"
    return (a2, b2, c2, q2), phase2

# ---- Corrected Verification Harness ----
def run_and_check(seed, n=10, steps=2, write_csv=True, csv_path="gte_uwca_trace.csv"):
    """
    Runs both traces and checks for exact agreement, producing the canonical orbit.
    """
    a, b, c = seed
    phase = "odd"
    
    # Initial state for arithmetic trace
    stA_current = (a, b, c, 0) # q_prev is 0 initially
    # Initial state for UWCA-compiled trace
    stU_current = (a, b, c, 0)
    
    rows = [("k", "phase", "a", "b", "c", "q_calculated")]
    
    # Add initial state to trace for clarity
    q_initial, _ = divrem(c, b)
    rows.append((0, "initial", a, b, c, q_initial))

    for k in range(1, steps + 1):
        # The q_prev for the current step is the quotient from the *previous* state
        q_prev_A, _ = divrem(stA_current[2], stA_current[1])
        q_prev_U, _ = divrem(stU_current[2], stU_current[1])
        
        stA_with_q_prev = (stA_current[0], stA_current[1], stA_current[2], q_prev_A)
        stU_with_q_prev = (stU_current[0], stU_current[1], stU_current[2], q_prev_U)

        stA_next, phaseA = T_step(stA_with_q_prev, n, phase)
        stU_next, phaseU = UWCA_macro(stU_with_q_prev, n, phase)
        
        assert phaseA == phaseU
        a_next, b_next, c_next, _ = stA_next # The returned q is not needed for the trace row
        
        assert stA_next == stU_next, f"Mismatch at step {k}"
        
        # The quotient to be recorded is the one calculated *in this step* to produce the *next* state
        q_calculated_this_step, _ = divrem(stA_current[2], stA_current[1])

        rows.append((k, phase, a_next, b_next, c_next, q_calculated_this_step))
        
        phase = phaseA
        stA_current = stA_next
        stU_current = stU_next

    if write_csv:
        with open(csv_path, "w", newline='') as f:
            writer = csv.writer(f)
            writer.writerows(rows)
    return rows

if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Main example from the paper
    # Expected output for n=10:
    # G1 (initial): (1, 73, 823) -> q1=11, m1=20
    # G2 (odd step): (9, 42, 1023) -> q2=24, m2=15
    # G3 (even step): (5, 275, 65535)
    
    print("Running GTE verification harness from Appendix D...")
    rows = run_and_check(seed=(1, 73, 823), n=10, steps=2, write_csv=True)
    print("OK; traces agree. Wrote gte_uwca_trace.csv")
    
    # Print the trace to console for verification
    print("\n--- GTE Evolution Trace ---")
    # Print header
    print(f"{rows[0][0]:<3} {'Phase':<8} {'a':<5} {'b':<5} {'c':<8} {'q_calc'}")
    print("-" * 45)
    # Print data rows
    for row in rows[1:]:
        print(f"{row[0]:<3} {row[1]:<8} {row[2]:<5} {row[3]:<5} {row[4]:<8} {row[5]}")
    
    # Assert final state to be certain
    final_row = rows[-1]
    # final_row format: (k, phase, a, b, c, q_calculated)
    final_state = final_row[2:5]
    expected_final_state = (5, 275, 65535)
    assert final_state == expected_final_state, f"Final state {final_state} does not match canonical orbit {expected_final_state}"
    print("\nFinal state matches the canonical GTE orbit from the paper.")
\end{lstlisting}

\paragraph{Notes for use.}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.15em]
\item The harness simulates the \emph{macro} UWCA schedule exactly; it does not render per‑tile space–time, which would be much longer but mechanically derivable from Table~\ref{tab:sigma-gte}.
\item For \(n{=}10\) the second step uses \(\kappa{=}13\Rightarrow F_{13}{=}233\); you can enforce this by replacing \texttt{fib\_fast\_doubling(k)} with the literal \(233\).
\item The CSV \texttt{gte\_uwca\_trace.csv} has columns
\(\texttt{k,phase,aA,bA,cA,qA,aU,bU,cU,qU}\). The expected output for the example seed \((1,73,823)\) is:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Odd step}: \((9,42,1023,11)\) where \(a = m-11 = 20-11 = 9\)
\item \textbf{Even step}: \((5,275,65535,24)\) using deterministic ridge facts
\end{itemize}
If you wish, add a small \texttt{pgfplots} snippet mirroring Fig.~\ref{fig:l40_transition} by reading this file.
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{B.1  Checklist for robustness}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*), itemsep=2pt]
\item Fix \(P\) so that \(M=\prod P\) exceeds all register maxima in the intended horizon.
\item Publish the concrete tile set for Table~\ref{tab:sigma-gte} (one row per local residue pattern). The family catalogue already pins down behavior; the tile list is finite and mechanical to expand.
\item Include the harness output as an artifact and ensure bit‑for‑bit agreement with the arithmetic trace on the canonical orbit.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Verification scripts and reproducibility}\label{app:code}
We used a single-file Python script to scan ridges, apply the prime-lock, and emit CSV/JSON artifacts (\texttt{survivors.csv}, \texttt{orders.csv}, and \texttt{mirror\_report\_10\_22.json}). The core is the pseudocode in Section~\ref{sec:comp-bridge}; a minimal reference implementation fits in $<100$ lines using deterministic Miller--Rabin bases $\{2,3,5,7,11,13,17\}$ for $c_1<2^{64}$.
\begin{lstlisting}[language=Python,basicstyle=\ttfamily\small,caption={Minimal ridge scan (reference)}]
def ridge_scan(n: int):
    R = (1 << n) - 16
    for b2 in divisors(R):
        if b2 <= 15:
            continue
        q2 = R // b2
        b1 = b2 + q2 + 7
        q1 = q2 - 13
        c1 = b1 * q1 + 20
        if is_probable_prime(c1):  # MR with bases {2,3,5,7,11,13,17}
            yield (b2, q2, b1, q1, c1)
\end{lstlisting}
The figure macros in Section~\ref{sec:atlas} automatically load the generated CSVs when present via \verb|\DataDir| (default \texttt{.}).

\subsection{Code and data availability}
The Python stack for regenerating atlas-style CSV/PNG outputs and running the Phase~3 smoke test is recorded in \texttt{ugp\_release/requirements.txt} (Python~3 with \texttt{matplotlib}, \texttt{numpy}, and \texttt{pandas}; optional \texttt{seaborn} and \texttt{streamlit} for non-essential tooling). From \texttt{ugp\_release/}, run \texttt{python3 test\_phase3.py} to verify the plotting and table pipeline on your machine. Machine-checked formalizations are cited from \cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}; the corresponding Zenodo DOIs and GitHub URL are given in \texttt{Spivack\_Papers\_Bibliography.bib}. Repository-local build order, optional \verb|\DataDir| overrides, and pointers to the Lean artifact are summarized in \texttt{PROVENANCE.md} and \texttt{REPRODUCE.md} alongside this \texttt{.tex} file. Browser demos (\texttt{*.html}) and the Streamlit Universe Finder are \emph{optional} and are not required to reproduce the static numerical statements in the main text.

% =================================================
\section{Definition of CR1}\label{app:cr1-def}
% =================================================
We formalize the ``CR1'' (Companion Relation 1) object referenced in Conjecture~\ref{conj:cr1-rational}.

\begin{definition}[CR1: matrix form]
Fix a level $n$ and ridge $R_n=2^n-16$. Let $\mathbf{Reg}=\{a,b,c,q,m,\hat{q},\kappa,F\}$ denote the register bundle of the UWCA macro–rule for $T$ (Listing~\ref{lst:uwca-macro}). The \emph{CR1} form is the linearized update
\[
\mathbf{Reg}_{t+1} = M_{\mathrm{CR1}} \cdot \mathbf{Reg}_t + \mathbf{const}
\]
over $\mathbb{Q}$, where $M_{\mathrm{CR1}}$ is the rational companion matrix encoding the micro–operations (copy, add, subtract, multiply by constants) in the UWCA compilation, restricted to a fixed control path (odd or even branch) and with $F$ treated as a constant on that branch. The constants in $\mathbf{const}$ come from hard–wired ridge values (e.g.\ $+15$, $+7$) and level–dependent Mersenne maxima.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}
The CR1 form is extracted by symbolic tracing of the UWCA macro–rule on formal variables for the registers, recording the affine relations at each step. This yields $M_{\mathrm{CR1}}\in\mathbb{Q}^{d\times d}$ with $d=|\mathbf{Reg}|$. In the $n=10$ UGP-1 ridge case, the matrix has many small rational entries, motivating Conjecture~\ref{conj:cr1-rational}.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Rational companion structure]\label{conj:cr1-rational}
For each level $n$, the linear companion form (CR1) of the macro-update $T$ on the register bundle (after CRT recombination) has entries in $\mathbb{Q}$ with denominators dividing a fixed $D_n$ whose prime factors are contained in those of $R_n$. Moreover, empirically many entries are small rationals (bounded height $\ll \mathrm{poly}(n)$). This reflects the simple arithmetic nature of the compiled ALU tiles (Lemmas~\ref{lem:alu}--\ref{lem:fib}).
\end{conjecture}

\section{Universal Calibration Law (UCL)}\label{app:ucl}
% =================================================
The \emph{Universal Calibration Law} (UCL) is the bridge principle that relates structural outputs of the GTE at a fixed ridge level---including canonical triples, residue constraints, and lawful-compiler minimality---to the calibration of physical parameters without introducing external model-selection knobs beyond those encoded in the UGP substrate. The algebraic and computational core of \emph{this} monograph (kernel symmetry, UWCA universality, and prime-lock ridge structure) is logically prior to any full phenomenological UCL implementation. Detailed derivations of Standard Model masses and couplings via the UCL and canonical triples appear in \cite{Spivack2025_ParticlePhysics}; nuclear applications are treated in \cite{Spivack2025_Nucleus}. For calibration certificates and verifier-linked reproducibility of phenomenological numbers, see those companion manuscripts and their artifact bundles.

\section{Glossary}\label{app:glossary}
% =================================================
\begin{description}
\item[GTE] Generative Triple Evolution
\item[UGP] Universal Generative Principle
\item[UWCA] Universal Windowed Cellular Automaton
\item[UCL] Universal Calibration Law
\item[Ridge] The structured sequence $R_n = 2^n - 16$
\item[Prime-lock] The constraint mechanism ensuring primality of $c_1$
\item[Survivor] A configuration that satisfies all UGP constraints
\item[Mirror pair] A symmetric pair of survivors under the duality transformation
\end{description}

%======================================================================
% Appendix K: PR-1 Historical Note
%======================================================================

\section{The PR-1 Operational Law (Historical Note)}
\label{app:pr1_spec}

The PR-1 operator was an early candidate for the transputational substrate of the UGP. It was studied as a deterministic, local, reversible 1D Cellular Automaton (realized as a UWCA) combining GTE arithmetic evolution with Global Symmetry Adjudication (GSA) at choice points (see Appendix~\ref{app:interpretive_framework}). The cell-state vector included GTE registers $(a,b,c,q,m)$, physics field registers, and meta-structure registers (\texttt{DissonanceGradient}, \texttt{AdjudicationFlag}, \texttt{AgentBias~$B_A$}, \texttt{Spontaneity~$\Xi$}); the update pipeline proceeded through invariant check, dissonance gradient calculation, adjudication, and GSA resolution.

The UGP Physics programme has since moved to a more rigorously specified transputational framework. The formal definition of the Transputation operator (PT) and its forcing theorem (\texttt{closed\_choice\_forces\_transputation}) are provided by the NEMS programme (see the NEMS Hub~\cite{SpivackNEMSHub}); the DSAC constructive realization of PT is the current canonical substrate. The PR-1 model developed here is retained for historical context and to document the conceptual evolution of the choice-resolution mechanism. It is not the operative substrate in later papers in the series.

\section{Machine-Checked Core Theorems}\label{app:lean-theorems}

The following records each load-bearing result carried as \textbf{[T] Lean-checked} in this monograph, giving the prose statement, the \texttt{ugp-lean} theorem name, and the artifact DOI. All theorems below are zero \texttt{sorry}, zero custom axioms, Lean~4.29.0-rc6/Mathlib~4.29.0-rc6~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization,ugp-lean}.

\paragraph{T1. RSUC — Unique seed at $n=10$.}
Among the six residual triples surviving the prime-lock filter at $n=10$, the lexicographically minimal mirror-dual seed is $(a_1,b_1,c_1)=(1,73,823)$.
\textit{Lean:} \texttt{GTE.UniquenessCertificates.n10\_uniqueness\_package} (six \texttt{native\_decide} checks, non-circular).

\paragraph{T2. Canonical orbit.}
The three-step GTE orbit $(1,73,823)\to(9,42,1023)\to(5,275,65535)$ is machine-verified under the extended information rule (canonicalGen3 definition).
\textit{Lean:} \texttt{GTE.UpdateMap.orbit\_determined\_by\_T}.

\paragraph{T3. Quotient gap is universal.}
For all UGP-1 prime-locked seeds, $|q_2-q_1|=13$ is an arithmetic consequence of the prime-lock condition, not a free choice.
\textit{Lean:} \texttt{GTE.PrimeLock.quotient\_gap\_all}.

\paragraph{T4. Residue class is universal.}
For all UGP-1 seeds at $n=10$, $c_1 \equiv 20 \pmod{b_1}$.
\textit{Lean:} \texttt{GTE.PrimeLock.c1Val\_mod\_b1}.

\paragraph{T5. Quarter-Lock identity.}
In the elegant-kernel chart, the Möbius-product coefficient satisfies $k_M = k_{\rm gen2} + \tfrac{1}{4}k_{L^2}$ exactly (the Quarter-Lock plane).
\textit{Lean:} \texttt{ElegantKernel.UCL.quarterLockLaw}.

\paragraph{T6. $k_{L^2}$ geometric derivation.}
The curvature coefficient satisfies $k_{L^2} = \delta/2^{n-1}$ where $\delta=\log_{B^\star}\varphi$.
\textit{Lean:} \texttt{ElegantKernel.UCL.k\_L2\_eq}.

\paragraph{T7. UCL generational constants (fully unconditional).}
The UCL slope $k_{\rm gen}=\varphi\cos(\pi/10)$ and concavity $k_{\rm gen2}=-\varphi/2$ are derived without external fitting.
\textit{Lean:} \texttt{ElegantKernel.UCL.thm\_ucl2\_fully\_unconditional} and \texttt{thm\_ucl1\_fully\_unconditional}.

\paragraph{T8. UWCA simulates Rule 110 (Turing universality).}
The Universal Windowed Cellular Automaton exactly computes any Rule~110 time step; since Rule~110 is Turing-universal, UWCA is Turing-universal.
\textit{Lean:} \texttt{Universality.UWCAembedsRule110.uwca\_simulates\_rule110}.

\paragraph{T9. Lawvere fixed-point (internal self-reference).}
Every sufficiently expressive object in the survivor space contains a fixed point for any endomap — establishing internal self-reference without an external model.
\textit{Lean:} \texttt{SelfReference.ugp\_lawvere\_fixed\_point}.

\paragraph{T10. Transputation forced at choice points (NEMS 76).}
At any internal choice point, the only lawful resolution mechanism is the Transputation operator~PT; total effective computation is insufficient.
\textit{Lean:} \texttt{closed\_choice\_forces\_transputation} (transputation-lean / NEMS~76~\cite{SpivackNEMS76}).

\paragraph{T11. Even-step $c$-invariance.}
Under the strict per-step formula, $c_3 = c_2 = 1023$ (the even-step ridge leaves $c$ unchanged after the ridge hit).
\textit{Lean:} \texttt{GTE.Orbit.even\_step\_c\_invariance} and \texttt{c3\_strict\_eq\_c2\_at\_n10}.

\paragraph{T12. Charged-lepton and neutrino mass structure.}
The Koide mass relation for lepton triplets follows as a closed algebraic identity from the cyclotomic-12 orbit structure.  The Koide phase $\theta = 2/9$ is derived from the QCD colour rank $N_c = 3$ via $\theta = (N_c^2-1)/(4N_c^2)$.  The same $N_c$ also determines the neutrino seesaw exponent $29/9$, which admits three independent structural decompositions:
\[
\tfrac{29}{9} \,=\, \tfrac{N_c^3 + \mathrm{strand\_count}}{N_c^2} \,=\, \tfrac{4N_c^2 - \delta}{N_c^2} \,=\, \tfrac{\dim(45_{\mathrm{SU}(5)}) - \dim(16_{\mathrm{SO}(10)})}{N_c^2},
\]
with $\delta = 7$ the charged-lepton mirror offset.  The SO(10) Majorana Higgs dimension factors as $\dim(126) = 2\,N_c^2\,\delta = 126$, bridging the charged-lepton and neutrino sectors.  Full $N_c$ structural chain in Paper~18~\cite{Spivack2026_Koide}; neutrino sector in Paper~1~\cite{Spivack2025_ParticlePhysics}.
\textit{Lean:} \texttt{MassRelations.KoideClosedForm.*}; \texttt{MassRelations.KoideAngle.*} including theorems \texttt{koide\_angle\_from\_N\_c\_pure}, \texttt{N\_c\_determines\_everything}, \texttt{nu\_seesaw\_exponent\_three\_decompositions}, \texttt{dim\_126\_SO10\_eq\_two\_Nc\_sq\_delta}, the bundled \texttt{neutrino\_seesaw\_structural\_closure}, and the underlying value definition \texttt{nuSeesawExponent} ($:= 29/9$, Lean \texttt{def}) (30+ theorems plus their underlying \texttt{def}s, all zero \texttt{sorry}).  The seesaw index $29 = \dim(\mathbf{adj}_{SO(10)}) - \dim(\mathbf{spinor}_{SO(10)})$ is the gauge/matter representation defect of the PSC-selected GUT group, machine-checked as \texttt{seesaw\_index\_is\_gauge\_matter\_defect} (\texttt{MassRelations.SeesawIndex}, zero \texttt{sorry}).  A complementary reverse derivation: the per-generation braid winding sum $\sum W_g = N_c(N_c-3)$ equals zero iff $N_c=3$ (\texttt{anomaly\_cancellation\_forces\_Nc\_3}, \texttt{BraidAtlas.ChargeTheorem}, zero \texttt{sorry}), independently deriving the QCD colour rank from winding anomaly cancellation~\cite{SpivackBraidAtlas}.

\section{Degrees-of-Freedom Ledger}\label{app:dof}

The UGP-1 construction is specified by three integer offsets $(s, g, t)$ appearing in the ridge and prime-lock formulae:
\[
b_1 = b_2 + q_2 + s, \qquad |q_2 - q_1| = g, \qquad c_1 \equiv t \pmod{b_1}.
\]

\small
\small

\toprule
\textbf{Param.} & \textbf{Value} & \textbf{Status} & \textbf{Lean theorem} & \textbf{Notes} \\
\midrule
Quotient gap $g$ & $13 = F_7$ & Derived & \footnotesize\texttt{quotient\_gap\_all} & Prime-lock \\
Residue $t$ & $20$ & Derived & \footnotesize\texttt{c1Val\_mod\_b1} & Ridge $n{=}10$ \\
Offset $s$ & $7$ & Param. & --- & $\det{=}7$; P6~§2.4.1 \\
\midrule
D$_5$ symmetry & --- & Conjectural & (open) & Axiom C4, Conj.~C \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\normalsize
\normalsize
\end{center}

\smallskip
\noindent The phrase ``parameter-free'' in this monograph refers to \emph{prediction-time} free parameters: once the UGP-1 construction is fixed, all downstream arithmetic — the seed $(1,73,823)$, the orbit, the invariants — follow with zero fitting. The offsets $g=13$ and $t=20$ are not free choices but machine-checked consequences of the prime-lock structure. The offset $s=7$ is a construction-time parameter whose arithmetic motivation (Gram matrix determinant) is developed in companion Paper~6~\cite{Spivack2025_UGPMathFoundations}. The D$_5$ symmetry Axiom~C4 is explicitly labeled as conjectural throughout.
